# Changelog

All notable changes to `hoody-sdk` are documented here.

Format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/) and [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/).

## [1.0.1] — 2026-11-16

1.0.1 is the first stable release of `hoody-sdk`. It ends the beta that ran from 1.0.0-beta.1 to 1.0.0-beta.17.

One package now gives you five ways to work with Hoody: the TypeScript SDK, the `hoody` CLI, the Hub in a browser, the same Hub full screen in a terminal with `hoody hub`, and `hoody-hub.html`, one file that is the whole Hub and opens from disk. At beta.1 it held the SDK and the CLI.

The SDK has 1416 typed methods and the CLI has 1185 commands, across 21 namespaces. From here the package keeps growing: later releases add commands, flags and methods, and what 1.0.1 has keeps working. A name from before 1.0.1 is refused with the name to use instead.

### What stable means

- **What you use today keeps working.** Later releases add commands, flags and methods. The names and the behaviour in this release are kept compatible. If one has to change, it is deprecated first, and the deprecation names its replacement. To get here, several hundred CLI commands, group aliases and flags and more than 200 SDK methods and members were renamed or removed. [docs/MIGRATION-2026-10-NAMES.md](docs/MIGRATION-2026-10-NAMES.md) lists every renamed command, flag and SDK member with its new name, and the rule for exported types.
- **A name from before 1.0.1 is refused, never ignored, with three exceptions.** An old command or flag exits 2 and names what to type instead. A renamed SDK option key throws `RetiredOptionError` and nothing is sent. An old method or exported type is gone from the types, so TypeScript reports it when you compile. Three old names are still read, each as its `computer` name: the config key `container`, `hoody local lock reveal --field container`, and the `HOODY_CONTAINER` and `HOODY_CONTAINER_ID` environment variables.
- **It is installed as `latest`.** `npm install hoody-sdk`, `npm install -g hoody-sdk` and `npx hoody-sdk` get 1.0.1 with no tag and no version. The version has no pre-release suffix.
- **Scripts do not get the new layout.** The terminal layout this release adds is drawn only on a terminal, never in pipes, in CI, in agent mode or with `-o json|yaml`. Where the output or the exit code of a command changed for scripts too, Breaking lists it.
- **The surface is 21 namespaces.** The SDK has 1416 typed methods and the CLI has 1185 commands. The package is ESM only and runs on Node.js 20.19 or later, on Bun and in browsers.
- **One compatibility window is still open.** A call to an old `/containers/` path keeps the old field names for this release. The next release removes those paths (see Breaking).

### Changes since 1.0.0-beta.17

#### CLI

- **Output on a terminal has one layout.** A command prints a tab line on stderr that names where it acted, then its data as a list, an item, a receipt, a value, a stream, bytes or an error. `-o table`, `-o wide`, `-o json` or `HOODY_AGENT_MODE=1` gives the old layout.
- **`hoody use` shows and saves where commands act.** `hoody use` prints the realm, computer, project and account. `hoody use <realm> [computer]` and `hoody use -c <computer>` save them. Commands with name lookup take a name where they took an id: `hoody computers get ember`, `--realm acme`.
- **Bare `hoody` prints one page and exits 0.** It no longer opens a menu, so `hoody | grep shell` works. The account steps it offered are in `hoody setup`, and `hoody welcome` opens the sign-in menu.
- **Five flags are new.** Three are global: `--http` and `--http-curl` print the request a command would send, and nothing that writes is sent; `--mask-urls` shortens every 24-character hex id on screen to its first and last 4 characters. On a read list, `--watch [interval]` redraws it in place, every 2s by default. On an asynchronous operation, `--until-done` waits and exits 0 when it is done, 1 when it failed and 124 on a timeout (10m by default).
- **Help is one line per flag, with examples.** `hoody files backends s3 create --help` is 45 lines; the full help, which `HOODY_HELP_SPLIT=0` still prints, is 83. On a terminal, a missing or mistyped input prints a short block with the near match (`did you mean --name?`) and a command that fixes it.
- **18 destructive commands that never asked now confirm on a terminal.** Scripts, pipes, CI and agent mode run them as before.
- **New commands.** `hoody vm` runs virtual machines inside a computer. `hoody computers urls list` shows a computer's Hoody Kit URLs. `hoody ai freeusage get` shows how much of each server's free AI allowance is used. `hoody ip lookup <ip>` looks up the country and network of up to 25 public IPv4 addresses. `hoody browser markdown get` returns a tab's page as Markdown.
- **Structured inputs take a file or stdin.** A flag that takes JSON accepts it inline, from a file with `@<path>`, or from stdin with `@-`.
- **Commands that generate a value print it in a pipe.** `hoody auth tokens create` prints a short block that starts with the new token, and `hoody proxy aliases create` one that starts with the alias URL, where a pipe got only `create completed successfully.` To read one field in a script, use `-o json`.
- **Over `ssh hoody.com`, `hoody` runs no programs on the gateway.** `hoody tunnel expose`, `hoody mount`, `hoody share` and `hoody pipe send --from-cmd` are refused there and say to run them on your own machine. Requests go only to HTTPS and WSS on port 443 under hoody.com or hoody.bot.
- **The Windows installer adds `hoody` to your PATH.** `install.ps1` adds `%USERPROFILE%\.hoody\bin` to your user PATH once.
- **A raw `--body` takes a file or stdin.** `hoody kv set <key> --db <db> --body @value.json` and `--body @-` send the JSON in the file or on stdin, where they stored the text `@value.json`. A file that cannot be read or does not hold JSON is refused before anything is sent. On the 14 commands whose body is a JSON object, such as `hoody agent todos triage`, malformed JSON and JSON that is not an object are refused too. A leading `@` always names a source: to store text that starts with `@`, write it as a JSON string, `--body '"@name"'`.
- **An empty list whose rows field is `null` prints the empty message.** `hoody kv list` with no matching key printed a blank row and `1 item`.
- **A list says where it continues.** On a terminal, a list that stops with rows left prints `continue with --cursor <value>` (or `--offset <n>`) on stderr under the rows. An empty answer that carries a cursor prints it in every tier: `hoody events list --bootstrap` prints `Continue with --after <cursor>` on stderr after `No items found.`
- **A text answer prints as text on a terminal.** `hoody files get notes.txt` and `hoody notes document get --format md` print the text, where they printed a JSON string with escaped newlines. A pipe now gets the text too (see Fixed); `-o json` and `-o yaml` still print the JSON string.
- **`-o table` on a terminal cuts a value longer than its column.** The value ends in `…` and the rows stay in line. Ids and URLs are never cut, and `-o wide` prints every value whole. A pipe prints the values whole, as before. `hoody ai models list` no longer caps its NAME column, so its rows line up in a pipe too.

#### SDK

- **`box.vm` runs virtual machines.** It has 54 methods, and the package also exports it as the `vm` subpath.
- **`ApiError` says whether the request reached the server.** `outcome` is `'answered'`, `'not_sent'` or `'unknown'`, `attempts` counts the sends, and `retryAfterMs` is the answer's Retry-After in milliseconds.
- **A change is not sent twice.** A PUT or DELETE that the server answered with success is not sent again when reading its answer fails, unless it carries the idempotency key its server honours. A request middleware that throws ends the call with code `REQUEST_REFUSED` (an `ApiError` it throws stays as it is), and nothing replays it.
- **Eight new client options.** `onStepUp` answers a two-factor challenge once. `maxResponseBytes` caps a buffered body and `maxStreamBytes` caps a whole stream. `fetchCredentials` and `fetchCache` pass `credentials` and `cache` to `fetch`. `keyedInProgressWait`, `redactBodyKeys` and `credentialHeaders` control keyed retries and what an `ApiError` redacts. Every generated method also takes `responseIsFinal: true`.
- **Agent streams end with their own turn.** `agent.sessions.turns.start(id, data, { stream: true })` ends after the turn it started; add `followSession: true` to keep reading the session. It replaces `agent.sessions.startTurnAndStream()`. `streamAgentPrompt` waits for its own turn, cancels only that turn, and cancels it on the agent when you abort its `signal`.
- **A WebSocket refuses to connect when a credential you passed for it cannot be sent.** `connect()` rejects with a `WebSocketCredentialNotSentError` and no socket opens.
- **`api.servers.plans.listPublic({ locale })` lists the server plans without signing in.**
- **`undici` needs 8.12.2 or later** (was 8.9.0).

#### Hub in the browser

- **The web Hub is at [hub.hoody.com](https://hub.hoody.com).** It is also at `/auth/home` on the API host. It shows your computers, Bots, apps, programs, files, schedules, logs, billing and settings.
- **The package ships the Hub's source.** It is in `hub/`, under Apache-2.0, and is new in the package since 1.0.0-beta.17. The same code is the web Hub, the one-file Hub and the terminal Hub. [hub/README.md](hub/README.md) shows how to build your own.
- **The web Hub offers the one-file Hub for download.** The file is served at `/auth/hoody-hub.html` on the API host.

#### Hub in the terminal

- **`hoody hub` opens the Hub full screen in your terminal,** with the same pages as the web Hub. It uses the sign-in saved by `hoody login`, or an access key.
- **`hoody hub --realm <id or name>` starts in that realm.** Without it, the Hub uses `HOODY_REALM`, then `HOODY_REALM_ID`, then the realm saved by `hoody use`, then the realm your last run ended in. `hoody hub --demo` opens a sample account that lives in memory and sends nothing.
- **It runs on Linux on x86-64 and arm64:** in the standalone `hoody` binary, or in the npm package with Node.js 26 or later. The musl build has none. On other platforms and without an interactive terminal, the command says why and exits 2.
- **Bare `hoody agent` opens it too** (see Breaking).
- **Keys.** `Ctrl+K` finds any page or action, `?` shows the key list and `q` leaves. Every page is laid out for 80 columns by 24 rows and up. The [Hub guide](docs/reference/guides/hub.md) has the full key list.

#### One-file Hub

- **`hoody-hub.html` is the whole Hub in one file.** It is at the root of the package, with its checksum in `hoody-hub.html.sha256`. Check it with `sha256sum -c hoody-hub.html.sha256` before you open it.
- **Open it from disk, from `localhost` or from an `https` address.** It has no server of its own. The copy in this package signs in to hoody.com, and you never type an address.
- **It offers three ways to sign in:** an email or user name and password (with a two-step check when the account has two-factor authentication), a sign-in on Hoody's site in a new tab, or an access key that starts with `hdy_`.
- **Opened from disk, it keeps nothing.** The sign-in stays in memory, and closing or reloading the page signs you out.
- **Payments open the web Hub.** The file sends no top-up or other payment itself.

#### Sign-in

- **`hoody welcome` opens a sign-in menu.** Signed out, it offers to create an account, sign in with a password, sign in with your browser (over SSH, with a code you enter on any device), paste a sign-in token, or resume an email verification. When the server offers them, it shows "Continue with GitHub" and "Continue with Google". `ssh hoody.com` runs it for you.
- **Signing in from the menu saves the session and nothing else.** It never creates or starts a computer and never changes your saved default computer or project.
- **`hoody setup` finishes setting up an account.** It resumes email verification and claims your free server.
- **`hoody login --web` is the browser sign-in.** `--oauth` exits 2 and names it. `hoody login --token` exits 3 when the token is accepted but cannot be saved, as the other sign-ins do.
- **A two-factor challenge is data under `-o json` and `-o yaml`.** The sign-in prints `requires_2fa`, `temp_token` and `next` on stdout, and the exit code stays 2. In the SDK, `onStepUp` answers the challenge once.
- **`hoody auth 2fa enrollment undo --link-token <token>` undoes a two-factor enrollment** from the "this was not me" link in the alert email. It needs no sign-in.
- **A failed save no longer pairs a new API address with the old token.** With the encrypted local store, both files are written in full before either replaces the old one.

#### Bots

- **The Bot URL group is `hoody boturls`.** `hoody boturls list` with no Bot URL suggests `hoody boturls create`.
- **A free label comes from `hoody boturls label suggest`.**
- **`hoody boturls answer` takes the gate handle as `--gate-id`.** In the SDK, `client.agent.bots.answerGate` answers a gate and `client.agent.bots.stream` is now `client.agent.bots.streamLogs`.
- **`-o json` replaces `--json`** on `hoody boturls ask`, `chat`, `status`, `answer`, `models list`, `updates list` and `events stream`. A command that fails prints the JSON error object, and `hoody boturls events stream -o ndjson` ends a failed stream with one `{"error":{...}}` line.
- **`hoody agent bots voice connect` reaches the agent of the selected computer.** Every call was refused before anything was sent.
- **The Hub has a Bots page in all three forms.** In the terminal Hub, press `g` then `b`.

### Breaking

**Upgrading from 1.0.0-beta.17.** This release is version 1.0.1. Run `hoody update`, or `npm i -g hoody-sdk@1.0.1`. A computer whose scripts load `hoody-sdk` keeps its own copy: update it with `hoody -c <computer> exec modules install --modules hoody-sdk@1.0.1`, which takes effect after hoody-exec restarts. hoody-exec does not move a scripts directory to a new prerelease or major on its own: it reports the update as `held` (`GET /api/v1/exec/dependencies/bundled`, `sdkUpdate`).

In the two CLI tables, an old spelling exits 2 and names the new one unless its row says otherwise. The tables hold the changes most scripts meet. The Changed list describes the main groups of renames, and [docs/MIGRATION-2026-10-NAMES.md](docs/MIGRATION-2026-10-NAMES.md) has a row for each command, flag and SDK member.

#### CLI commands, before and after

| Before | After |
|---|---|
| `hoody containers list` | `hoody computers list` |
| `hoody c list`, `hoody f get` and the other short group aliases (`br`, `c`, `d`, `sql`, `disp`, `ev`, `x`, `f`, `fs`, `fw`, `img`, `m`, `net`, `notif`, `p`, `proj`, `px`, `srv`, `snap`, `t`, `term`, `tun`, `u`, `v`) | The group name: `hoody computers list`, `hoody files get` |
| `hoody agent` (attached to the agent's terminal app) | `hoody hub` opens; the agent's terminal app stays at the computer's `agent-1` link |
| `hoody` (opened a menu with the account steps) | `hoody setup` for the account steps; bare `hoody` prints one page |
| `hoody users free tier status` | `hoody users freetier status` |
| `hoody agent work stop` (stopped everything) | `hoody agent work stop --all` |
| `hoody exec modules list` | `hoody exec modules bundled list` |
| `hoody daemon programs logs stream --id <id>` | `hoody daemon programs logs stream <id>` |
| `hoody watch events stream --id <id>` | `hoody watch events stream <id>` |
| `hoody watch get --id <id>` | `hoody watch get <id>` |
| `hoody daemon programs attach` | `hoody daemon programs terminal connect` |
| `hoody agent headless stream` | `hoody agent headless start --stream` |
| `hoody agent github diff` | `hoody agent github diff get` |
| `hoody screenshot browser` | `hoody browser screenshots save` |
| `hoody screenshot display` | `hoody display screenshots save` |
| `hoody screenshot terminal` | `hoody terminal sessions screenshots save` |
| `hoody files get --stat` | `hoody files stat` |
| `hoody files get --grep` | `hoody files grep` |
| `hoody files get --zip` | `hoody files zip` |
| `hoody servers plans quote --plan-id <plan-id>` | `hoody servers plans quote <plan-id>` |
| `hoody exec scripts delete --path <path>` | `hoody exec scripts delete <path>` |
| `hoody tunnel expose --to <target>` | `hoody tunnel expose <target>` |
| `hoody tunnel expose --url http://localhost:3000` | `hoody tunnel expose http://localhost:3000` |
| `hoody tunnel pull --to <target>` | `hoody tunnel pull <target>` |
| `hoody -c A shell A` (ran a command called `A` on computer A) | `hoody -c A shell -- A`; `hoody -c A shell A` now opens a shell on A |
| `hoody -c A shell B`, `hoody -c A agent B` (the argument won) | Pass one computer; two different ones exit 2 |
| The card payment commands under `hoody wallet payments`: checkout create, intents list, intents get | `hoody wallet payments card checkout create`, `hoody wallet payments card intents list`, `hoody wallet payments card intents get <intent>`. The earlier group name is not a command any more and has no alias; the CLI reports it as an unknown command and does not name the new one |

#### CLI flags, before and after

| Before | After |
|---|---|
| `--container <id>` | `--computer <id>` (`-c <id>` is unchanged) |
| `--realm-id <id>` | `--realm <id or name>`; on `hoody auth tokens realms add` and `remove`, where it named the realm to add or remove, the positional `<realm-id>` |
| `--container-id <id>` | `-c <id>` or `--computer <id>`, except where it is a filter field (see Changed) |
| `--baseUrl`, `--base_url`, `--noColor`, `--nonInteractive`, `--kitAuth`, `--kit_token` and the other camelCase and snake_case spellings of global flags | The kebab-case flag: `--base-url`, `--no-color`, `--non-interactive`, `--kit-auth`, `--kit-token` |
| `HOODY_USER`, `HOODY_PASS`, `HOODY_KIT_PASS`, `HOODY_KIT_AUTH_TYPE` (environment variables) | `HOODY_USERNAME`, `HOODY_PASSWORD`, `HOODY_KIT_PASSWORD`, `HOODY_KIT_AUTH`; an old name set without the new one exits 2 |
| `hoody login --oauth` | `hoody login --web` |
| `hoody config get --format yaml` | `hoody config get -o yaml` |
| `hoody containers wait --format json` | `hoody computers wait -o json` |
| `hoody events stream --format ndjson` | `hoody events stream -o ndjson` |
| `--json` on `hoody mount`, `unmount`, `share list`, `local lock status`, `pipe send` and the other hand-written commands | `-o json` |
| `hoody terminal sessions wait --terminal-id <n>`, `hoody terminal sessions search --terminal-id <n>` | `hoody terminal sessions wait <n>`, `hoody terminal sessions search <n>` |
| `hoody notes document get --output <format>` (the document format) | `hoody notes document get --format <format>`; use `-o` for the output format |
| `hoody files search --q <text>`, `hoody notes records search --q <text>` | `--query <text>` on each |
| `hoody exec logs get --search <text>` | `hoody exec logs get --query <text>` |
| `hoody files images convert --q <n>` | `hoody files images convert --jpeg-quality <n>` |
| `hoody db create --path <file>` | `hoody db create --db <file>` |
| `hoody files archives extract --extract <member>` | `hoody files archives extract --member <member>` |
| `hoody mount --list --verbose` | `hoody mount --list --full` |
| `hoody kv table rollback --dry-run` | `hoody kv table rollback --preview` |
| `hoody kv table rollback --confirm true` | `hoody kv table rollback --yes` |
| `hoody exec scripts delete <path> --confirm true`, `hoody exec logs clear --confirm true` | `--yes` |
| `hoody agent prompt --session <id>` | `hoody agent prompt --session-id <id>` |
| `hoody agent prompt -y`, `--yes` | `hoody agent prompt --gate-policy auto_approve` |
| `hoody agent memory items delete --name <name>` | `hoody agent memory items delete --id <id> --kind slot` |
| `--last-event-id` on the stream commands | The stream's own cursor: `--since-seq` on `hoody agent logs stream`, `--since` on `hoody agent bots logs stream`, `agent sessions stream`, `curl jobs stream` and `kv changes stream`. `hoody proxy logs stream` has none: it resumes by itself after a dropped connection |
| `hoody containers create --environment-vars KEY=VALUE` | `hoody computers create --env KEY=VALUE` |
| `--x-hoody-realm`, `--x-hoody-container`, `--x-hoody-cwd`, `--x-hoody-config-dir` on `hoody agent` commands | `--agent-realm`, `--tool-computer`, `--cwd`, `--config-dir` |
| `--x-hoody-approver-lease`, `--x-hoody-gate-policy`, `--x-hoody-dir-scope`, `--x-hoody-tool-mode`, `--x-expected-length`, `--x-idempotency-key` | `--approver-lease`, `--gate-policy`, `--dir-scope`, `--tool-mode`, `--expected-length`, `--idempotency-key` |
| `hoody pipe receive <path> -o <file>`, `--output <file>` | `hoody pipe receive <path> --out-file <file>` |
| `--path <path>` on `hoody screenshot browser`, `screenshot display` and `screenshot terminal` | `--out-file <path>` on `hoody browser screenshots save`, `display screenshots save` and `terminal sessions screenshots save` |
| `--selector-<x>` on `hoody run search`, `--selector-template-<x>` on `run recipes create` and `update`, `--overrides-<x>` on `run recipes resolve` and `search` | `--<x>`; the profile flag is `--run-profile` |
| `hoody agent --cols`, `--rows`, `--cwd`, `--display`, `--terminal-id`, `--no-clear`, `--onboarding` | removed: bare `hoody agent` opens the Hub, which takes none of them |
| `hoody exec remote call --json` | `hoody exec remote call --json-args` |
| `hoody files open --directory <dir>` | `hoody files open --dir <dir>` |
| `hoody pipe open --n <count>` | `hoody pipe open --receivers <count>` |

#### SDK names, before and after

| Before | After |
|---|---|
| `client.api.containers` | `client.api.computers` |
| `withContainer(id)` | `withComputer(id)` |
| `client.agent.bots.stream` | `client.agent.bots.streamLogs` |
| `client.agent.bots.getLog`, `client.agent.bots.follow()` | `client.agent.bots.getLogs`, `client.agent.bots.followLogs()` |
| `client.agent.bots.sendMessage` | `client.agent.bots.messages.send` |
| `client.agent.sessions.startTurnAndStream` | `client.agent.sessions.turns.start(id, data, { stream: true })` |
| `client.agent.sessions.startTurn` | `client.agent.sessions.turns.start` |
| `tunnelExpose`, `tunnelPull`, `tunnelServe`, `tunnelResumeExpose`, `tunnelResumePull`, `tunnelSocks5` | `exposeTunnel`, `pullTunnel`, `serveTunnel`, `resumeTunnelExpose`, `resumeTunnelPull`, `startTunnelSocks5` |
| `tunnelConnect` | removed; use `TunnelSession` |
| `discoverScripts()`, `clearDiscoveryCache()` | `listCallableScripts()`, `clearCallableScriptsCache()` |
| `client.embeds.url()`, `catalog()`, `views(kit)` | `client.embeds.getUrl()`, `getCatalog()`, `listViews(kit)` |
| `ShareHandle.state()` | `ShareHandle.getState()` |
| On every generated WebSocket client: `url`, `connected`, `readyState`, `reconnecting`, `autoReconnect`, `cursor`, `rawSocket`, `incarnation` | `getUrl()`, `isConnected()`, `getReadyState()`, `isReconnecting()`, `isAutoReconnect()`, `getCursor()`, `getRawSocket()`, `getIncarnation()` |
| `EventsClient.disconnect()`, `EventsManager.disconnect()`, `EventsManager.addEventListener()` | removed; call `close()`, and `EventsManager.subscribe()` |
| `client.agent.signIn` | `client.agent.login` |
| `client.agent.workflows.listRuns` | `client.agent.workflows.runs.list` |
| `client.api.proxy.hooks.create` | `client.api.computers.proxy.createHook` |
| `client.api.proxy.projectPermissions.get`, `set` | `client.api.projects.proxy.getPermissions`, `setPermissions` |
| `client.agent.github.diff` | `client.agent.github.getDiff` |
| `HoodyClient.getKitUrls()` | `listKitUrls()` |
| `client.daemon.programs.attachTerminal()` | `connectTerminal()` |
| Option keys `XHoodyCwd`, `IfMatch`, `IdempotencyKey` | `cwd`, `ifMatch`, `idempotencyKey` |
| Option keys `XHoodyGatePolicy`, `policy` on `agent.sessions.turns.*` | `gatePolicy` |
| Option key `realm` on `agent` methods | `agentRealm` |
| Option key `if_match` on `sqlite.kv.set()` | `ifValue` |
| Option key `LastEventID` | `lastEventId` on `proxyLogs.stream()`; removed on the other streams, which resume with `since`, `since_seq` or `offset` |
| `files.get(path, { stat })`, `{ grep }`, `{ glob }`, `{ q }`, `{ thumbnail }`, `{ zip }`, `{ preview }` | `files.stat(path)`, `files.grep(path, { pattern })`, `files.glob(path, { pattern })`, `files.search(directory, { q })`, `files.images.convert(image)`, `files.zip(directory)`, `files.archives.preview(archive)` |
| `sha256` on `files.get()` | `hash` |
| Client options `forceIPv4`, `forceIPv4Cache` | `forceIpv4`, `forceIpv4Cache` |
| `TerminalClient.state` | `getState()` |
| `TunnelSession.id` | `getId()` |
| `MediaSession.active` | `isActive()` |
| `NotificationDisplayClient.isActive` | `isRunning()` |
| `EventsClient.getConnectionState()`, `EventsManager.getState()` | `getLifecycleState()`; its values changed too: `connected` is `live` or `recovering`, `reconnecting` is `offline`, and a closed stream is `closed`, not `idle` |
| `EventsConnectionState` | `EventsLifecycleState` |
| `IForceIPv4CacheConfig` | `IForceIpv4CacheConfig` |
| `EventsManager.addLifecycleListener()` | `addConnectionListener(event, callback)` |
| `EventsClient.onLifecycle()` | `onConnection()` |
| `token` on `exposeTunnel`, `pullTunnel` and the other tunnel functions | `kitAuth` |
| `serviceIndex` on `shell()` and `terminal.run()` | `terminalId`; where you passed `serviceIndex: 0`, leave `terminalId` out |
| `paramTimeoutMs` on `browser.page.getSnapshot()` | `operationTimeoutMs` |
| `TunnelExposeOptions` | `ExposeTunnelOptions` |
| `main_KVEntry` | `KVEntry` |
| `data.deferred` and `data.note` on the answer of `agent.sessions.trim()` | `data.status`; `AgentSessionsTrimResponse` is now a union of a finished trim (`data.outcome: 'done'`) and a pending one (`data.status: 'pending'`), so narrow it before you read a field |
| `_realm` on 20 account methods | dropped; the methods always go to the base host |
| `import ... from 'hoody-sdk/chat'`, `'hoody-sdk/ai'`, `'hoody-sdk/update-check'` | removed; the import fails with `ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED` |
| The card payment methods on `client.api.wallet.payments` | `createCardCheckout`, `listCardIntents`, `getCardIntent`. The earlier methods are gone, with no alias |

An old generated method or exported type is gone from the types. An old method on the client and handle classes that Changed lists throws a `TypeError` that names the new one. Other old hand-written methods and functions are gone and read `undefined`. A renamed option key throws `RetiredOptionError`.

#### Behaviour, before and after

| Before | After |
|---|---|
| The card payment routes and the payment availability answer were named after the payment backend | The routes are `/api/v1/wallet/payments/card/checkout` and `/api/v1/wallet/payments/card/intents`; `hoody wallet payments availability get -o json` has the keys `card` and `crypto`, and a payment intent's `provider` is `card` or `crypto`. The earlier paths answer 404 and the earlier keys and values are gone |
| A value that is not shaped like an id was sent as typed | On a command with name lookup it is looked up by name; an unknown or ambiguous name exits 1 |
| `--status nope` was sent and the server answered | A value outside a flag's closed list exits 1 before the request is sent |
| `hoody files get` had mode flags (`--stat`, `--preview`, `--grep`, `--glob`, `--q`, `--thumbnail`, `--zip`) | It only lists a directory or reads a file; each flag exits 2 and names its command |
| A PUT or DELETE answered with success was sent again when reading its answer failed | It is not sent again unless it carries the idempotency key its server honours; the call ends with that failure |
| A request middleware that threw could be retried and replayed by `onError` | The call ends with `REQUEST_REFUSED`, or with the `ApiError` the middleware threw; a refused WebSocket `connect()` carries the original error on `cause` |
| `agent.sessions.startTurnAndStream()` stayed open after its turn | `agent.sessions.turns.start(id, data, { stream: true })` ends after its own turn; `followSession: true` keeps it open |
| Aborting the `signal` of `streamAgentPrompt` dropped the connection and the turn ran on, while `done` said `cancelled` | The abort cancels the turn on the agent; `done` rejects when the cancel fails or no turn id arrives |
| `kitAuth: { type: 'jwt', token, header }` sent the bare token under a custom header | It sends `Bearer <jwt>` |
| A generated WebSocket message type that merges several frames required a field when one frame did | The field is optional unless every frame has it, and a field the frames type differently is a union; check for `undefined` and update exhaustive handling |
| An option that only the other form of a method takes was dropped without a word (`agent.sessions.turns.start({ gatePolicy })` without `stream: true`, `display.screenshots.capture({ metadata: true, base64 })`) | The call throws a `ValidationError` |
| `formatEd25519SshPublicKey` and `parseEd25519SshPublicKey` accepted a line break inside a key line | Both throw |
| A WebSocket `connect()` given a `kitAuth` or credential header it could not send warned and opened | It rejects with `WebSocketCredentialNotSentError` and no socket opens; a `kitAuth` set on the client still warns and opens |
| `mount()` sent a query-parameter token as `Authorization: Bearer` | It refuses the token before anything is sent |
| A kit URL was built from a base URL that is an IP address | The call throws; use a DNS name (`localhost` works for a local API) |
| With `http://api.localhost` as the base URL, kit hosts were built under `api.localhost` | They are built under `computers.localhost` |
| `-o json` on `hoody mount`, `unmount`, `share list`, `local lock status` and `doctor`, `pipe send` and `share`, `run close` and `status` and the `hoody boturls` commands printed the plain lines | It prints JSON |
| The curl channel kept unread response bytes without a limit | A stream more than 64 MiB behind fails with `body_buffer_full`; `maxBufferedBodyBytes` sets the limit |
| A foreground `mount()` called `process.exit` on SIGINT, SIGTERM or SIGHUP | It unmounts and leaves the exit to your app; `hoody mount` exits 129 after a SIGHUP, where it exited 0 |
| `hoody login --token` exited 1 when the token could not be saved | It exits 3 |
| In a pipe, `hoody auth tokens create` and `copy`, `hoody proxy aliases create` and `update`, `hoody exec templates generate`, `hoody servers commands run`, and `hoody auth 2fa setup confirm` and `disable` printed one success line | They print a short block with the value; use `-o json` to read one field |
| A command answered with no body printed `null` | It prints nothing; `-o json` still prints `null`, and the exit code is unchanged |
| In a pipe, a success line named its operation (`set-display-clipboard completed successfully.`) | It reads as plain words (`Clipboard set.`, `File copied.`, `Computer copy started.`) |
| `hoody terminal sessions wait` printed a success line and exited 0 when it timed out | It says what happened; a timeout, a process that exited, a re-initialized terminal or a pattern that cannot be evaluated exits 1, in every output format |
| `hoody exec scripts validate` printed a success line and exited 0 for an invalid script | An invalid script prints the results and exits 1, in every output format |
| `hoody terminal commands run --env BAD` was sent, and the kit dropped the entry | An entry with no `=` or an empty key exits 1 before the request is sent, as `hoody shell --env` does |
| `hoody config get -o yaml` printed JSON, and `hoody config validate -o json` printed the text report | YAML, and JSON |
| Under `-o json` or `-o yaml`, a sign-in that needs a two-factor code printed its text | It prints the challenge as JSON on stdout and nothing on stderr; the exit code stays 2 |
| With the local lock's credential policy on, `-u` with `HOODY_PASSWORD` (or `HOODY_USERNAME` with `-p`) signed in | The command exits 14, as for two flags |
| `hoody events stream`, `computers wait`, `config validate` and `config get` took any `-o` value | An `-o` value the command does not print exits 2 |
| `hoody agent prompt --session-id <id> --model <model>` dropped `--model`, `--agent` and `--cwd` on an existing session | It exits 2 before the turn is sent |
| `hoody agent prompt` and `hoody shell` sent the account token when the computer gave no kit token | They send no account token (see Security) |
| Over `ssh hoody.com`, `hoody tunnel expose`, `tunnel pull`, `egress local start` and `hoody pipe` with a local command or socket ran on the gateway | They exit 1 and say to run them on your own machine; `hoody share`, `mount` and `unmount` exit 2 |
| `hoody open <service>` over SSH started an opener on the remote machine | With none of `BROWSER`, `DISPLAY` or `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` set, it prints the URL |
| A token-authenticated SSH session on a terminal counted as scripted | It gets prompts and confirmations |
| `override_height`, `web_notifications` and `notification_connection_type` were accepted by `client.embeds.display.client()` | A call that passes one throws `PARAM_EXCLUDED` or `PARAM_DEAD` |

#### Upgrade notes

- **A container is now a computer, everywhere you can type or read the word.** The CLI group is `hoody computers`, the flag is `--computer` (`-c` is unchanged), the SDK reads `client.api.computers` and `withComputer(id)`, and fields, permission names, events and error codes follow (`computerId`, `computers`, `computer.*`, `INVALID_COMPUTER_ID`, `COMPUTER_PROVISIONING`). `--container` exits 2 with a message that names `--computer`. [MIGRATION-2026-10-NAMES.md](docs/MIGRATION-2026-10-NAMES.md) has the table.
- **A request that carries a retired name is refused.** The API answers `400 UPGRADE_REQUIRED` (the Hoody Kits answer `upgrade_required`) and changes nothing; the message lists the names that changed. The CLI shows a banner with the update command.
- **Old scripts on the old paths keep the old field names for one release.** A call to an old `/containers/` path returns `containers`, `containerId`, `container_id` and `container` in its JSON, with a `Deprecation: true` header, so a script that only reads responses keeps working. The values (error codes, event names) are the new ones, requests that carry a retired name are still refused, and the old paths are removed in the next release. The canonical `/computers/` paths and the new SDK and CLI use the new names only.
- **Computer URLs use `computers` in the host.** The CLI and SDK use `https://{projectId}-{computerId}-{service}-{n}.{server}.computers.hoody.com`.
- **The claim header is `X-Hoody-Computer-Claim`.**
- **Bare `hoody agent` opens the Hub in the terminal.** `hoody agent` with no subcommand, or with only a computer, now runs `hoody hub`. It no longer attaches to the agent's own terminal app, which stays at the computer's `agent-1` link. A computer you name is checked first, but the Hub does not open on it. `hoody agent prompt`, `hoody agent login` and the other subcommands are unchanged.
- **The older computer variables and saved mount and share records still work.** `HOODY_COMPUTER` and `HOODY_COMPUTER_ID` name the default computer, and `HOODY_CONTAINER` and `HOODY_CONTAINER_ID` are still read when neither is set. Mount and share records written by an older version still list, unmount and clean up.

- **`box.terminal.run()` and `hoody shell <id> -- <command>` return the command's exact output.** A one-shot command ran in a terminal, so its output came back as terminal text: stderr mixed into stdout, CRLF line endings, escape sequences removed and whitespace trimmed. It now runs as a process of its own: stdout and stderr come back apart and byte for byte, and the exit code is the command's own (128+N for a signal, 124 on a timeout). In the SDK the raw bytes are in `stdoutBytes` / `stderrBytes`, `exitCode` is `null` when a signal ended the command and `signal` names it. Output cut at the terminal kit's capture limit is no longer returned as complete: the CLI says so and never exits 0, and `terminal.run()` rejects with `code: 'OUTPUT_TRUNCATED'` (the cut result is `err.result`). `stdout` now usually ends with a newline: `trimEnd()` it where you parsed the trimmed text. `--terminal-id N` / `terminalId: N`, `--shell tmux` and the SSH bridge still run in a terminal and still return terminal text. A computer whose terminal kit is too old for this fails with "the computer's terminal kit did not return the command's exact output; update the terminal kit in the computer" (CLI exit 70).
- **`hoody agent` exits 1 when the agent refuses a write.** `hoody agent definitions delete`, `definitions reset`, `sessions tools run`, `tools run`, `workflows get`, `workflows set` and `workflows delete` printed the agent's refusal and exited 0, so a script under `set -e` took a refused write as done. They now print the answer in the chosen format, add `✗ hoody <command> was refused: <the agent's message>` on stderr, and exit 1.
- **`hoody exec modules install` exits 1 when a module fails to install.** It printed a green tick and exited 0 even when the answer counted failed modules. It now prints the answer, then `✗ <n> of <total> modules failed to install: <names>`, and exits 1, in human and JSON output.
- **CLI messages name the command you ran.** The "refused", "reported success: false", "may be destructive. Continue?", `--out-file does not apply to …`, "answered with an HTML page / a file / binary data", "this body is …" and `--realm all does not apply to …` messages now say `hoody <command>` (the documented command, also when you typed an alias), or "the command" outside a normal CLI run, where they named an internal operation id.
- **The `bot` namespace and the bot kit are removed.** `client.bot.*` (registrations, kit health, manifest and key rotation), the `hoody-sdk/bot` subpath export and every `hoody bot` command are gone, and computers no longer run the bot kit, so its addresses no longer answer. Remove those calls. Agent Bots are a different feature and are unchanged: `agent.bots.*` and `hoody agent bots`.
- **An event stream refused with an HTTP error rejects with the server's error.** `stream()` and `streamEvents()` calls (such as `agent.audio.createSpeech(data, { stream: true })`, `agent.completions.create()` with `stream` and `agent.bots.followLogs()`) rejected an answer of 400 or more that is not an event stream with code `STREAM_HTTP_ERROR` and a fixed sentence. They now reject with the same `ApiError` a plain call gets: the server's code, message and body, and `Retry-After`. An answer with no code still gets `STREAM_HTTP_ERROR`. Transport, timeout and abort errors keep their fixed text.
- **WebSocket clients hand an error frame from the server to `onError` as an `Error`.** A frame of type `error` reached `onError` as the bare frame object. It is now an `Error` whose `name` is `"WebSocketServerError"`, with `code` (the frame's `code`, else `error.code`, else `error.type`, else `server_error`), `message` and the original `frame`; the connection stays open. This applies to every generated WebSocket client that declares an error frame, among them the curl channel, notifications and the Bot voice and realtime clients.
- **The display viewer's `mediasource_video` option is removed.** It is gone from the viewer and from the `display.client` query type of `client.embeds.display.client()`, so TypeScript code that passes it no longer compiles. Remove it.
- **Mounting with a password or a header credential needs a newer rclone.** `hoody mount` and `mount()` now give rclone a password on standard input, which needs rclone 1.53 or newer, and header credentials (a token sent in a header other than `Authorization`, a computer claim) in the mount's private config, which needs rclone 1.56 or newer. With an older rclone, or one whose version cannot be read, such a mount is refused before anything starts, and the message names the version needed; `buildAuthDelivery()` throws for header credentials that need the config. IP and `Authorization` bearer mounts have no minimum.
- **`agent.bots.list()` items can be a locked row.** A Bot that another agent process on the same machine serves is listed with only `id`, `realm`, `address` and `owned_elsewhere`, and nothing else of it is read. The item type is now a union, so TypeScript code that reads `name`, `session_id` or another Bot field on a list item no longer compiles until it narrows (`'owned_elsewhere' in bot`). Calls on such a Bot answer 409 `bot_owned_elsewhere` (see Changed).
- **`hoody files backends sftp create` needs `--user`.** The CLI sent `user: "user"` when `--user` was left out, a default that came from the files kit's API description. The kit requires `user`, so the CLI now refuses the command without `--user`. ftp and smb sent no default and are unchanged; their help now says the files service's own account is used when `--user` is left out.
- **Error-code types drop codes no answer carries.** TypeScript code that names one of these in `isApiErrorCode<…>(e, '<code>')` no longer compiles; remove the code. `OPERATION_NOT_PERMITTED_ON_EXPIRED` and `CONTAINER_ACCESS_SUSPENDED` on `api.computers` lifecycle calls (now `COMPUTER_EXPIRED` and `ACCESS_SUSPENDED`, see Changed), `OPERATION_NOT_PERMITTED_ON_EXPIRED` on `api.computers.delete()` and `update()`, `INVALID_PARAMETER` on `curl.sessions.delete()`, `MOUNT_COUNT_LIMIT` and `MOUNTS_UNSUPPORTED` on `files.mounts.create()`, `MOUNTS_UNSUPPORTED` on `files.mounts.update()`, `INVALID_PAGINATION` on `watch.events.stream()` and `connect()`, `INVALID_PAGINATION` and `INVALID_CURSOR` on `watch.watchers.create()` and `update()`, and `INVALID_CURSOR` on `watch.watchers.list()`.
- **`terminal.commands.list()` is typed.** The `data` of its answer is `{ terminal_id, command_count, commands }`, where it was `Record<string, unknown>`, so code that reads another key of `data` no longer compiles. The entries in `commands` stay untyped; their fields are listed under Changed.

### Added

The main entries, one line each. Every entry in full is in `CHANGELOG.md` under "Full record of 1.0.1", after the oldest release.

#### CLI

- `hoody use` shows and saves where commands act.
- `hoody setup` finishes setting up your account.
- `hoody welcome` opens a sign-in menu.
- `hoody signup` handles the browser check.
- `hoody computers urls list [computer] [--pick]` shows the Hoody Kit URLs of a computer, whole.
- `hoody ai freeusage get` shows how much of each of your servers' free AI allowance is used.
- `hoody browser markdown get` returns a tab's page as Markdown.
- `hoody auth 2fa enrollment undo --link-token <token>` undoes a two-factor enrollment from the "this was not me" link in the alert email.
- `hoody vm display get <name>` shows the browser address of a VM's screen.
- `hoody servers plans quote`, `servers subscriptions quote`, `buy` and `upgrade` take `--region`.
- Proxy logs can be narrowed to one Bot URL.
- Names in place of ids: on a command with name lookup, you can type the name where it takes an id.
- `--http` and `--http-curl` print the request a command would send.
- `--mask-urls` (or `HOODY_MASK_URLS=1`) shortens every 24-character hex id on screen to its first and last 4 characters.
- The `stream` commands follow a log.
- `--watch [interval]` redraws a list in place.
- `--until-done` waits for an asynchronous operation to finish.
- The 18 destructive commands that never asked now confirm on a terminal.
- Structured CLI inputs accept JSON inline, from a file with `@<path>`, or from stdin with `@-`.
- `hoody use -c`, `hoody agent` and `hoody config set computer` take a computer name as well as an id.
- Four new `hoody agent` commands: spend caps, an agent's reasoning effort and a session's slash commands.
- `hoody unmount --force` unmounts without waiting for files still uploading.
- A mount reports files an earlier mount of the same URL left unsent.
- `hoody unmount --all` / `--computer` with `-o json` say whether each failed path is still mounted.
- `hoody terminal commands run` takes `--command-env` and `--command-cwd`.
- `hoody browser viewport set --viewport null` sends `viewport: null` (a responsive viewport).
- `hoody exec templates generate -o raw` prints the generated code.
- Shell completion follows today's API descriptions.

#### Hub

- `hoody hub` is a new command: it opens the Hub in the terminal.

#### SDK

- The `vm` namespace runs virtual machines inside a computer.
- `ApiError` says whether the request reached the server.
- Three client options for keyed retries and redaction.
- A request middleware can refuse a request, and the refusal is final.
- `onStepUp` answers a two-factor challenge once.
- `fetchCredentials` and `fetchCache` pass `credentials` and `cache` to `fetch`.
- `maxResponseBytes` caps a buffered body, and `maxStreamBytes` caps a whole stream.
- A WebSocket refuses to connect when a credential you passed for it cannot be sent.
- `api.servers.plans.listPublic({ locale })` lists the server plans, and works without signing in.
- `curl.run()` JSON responses have `raw_headers`, every header line in order.
- `exec.modules.listBundled()` returns `sdkUpdate`.
- `files.archives.extract()` results and errors carry `replaced` and `replaced_count`.
- `cron.events.stream()`, `cron.events.connect()` and `cron.runs.list()`, with `hoody cron events stream` and `hoody cron runs list`.
- `cron.crontabs.set()` takes `ifMatch`, and crontab reads return `etag`.
- `terminal.sessions.wait()` takes `fresh` and `after_seq`.
- `files.touch(path, { touch, type })` takes `type`.
- Web Push on `notifications.push`.

#### Agent and Bots

- `hoody agent sessions images get` and `agent.sessions.getImage()` read an image attached to a session message.
- Speech in and out of the agent: `agent.audio`.
- Spoken messages to a Bot.
- Talk to a Bot live (SDK only).
- The live Bot clients carry audio.
- Bot speech settings and a realtime guardrail.
- Bots can take a phone call through Twilio. Known limitation: this release was tested against a Twilio Media Streams simulator only; a live Twilio account is not supported in this release.
- Agent sessions can wait for an outcome in another session's work.
- Bots can pick the model each new delegate starts with.
- Bot chat channels: `agent.bots.channels`.
- `agent.bots.channels.getSettings()` and `updateSettings()`.
- `agent.bots.resumeAll()` (CLI: `hoody agent bots resume`) brings back suspended Bots.

### Changed

#### CLI

- On a terminal, `hoody` commands print a tab line and their data in a fixed layout.
- A command's `--help` is one line per flag, with examples, on a terminal and in pipes.
- On a terminal, a missing or mistyped input prints a short block instead of the whole help.
- Bare `hoody` prints one page and exits 0 in every mode.
- Messages that said to run `hoody` now say `hoody setup`.
- A name works where an id did.
- `--realm <name>` accepts a realm name from your realm aliases and sends the realm id.
- On a terminal, the 18 destructive commands that never asked now confirm.
- On a terminal, 13 more delete commands ask before they delete.
- `hoody open <service>` over SSH with no browser available prints the URL.
- `hoody --help` opens with the same context line as bare `hoody` and honours `HOODY_PROFILE`.
- On a terminal, confirmations ask on stderr and name the target.
- Commands that generate a value now print it in a pipe.
- Success lines and three tables say what the answer holds.
- Over the Hoody SSH gateway (`ssh hoody.com`), `hoody` runs no programs on the gateway and connects only to Hoody services.
- A flag with a closed list of values now refuses any other value before the request is sent.
- CLI commands and flags have their stable names, and the old spellings stop with exit 2.
- `--realm-id` and `--computer-id` are no longer CLI-wide flags.
- `hoody files get` and `files.get()` only list a directory or read a file.
- `hoody tunnel expose` and `hoody tunnel pull` take the local target only as their argument.
- `--inline` on `hoody pipe send`, and `--json` and `--no-progress` on `hoody pipe receive`, are removed.
- More CLI flags have their stable names, and the old spellings exit 2.
- More ids and paths are arguments instead of flags.
- Flags that did nothing, or that only the platform uses, are removed.
- `--json` on hand-written commands is replaced by the global `-o json`.
- `--format` no longer chooses the output of `hoody config get`, `config validate`, `events stream` and `computers wait`.
- The `open` commands take CLI flag names instead of the page's URL keys.
- `hoody events stream`, `computers wait`, `config validate` and `config get` refuse an `-o` value they do not print, with exit 2.
- `-o json` on the commands that took `--json` prints their JSON.
- `hoody agent <computer>` and `hoody shell <computer>` refuse a second, different computer typed with `-c` or `--computer`.
- A two-factor challenge is data under `-o json` and `-o yaml`.
- The SDK and CLI give a computer create 7 minutes and send it with an `Idempotency-Key`.
- A second Ctrl-C on a foreground `hoody mount` exits at once and leaves the path mounted.
- A kit's JSON answer is printed as sent.
- A required body field is a required flag.
- `computers update` has no `--env` any more.
- `browser restart` keeps the instance's settings.
- `notifications list` draws the notifications as a table, and `notifications restore` says what it did.
- `hoody shell --shell tmux -- <command>` is refused (exit 2).
- `--terminal-id` must be a positive whole number.
- `db transactions run` needs `--transaction`.

#### SDK

- `agent.sessions.turns.start(id, data, { stream: true })` ends after its own turn.
- `streamAgentPrompt` waits for its own turn and cancels only that turn.
- A PUT or DELETE that the server answered with success is not sent again when reading its answer fails.
- A request middleware that throws now ends the call, where before the call could run it again.
- A localhost or IP address base URL has one computers domain, in the SDK and the CLI.
- 20 account methods no longer take a `_realm` option, and always go to the base host.
- `undici` now needs 8.12.2 or later.
- Aborting `streamAgentPrompt`'s `signal` cancels the turn on the agent.
- A JWT under a custom header is sent as `Bearer <jwt>`.
- An SDK app that runs a foreground `mount()` keeps control of its own shutdown.
- `TerminalClient` bounds its queue of unread stream output at 8 MiB.
- SDK methods have their stable names.
- An old option key throws `RetiredOptionError`.
- State getters became methods, and the old getters throw.
- `forceIPv4` and `forceIPv4Cache` are now `forceIpv4` and `forceIpv4Cache`.
- Exported type names follow the method names.
- More SDK option keys have their stable names, and the old keys throw `RetiredOptionError`.
- An old hand-written method name throws a `TypeError` that names the new method.
- An option that only the other form of a method takes now throws a `ValidationError`.
- Agent stream errors say when to send the message again.
- `agent.completions.create()` waits up to 125 seconds, and an operation's own deadline no longer shortens yours.

#### Account and computers

- Creating a computer is safe to retry with an `Idempotency-Key`.
- A name already in use is reported with the computer that has it.
- Every account API 409 carries a specific error `code`.
- A failed start, stop or restart says what state the computer is in.
- A deleted computer's URLs stop at once.
- Every sign-in is a session you can list and end on its own.
- A kit address of a stopped computer answers at once with 503 `COMPUTER_STOPPED`.
- hoody-exec no longer moves scripts to a new hoody-sdk prerelease or major on its own.
- Proxy permission and hook writes refuse bodies that do not match their schema.
- Updating or copying an auth token refuses fields it does not know.
- Clearing events with no filter needs `all: true`.
- Auth tokens can read events but not delete them.
- An auth token cannot change the account profile.
- A computer claim needs exec permission.
- A project's member list needs a member-management permission.
- The project's proxy permission document is withheld from realm-scoped requests.
- An expired computer answers 409 `COMPUTER_EXPIRED`.

#### Hoody Kit

- `curl.run({ response: 'json' })` keeps the first `Set-Cookie` in `headers` and adds `raw_headers` with every header line.
- SQLite refuses values that do not fit the statement's placeholders.
- Cron publishes its events and keeps a list of runs.
- Notifications reach enrolled browsers and phones as Web Push, also with no page open.
- Display screenshots are several times faster.
- A display that cannot start for missing software reports `details.reason: "dependency_missing"`.
- `terminal.commands.run()` refuses a negative, too large or non-numeric `timeout`.
- A daemon program with a `ready_port` is reported started as soon as it serves.
- The first browser start in a computer no longer downloads Chrome.
- A browser navigation sent right after a failed one loads its page.
- A pipe transfer whose sender left mid-way is reported failed.
- Extracting an archive over existing files no longer destroys them when the archive is damaged.
- A create-only upload that loses a race answers 412.
- A server-side mount of a WebDAV backend no longer overwrites a newer change made on the remote.
- `files.append()` refuses a cut-off body instead of appending part of it.
- `sqlite.sql.runTransaction()` cannot change the KV store's internal tables.
- Run can search Docker Hub (off by default).
- A files backend connect with wrong credentials no longer replaces working ones.
- A read from a remote backend that does not answer no longer hangs, and a remote that refuses is no longer a 404.
- SQLite keeps the latest commits of a database file that is renamed or replaced while open.
- SQLite history writes no longer slow down as a key's history grows.
- The daemon no longer writes program environment values to its logs.

#### Agent and Bots

- A running agent session stops using a provider key you deleted or replaced.
- `agent.sessions.setModel()` refuses a model the catalogue does not list.
- An agent whose Hoody sign-in was revoked or has expired says so.
- Agent errors about setting up credentials on a computer say what did not happen and what to do.
- A Bot takes `instructions`, and its log can be read from the end.
- A burst of SSH logins no longer locks out a valid key with "Permission denied".
- A Bot served by another agent process answers 409 `bot_owned_elsewhere`.
- Bots are suspended when the agent's account signs out, changes, or no longer serves the Bot's realm.
- Bots can talk on Discord, Slack and Telegram through chat channels.
- Bots report their channels and whether a turn is running.

### Fixed

#### CLI

- `hoody help` refuses a command that does not exist.
- `hoody agent logs list` and `hoody agent logs export` read every log source again.
- `hoody agent prompt --wait` waits for the turn.
- Required nested fields are checked before anything is sent.
- A text answer prints as written on a terminal.
- `--until-done` says when a command cannot wait.
- Event streams without a resume cursor no longer say they can resume.
- Tunnels with more than one socket (`maxConnections` above 1) keep each stream on one socket until it ends.
- A command answered with no body prints nothing instead of `null`.
- `hoody shell` no longer leaves stale text on screen after a dialog closes.
- A save through the encrypted local store no longer pairs a new API address with the old token.
- `hoody local lock enable` no longer stops every later command when its password step fails.
- The Windows installer (`install.ps1`) adds its folder to your PATH.
- `hoody agent bots voice connect` reaches the agent of the selected computer.
- `hoody browser screenshots capture` works again without `--mask`.
- `hoody` commands that only work on the base host no longer fail with 403 from a realm.
- `hoody login --token` exits 3 when the token is accepted but cannot be saved.
- `hoody agent prompt` refuses `--model`, `--agent` and `--cwd` on an existing session.
- `hoody agent prompt` on a busy session waits for its own turn.
- `hoody computers stats` and `hoody projects stats` now show the readable usage figures in the standalone binary too.
- `hoody unmount` right after saving a file no longer loses it.
- Ctrl+C on `hoody agent prompt` cancels the turn.
- Interrupting a CLI write tells you it may still complete.
- Uploads that are cut off on the way no longer replace the file.
- A pipe transfer cut short is no longer seen as complete.
- `hoody mount` no longer puts credentials on a command line.
- The CLI never shortens a URL.
- An error printed through a pipe shows the server's message, not its JSON.
- A validation error lists what was refused.
- A computer name works wherever a computer is taken.
- `hoody shell <computer> -i -- <command>` passes piped input to the command.
- `-o yaml` reads back as the data it printed.
- `hoody exec <script>` sees a script right after `hoody exec scripts write`, `move` or `delete`.
- An answer that holds a list shows its rows on a terminal.
- `-q` silences the success line of `hoody config set`, `hoody config clear` and `hoody use`.
- `hoody agent prompt --wait` exits 1 when the turn was cancelled.
- `-o ndjson` on a command that answers one item prints the whole item on one line.
- Confirmation questions on a terminal say what the command does.
- `hoody exec scripts types validate`, `returntype validate` and `dependencies validate` exit 1 when the result is invalid.
- `--help` lists the values a flag with a short fixed set of values accepts.
- A mistyped command before `--help` is refused.
- A stopped or paused computer is not retried.
- Pipe commands name the cause when the pipe server cannot be reached.
- A GitHub sign-in recovery is shown as the command.

#### SDK

- A kit answer is never read as an envelope.
- `startLocalExit` sends the egress credential on its startup exit-IP check.
- `mount()` refuses a token sent as a query parameter.
- `formatEd25519SshPublicKey` and `parseEd25519SshPublicKey` refuse a line break inside a key line.
- The curl channel holds at most 64 MiB of unread response bytes per stream.
- A token that already starts with `Bearer ` is sent once.
- Calls through `withRealm()` keep the base URL's path.
- Aborting a call while it waits to retry rejects at once.
- `agent.bots.ask()` does not take a cut turn's marker for the reply.

### Security

#### CLI and SDK

- `hoody agent prompt` no longer sends your account token to the computer.
- A CLI request to a full URL on another site no longer carries your Hoody credentials.
- `hoody agent prompt -o pretty` no longer passes control characters from the agent's stream to your terminal.
- Use undici 8.12.2 or later with the SDK.
- More CLI views replace control characters and bidi marks in text that comes from a computer or the API.
- A stream frame with a `__proto__` key can no longer change what `-o pretty` draws.
- Shown-ID masking covers the generic stream line.
- `hoody boturls ask`, `answer`, `chat` and `updates list` replace control characters in what a Bot sends.
- `hoody update` cleans the failure detail of an update channel that fails its signature check.
- `hoody help <path>` never runs a command.
- Sign-in passwords no longer follow a redirect to another site.
- The SDK never resends a request body to another site on a redirect.
- Error messages no longer quote secret values.
- `hoody shell <computer> --env NAME=VALUE` refuses a name that is not a variable name.

## [1.0.0-beta.17] — 2026-10-08

**Upgrading from beta.16.** This release is installed as `latest`, and scripts that load `hoody-sdk` from npm at run time (hoody-exec scripts) pick it up within six hours. Each breaking change, with what to do; details under Breaking below.

- Timeouts: `err.code` is `ETIMEDOUT`, and `ABORTED` now means your own `signal`. Check `'ETIMEDOUT'` where you checked `'ABORTED'` for a timeout.
- `mount({ container })`: pass `client: yourHoodyClient` (or `containersDomain`).
- `api.servers.listRegions()`, `api.auth.getConfig()`, `api.auth.oauth.authorize()` / `startLaunch()`, `api.auth.device.start()` / `verifyCode()` / `login()` / `deny()` / `poll()`, `api.users.retrySetup()`: read `r.data.<field>`, not `r.data.data.<field>`.
- Types named `*Response` (list items, error bodies): read fields directly (`items[0].user`; error bodies carry `code` and `error`), not through `.data`.
- `box.files.list()`: drop `responseType` and `rawResponse` from its options; use `getPage()` for raw bytes.
- Target in the wrong argument: move `projectId`, `containerId`, `serviceIndex`, `serverName` or `server` out of the options into the method's target argument (or call on `withContainer()`), or the call throws `ValidationError`.
- `exec.scripts.write()` / `writeFile()`: fix the script, or pass `validate: false` to store it unvalidated.
- Account token scope: a kit host under your API's domain now gets `kitAuth`, never the account token. Set `kitAuth` where a kit needs a credential.
- OpenAPI document: read it as 3.1.0, take Files query-string and WebDAV routes from `x-additional-paths`, and kit servers from each operation.
- `hoody kv set --if-match <value>` for compare-and-swap: use `--if-value <value>`; `--if-match` is now the ETag.
- `hoody curl run --proxy <url>`: put it before the command, `hoody --proxy <url> curl run …`, if you meant the CLI's proxy; the kit takes none.
- A saved sign-in from before beta.16 without an API address: run `hoody login` again, or set `hoody config set baseUrl <url>`.
- `--kit-auth password`: set `HOODY_KIT_PASSWORD` in scripts; on a terminal you are asked for it.
- `hoody files search`: parse JSON (it no longer prints HTML); drop `--json`.
- `hoody mount`, `unmount`, `tunnel expose|pull`: scripts that expected exit 0 after a failed probe, a busy unmount or Ctrl-C now get 1, 130 or 143; add `--no-probe` when rclone reaches the kit through a proxy.
- A container's services still starting: a request the SDK waited on fails with `err.code === 'KIT_NOT_READY'` (status 502) instead of `BACKEND_GATEWAY_ERROR`; the edge error is `err.cause`.
- CLI lists print every page: pass `--limit N` where a script expected the first page only.
- Account API errors: match the error body's code (`INVALID_TOKEN`, `INSUFFICIENT_PERMISSIONS`, `ACCOUNT_BANNED`, ...), not the text "Unauthorized" or "Forbidden".
- `cron` and `curl` creates: an identical repeat now returns the existing entry (200) and a conflicting one 409; handle 409 where a script created the same name twice.
- Agent calls: rename the `XHoodyRealm` option to `realm`. Display: pass `windowId` as a string.
- Platform (services updated with this release): a realm-restricted token cannot lose its last realm (create a new token instead); sign in once after the update (refresh tokens are single-use now); SQLite enforces foreign keys (run `PRAGMA foreign_key_check`); `curl.jobs.getResult()` answers 200 with the target's status in `X-Curl-Status`; `curl.schedules.create()` refuses unknown fields. `api.auth.tokens.create()` refuses fields it does not know (400 `UNKNOWN_FIELD`, remove them); an `http`/`https` alias needs a `port` (400 `PORT_REQUIRED`). Exec, SQLite, daemon and files lists refuse query parameters they do not read (400): remove misspelt filters.

### Breaking

- **`mount({ container })` needs the API the container belongs to.** Pass `client` (your `HoodyClient`) or `containersDomain`; without either it throws. The kit URL used to be built on one fixed platform domain whatever API you used, so a mount on another platform went to the wrong host. `mount({ kitUrl })` is unchanged, and `hoody mount <containerId>` uses the configured API.
- **A request timeout now has code `ETIMEDOUT`, and `ABORTED` means your `signal` aborted the call.** Both used to be `ABORTED` with the message "Request timed out after <ms>ms", even when you aborted the call yourself after 300 ms; the number shown was the operation's default timeout. A timeout keeps that message. An abort says "Request aborted by the caller", followed by the reason when you passed one to `abort()`. Code that checks `err.code === 'ABORTED'` to detect a timeout must check `'ETIMEDOUT'`. Neither is retried. This applies to the Node and browser clients; the CLI already used `ETIMEDOUT` for a timeout.
- **The published OpenAPI document (`hoody-sdk/openapi.json`, `openapi.yaml`) is OpenAPI 3.1.0.** It was stamped 3.0.3 while it already held 3.1 schemas. `nullable: true` is now a `null` member of `type`, `anyOf` or `enum`, and `exclusiveMinimum`/`exclusiveMaximum` are numbers. The Files routes addressed by a query string (such as `/?download_history`) and the WebDAV methods are listed under the top-level `x-additional-paths` key instead of `paths`, because their keys are not valid 3.1 path templates; their operation IDs are unchanged. The top-level `servers` now lists the account API only, and each kit operation names its own kit server template. A tool that reads only `paths`, or that takes kit hosts from the top-level `servers`, must read the new places.
- **Ten account API methods return their data one level up.** `api.servers.listRegions()`, `api.auth.getConfig()`, `api.auth.oauth.authorize()`, `api.auth.oauth.startLaunch()`, `api.auth.device.start()`, `.verifyCode()`, `.login()`, `.deny()`, `.poll()` and `api.users.retrySetup()` answer without a `message` field, and were wrapped twice. Read `r.data.regions`, not `r.data.data.regions`. Their response types changed the same way.
- **Types whose schema name ends in `Response` have their declared shape.** About 75 component types, such as `RawCrontabResponse`, `WatcherResponse`, `CurlResponse` and the SQLite result types, were typed as a whole `{ statusCode, message, data }` response, so `items[0].user` did not compile on `cron.crontabs.list()` and `items[0].data.user` did. They now carry the fields the kit sends. The error component types (`ErrorResponse`, `CurlErrorResponse` and the like) now have `code` and `error` instead of `message` and `data`. No type was renamed or removed.
- **`box.files.list(dir)` resolves to the directory listing type.** It was typed as the union `getPage()` returns, raw bytes included, so `r.data.paths` did not compile. `responseType` and `rawResponse` are no longer accepted in its options.
- **A kit call with its target in the wrong argument throws instead of running on the client's own container.** Since beta.16, `curl.storage.delete()`, `egress.upstream.set()`, `notes.document.set()`, `notes.document.update()` and `agent.platform.bootstrapToken()` take their options before `_templateVars`. A call in the beta.15 order, such as `box.curl.storage.delete(path, { projectId, containerId, server })`, put the target in the options, where it was ignored. Every kit method, its `…All()` and `…Iterator()` helpers included, now throws a `ValidationError` that names the argument to use when its options or request options carry `projectId`, `containerId`, `serviceIndex`, `serverName` or `server`, unless the method takes a parameter of that name.
- **`exec.scripts.write()` and `exec.scripts.writeFile()` validate the script by default**, as the kit does when it is called directly. The SDK used to send `validate: false` unless you set it, so a script with a syntax error was stored. Such a write now fails with 400 "Script validation failed". Markdown files and data files are still written without validation. Pass `validate: false` for the previous behaviour.
- **The account token is sent only to the account API and its realm hosts.** A realm host is one 24-character realm ID label in front of the API host. Every other host under the API's domain is treated as a kit or an external site: it gets the kit credential (`kitAuth`) and never the account token. With an API host that has no `api.` label, kit hosts used to receive the account token. The CLI applies the same rule to the URL it resolves, so `HTTPS://`, mixed-case schemes and `///host` spellings no longer carry the account token, and a 401 from an external URL is not retried with a renewed token.
- **`hoody kv set --if-match` sends the ETag only.** It used to fill both the `If-Match` header and the `if_match` query parameter, so a compare-and-swap on the value never succeeded. Compare-and-swap on the current value is the new `--if-value <value>`; `--if-match <etag>` is the conditional write on the entry's ETag.
- **`hoody curl run --proxy` is refused.** `curl run` has had no `--proxy` option since beta.16, so the flag was silently taken as the CLI's own proxy for the call to the kit. It now exits 1 and says to use the global form, `hoody --proxy <url> curl run …`, when that is what you meant.
- **A token saved without an API address is no longer used.** A sign-in saved before beta.16 can lack `baseUrl`; the CLI sent that token to the built-in default API whatever API you use now. It now sets the token aside and asks you to sign in again, or to name the API with `--base-url`, `HOODY_API_URL` or `hoody config set baseUrl <url>`. A config that spells the key `baseURL` gets a one-time notice.
- **`--kit-auth password` without a password asks for one or fails.** When `HOODY_KIT_PASSWORD` was unset, the CLI fell back to the kit token or sent no credential. It now prompts on the terminal (input hidden) when standard input and standard error are terminals and `--non-interactive` is not set, and exits with an error otherwise. `hoody tunnel expose`, `tunnel pull`, `share` and `pipe` follow the same rule.
- **`hoody files search` prints JSON results.** It printed the kit's HTML page unless you passed `--json`; it now always asks the kit for JSON, and `--json` is accepted and does nothing. With `-o json`, `-o yaml` or `-o ndjson`, an HTML answer exits 1 and prints nothing on standard output. The page-only options (`--theme`, `--chromeless` and the like) no longer change the output.
- **Agent methods take the realm as `realm`.** The `XHoodyRealm` option is gone from the SDK; pass `realm` instead (`"global"` or a realm ID). The request still carries the `X-Hoody-Realm` header, and the CLI keeps `--x-hoody-realm`.

#### Platform changes that ship with this release

- **Removing the last realm of a realm-restricted token is refused** with 409 `LAST_REALM_REMOVAL`, on `api.auth.tokens.removeRealm()` and on a token update that sets `realm_ids` to an empty list. It used to turn the token into one valid for every realm. Create a new token to widen access.
- **Refresh tokens are single-use.** Each refresh returns a new pair; the SDK and CLI save it as before. A refresh token used a second time is refused with 401 "Refresh token already used", and a reuse more than 30 seconds after the first use signs the account out on every device. Refresh tokens issued before this change are refused once, so every session signs in again one time.
- **SQLite enforces foreign keys by default.** Deleting a parent row that has `NO ACTION` or `RESTRICT` children answers 409 `SQL_CONSTRAINT`, `ON DELETE CASCADE` and `SET NULL` now run, and a child row that names a missing parent is refused. A request that runs `PRAGMA foreign_keys = OFF` runs with them off. Run `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` on an existing database to find rows that would now be refused.
- **`curl.jobs.getResult()` always answers 200.** The target's status is in the `X-Curl-Status` header. A stored 429 or 503 used to be replayed as the answer's own status, which made the SDK retry it.
- **`curl.schedules.create()` honours `enabled: false`** and refuses fields it does not know with 400. A schedule created with `enabled: false` used to start running. Against a curl kit older than this release, `enabled: false` is still ignored.
- **New refusals where a change was reported but not made.** Files: `chmod` and `chown` that the file system did not apply answer 409 `PERMISSIONS_NOT_APPLIED` or `OWNER_NOT_APPLIED`; a folder download over the archive limit (now 100,000 files) answers 422 `ARCHIVE_TOO_LARGE` instead of stopping at 10,000 files; a journal `since` that cannot be read answers 400, and a value below 10^11 is read as Unix seconds. Display: a key press or typed text with no window to receive it answers 409 `WINDOW_NOT_VIEWABLE`.
- **A port label that is not a valid port answers 404** (`http-99999`, `http-080`). It used to reach port 80.
- **The code editor's own address is governed by the `code` permission**, like the rest of the editor. An `http` permission rule no longer opens it.
- **Exec scripts.** `return` on a line of its own returns the value on the next line (it returned nothing). `$.env()`, `$.nothrow()` and `$.throws()` apply to one call only. Relative file paths, `process.cwd()` and spawned processes start in the script's data directory. A script that is not found answers with up to three likely routes in `suggestions`.

- **Token creation refuses fields it does not know** with 400 `UNKNOWN_FIELD`, naming the field; they used to be dropped silently, so a misspelt option had no effect. **An `http` or `https` alias needs a `port`** (400 `PORT_REQUIRED`), on create and on an update that switches an alias to `http`/`https`. A browser opening a port of a running container where nothing listens gets a 502 page that says so.
- **Files: uploads and copies never leave a partial file.** An upload that sends `X-Expected-Length` and ends short or runs long is refused with 400 and nothing is written. A copy is written under a temporary name and appears whole, or not at all, and keeps the source's permission bits. Unmounting with `uploads=keep` answers only after the writes it kept are listed.

- **List endpoints of the exec, SQLite, daemon and files kits refuse query parameters they do not read** with 400, naming each unknown parameter and listing the accepted ones. A misspelt filter used to be ignored, so the list came back unfiltered.
- **Account API 401 and 403 answers carry a machine code.** The error body's `error` is a code such as `MISSING_TOKEN`, `INVALID_TOKEN`, `TOKEN_EXPIRED`, `INVALID_CREDENTIALS`, `INVALID_OTP_CODE`, `ACCOUNT_BANNED` or `INSUFFICIENT_PERMISSIONS`, not the HTTP reason phrase ("Unauthorized", "Forbidden"). Status codes are unchanged. Sign-in, token refresh, two-factor verification, email verification and token exchange document `ACCOUNT_BANNED` (403).
- **Repeating a create no longer makes a duplicate.** `cron` entries and `curl` schedules: an identical repeat answers 200 with the existing entry, and a conflicting one answers 409 `ENTRY_EXISTS` or `SCHEDULE_EXISTS`. `watch.watchers.create()` takes an `Idempotency-Key`, and notes declares its `X-Idempotency-Key`.
- **Container permission checks verify a token's signature before anything else**, so a malformed or forged token is refused early. A JWT permission group can carry `header_authoritative` (boolean): when `true` and a configured header source is present, only header sources decide; when `false` or absent, any configured source with a valid token counts. A value that is not a JSON boolean is refused with 400.
- **The bot and code-editor kits scope their sign-in cookies to their own address** (`__Host-hb_session`, `hoody-code-auth`: no `Domain`, `Secure` over HTTPS). Sign in again to these pages once after the update.
- **A cookie that a container app sets for a parent domain is kept for the app's own address only.** Cookies set by page scripts, and cookies a browser already holds, keep their scope.

### Added

- **`curl.schedules.create()` takes `enabled`** (CLI: `hoody curl schedules create --enabled / --no-enabled`).
- **SQLite maintenance `restore`** restores a snapshot into an open database (`sqlite.databases.runMaintenance({ op: 'restore', src_path })`, CLI: `hoody db run --src-path`). It needs a SQLite kit from this release.
- **`mount()` options `probe` and `readyTimeoutMs`**, the `--no-probe` flag of `hoody mount`, and the exports `UnmountError`, `KitUrlSource`, `MOUNT_DIR_CACHE_TIME` and `MOUNT_WRITE_BACK`.

- **`hoody containers list --name <text>` and `hoody containers get <name>`.** `--name` matches part of the name, in any letter case, over every page. `get` accepts a name that matches exactly one container; an ambiguous name lists the matches. A 24-character hex value is always an ID.
- **`exec.sdkTypes.list()` looks up hoody-sdk methods** in the copy that exec scripts load, by kit (`kit: 'files'`) and words (`q`), with signatures and examples (CLI: `hoody exec sdk types list --kit files --q upload`). It needs the exec kit that ships with this release.

- **Stream frames are typed where the API documents them.** Each frame of `agent.bots.stream()`, `agent.logs.stream()`, `agent.changes.stream()`, `agent.completions.create({ stream: true })`, `agent.headless.stream()`, `agent.sessions.stream()` and `startTurnAndStream()` (each session event, such as `agent_done` or `confirm_request`, with `seq`, `incarnation` and the event under `event.data`, plus `lagged`, `replay_boundary` and `end`), `watch.events.stream()`, `sqlite.kv.streamChanges()` and `agent.tools.run(name, data, { stream: true })` now also has `data`, the parsed payload, and `declared`. After `if (frame.declared && frame.event === 'row')`, `frame.data` has the documented type. A frame the API does not document still arrives, with `declared: false`. `raw` is unchanged. New type: `ITypedStreamEvent`.
- **Each operation's documented error codes are a type, and `isApiErrorCode()` checks for them.** An operation that documents its error codes has a `<Namespace><Service><Method>ErrorCode` type, named like its Request and Response types (`AgentBotsSendMessageErrorCode`). `isApiErrorCode<AgentBotsSendMessageErrorCode>(e, 'rate_limited')` is true when `e` is an `ApiError` with that code, and a code the operation does not document does not compile.
- **`agent.bots.ask()` and `agent.bots.follow()` talk to a Bot.** `ask(id, text)` sends a message and resolves with the Bot's reply for that message, or with how its turn ended when the Bot wrote no reply. On a container whose agent keeps message keys the message is sent under an idempotency key, so a lost answer is retried without sending it twice; elsewhere it is sent once, without a key. The result's `keyed` says which: only when it is `true` does asking again with the returned `idempotencyKey` not send the message twice. `follow(id)` is an async iterator over the rows of the Bot's log from `since` on; it reconnects by itself and resumes after the last row it yielded. Both work in the browser client.
- **`idleTimeoutMs` on a stream call.** A stream that receives nothing, not even a keep-alive, for that long ends with an `ApiError` whose code is `ETIMEDOUT`. Without it a stream waits as before. `agent.bots.follow()` sets 45 seconds by default and reconnects when it expires.
- **`hoody agent bots delegates stop`** stops a Bot's delegate, and `hoody agent bots delegates list` shows each delegate's last command.
- **The Bot API.** `agent.bots.create()`, `list()`, `get()`, `update()`, `delete()`, `setGuardrails()`, `sendMessage()`, `getLog()`, `stream()`, `getArchive()`, `purgeArchive()`, `listDelegates()`, `stopDelegate()`, `forget()` and `reset()` create a Bot, talk to it and manage it. CLI: `hoody agent bots create`, `list`, `get`, `update`, `delete`, `guardrails set`, `messages send`, `log get`, `stream`, `archive get`, `archive purge`, `delegates list`, `delegates stop`, `forget` and `reset`.
