- **`invite_code` on GitHub and Google sign-in.** Both provider redirects now accept the invite code from a signup link, so a coupon captured by your own signup page survives the round trip to the provider and applies to the new account. Only a hash of the code travels in the OAuth state.

### Fixed

- **`hoody exec` wrote where the SDK does not read.** Commands in namespaces that take no explicit instance id resolved to service index `0`, while the SDK uses `1`. For `exec` that index *is* the namespace, so a script written through the CLI landed under `default/0/` and was invisible to the matching SDK call. Every namespace now defaults to `1`; an explicit `--…-id` still wins.
- **`hoody shell -- <command>` ran inside your interactive terminal.** One-shot commands now get a session of their own instead of executing in terminal 1 and inheriting its state.
- **`--terminal-id` was silently ignored.** The id was sent as a query parameter that the container gateway overwrites, so every session landed on terminal 1 regardless. It now travels where it takes effect: `--terminal-id 3` attaches to terminal 3. The same fix applies to the SDK's terminal helpers — SSH, local, desktop, and remote command execution — where a `terminal_id` option was dropped the same way, and to the connect URL returned after creating a session, which pointed at a fresh ephemeral terminal instead of the session just created.
- **Agent event streams resolved a different address than every other agent command** — index `0` instead of the singleton the rest of the CLI uses.

### Changed

- **`ramdisk` is now described accurately.** It read as a private allocation of up to half the host's memory that persisted across container reboots. In fact `/ramdisk` draws on a shared per-server pool — 512 MiB by default, never more than half the server's memory, shared with your other containers on that server — and its contents are **lost on a host reboot**, which is what makes it suitable for secrets and scratch data that must not reach disk. It counts against memory, not disk.
- Reference documentation regenerated for this release.

## [1.0.0-beta.7] — 2026-07-27

### Fixed

- **The incoming-shares example described a response the API never returns.** The receiver's view of a storage share leaves out the sharer's `alias`, `label`, `description` and `expiry_notified`, and the endpoint documents that — but its example response still listed all four. Anyone writing against that example was expecting four fields that never arrive. The example now matches the real payload, and the omitted field is described as the expiry-notification flag it actually is.

## [1.0.0-beta.6] — 2026-07-26

### Fixed

- **`isRetryableApiError` no longer breaks type narrowing.** The guard claimed to prove `ApiError`, so TypeScript read the negative branch as impossible and this ordinary check would not compile:

  ```ts
  if (isApiError(err) && !isRetryableApiError(err)) {
    console.log(err.status); // 'status' does not exist on type 'never'
  }
  ```

  It now narrows to `RetryableApiError`, so both branches type correctly — including when the value is a union such as `ApiError | null`.

### Added

- **`RetryableApiError` and `RetryableStatus` types**, exported from both the package root and the browser entry, so you can name what `isRetryableApiError` proves: an `ApiError` whose `status` is one of the retryable codes.

## [1.0.0-beta.5] — 2026-07-26

### Added

- **Device sign-in methods** — `oauthDeviceLogin` and `oauthDeviceDeny` round out the device authorization flow that backs "Continue in browser" sign-in. The confirmation page can now approve the waiting terminal with an email and password, or refuse it outright, so you can build your own confirmation screen instead of only the hosted one. The SDK is now 1077 typed methods, up from 1075.
- **`--no-allow-no-realm` and `--no-event-access`** on `hoody auth tokens create`. Both settings are on by default and the CLI previously had no way to turn either one off.
- **Clickable menus.** A left-click now picks a row in any `hoody` menu — the scroll wheel already moved the highlight — and the "Generate a strong password" button is a real click target. Terminals that don't report clicks behave exactly as before; the keyboard path is unchanged.

### Fixed

- **A mistyped password no longer ends `hoody signup`.** Resuming a pending signup used to drop you back to the shell with an account you could not finish creating. You now get another try, and pressing `r` sends a fresh verification link straight from that screen. An account that cannot succeed by retyping — locked out, or disabled — still stops immediately rather than asking three times.
- **Verification resends report what actually happened.** A resend that hits the hourly limit now says so, with the wait when one is supplied, instead of reporting that a link was on its way. The confirmation elsewhere reads "request accepted" rather than "sent", which is all the service will confirm.
- **`--version <value>` is no longer mistaken for a version request.** On the commands that take a `--version` value, a failure now reaches the built-in fixer normally; `help` used as a verb is recognized alongside `--help`.
- **A smaller `hoody` bundle.** The published CLI carried a copy of the repository's build-time metadata that nothing referenced at runtime — it only ever needed the version string. The bundle is about 8 KB lighter and now contains runtime code only.
- **Installers.** `install.sh` checks for `awk` up front, rejects version strings carrying whitespace or control characters, and parses checksum manifests unambiguously. `install.ps1` restores the caller's TLS setting on every exit path, and a failed upgrade now states whether the previous `hoody.exe` was put back — and where to find it if it was not.

### Changed

- Device-flow endpoint descriptions now spell out how the flow differs from RFC 8628 rather than referring to a note that was not part of the published docs.
- Reference documentation regenerated for this release.

## [1.0.0-beta.4] — 2026-07-23

### Changed

- **README overhaul.** Every code snippet, method shape, count, and behavioral claim was re-verified against the generated SDK and CLI, and the prose was tightened for clarity — shorter and easier to skim, with no change to the documented surface.
- **`AGENTS.md` reframed** around the three ways to build on Hoody: the SDK for scripting, and the CLI or plain HTTP/`curl` for terminal and agent use, with a task-by-task comparison of the same operation in each.

## [1.0.0-beta.3] — 2026-07-21

### Changed

- **Documentation overhaul.** A rewritten `AGENTS.md` build guide (now shipped in the package), a clearer README with a hands-on examples tour (spawn and drive a cloud browser, mount cloud storage and local drives both ways, drive the built-in agent, and serve any port over HTTPS), per-namespace descriptions in the [namespace index](./docs/reference/namespaces/_INDEX.md), and a plain-English `hoody chat` privacy guide.
- Licensing and ecosystem notes added to `LICENSE`.

### Fixed

- Reference documentation now renders generic type parameters correctly (e.g. `ApiResponse<unknown>`).

## [1.0.0-beta.2] — 2026-07-21

### Added

- **PKCE OAuth sign-in** — new authentication endpoints for building a browser-based sign-in flow against the Hoody API: `oauthAuthorize`, `oauthExchange`, and `getOAuthConfig`.
- **Identity claims** — `issueIdentityClaim` for issuing verifiable identity claims.

### Changed

- Refreshed the generated API reference under [`docs/reference/`](./docs/reference/) and the [README](./README.md) for clarity and accuracy.
- CLI: output, flag, and help-text consistency refinements across commands (no change to the command surface).

## [1.0.0-beta.1] — 2026-07-11

Initial public release of the Hoody SDK (`hoody-sdk`) and the bundled `hoody` CLI.

### Added

- Typed TypeScript client for the public Hoody API and the container **Kit** service layer — terminal, files, browser, display, code, agent, sqlite, cron, daemon, watch, notes, tunnels, and more. See the [README](./README.md) and the full [`docs/reference/`](./docs/reference/).
- The `hoody` CLI, shipped in the same package — run with zero install via `npx hoody-sdk`, or install globally with `npm install -g hoody-sdk`.
- Node.js ≥ 22.19.0 and Bun support; browser IIFE global (`window.HoodySDK`) and ESM bundles.

> Future releases are documented here automatically, generated from the public API surface between published versions.

## [Full record of 1.0.1]

The Added, Changed, Fixed and Security entries of 1.0.1 in full. The 1.0.1 section at the top lists the main ones in one line each.

### Added

- **`hoody servers plans quote`, `servers subscriptions quote`, `buy` and `upgrade` take `--region`.** The region code comes from the list of subserver regions; leave it out to let Hoody choose. An upgrade never moves a subserver: another region is refused. `hoody completion` lists the flag.
- **Proxy logs can be narrowed to one Bot URL.** `hoody proxy logs list --bot-url <id>` and `hoody proxy logs stats --bot-url <id>` keep only the Bot calls of that Bot URL. The id is `bu_` followed by 24 lowercase hex digits; use it with `--service-name bots`. In the SDK the option is `botUrl` on `proxyLogs.list()`, `listAll()`, `listIterator()` and `getStats()`.
- **The `vm` namespace runs virtual machines inside a computer.** `box.vm` (54 methods; the package also exports it as the `vm` subpath) and `hoody vm` create VMs from a template, a blank disk, an ISO image or an imported snapshot; start, stop, pause and resume them; take, restore and export snapshots; upload ISO images in parts or download them from a URL; read and write a VM's serial console; and follow VM events live (`hoody vm events stream <name>`, or `hoody vm events stream` for every VM). Uploads read the bytes from `--input <file>` or stdin, an export part is saved with `--out-file`, and the commands that delete or force something confirm on a terminal. `hoody kits list` lists the `vm` kit.
- **`hoody use` shows and saves where commands act.** `hoody use` prints the realm, computer, project and account your commands act in, with the source of each. `hoody use <realm> [computer]` saves the realm and, if given, the computer (a saved computer that is not in the new realm is cleared). `hoody use -c <computer>` saves only the computer. `hoody use <realm> --color <name>` shows that realm in a colour on this device: `red`, `orange`, `green`, `teal`, `blue`, `purple` or `pink`, or `none` to clear it. A realm is its id or its name.
- **`hoody setup` finishes setting up your account.** It resumes email verification and claims your free server. It needs a terminal: in agent mode and in CI it exits 2.
- **`hoody welcome` opens a sign-in menu.** On a terminal it is a full-screen page in Hoody's ink and yellow with the logo: pick with the arrow keys, a digit or a click. Signed out, it offers to create an account, sign in with an email or username and password (a wrong password, two-step codes and an unverified email each get their own screen; Show reveals the password for at most 30 seconds), sign in with your browser (or, over SSH, with a code you enter on any device), paste a sign-in token, or resume an email verification you started. Signed in, it lists your computers. Only the button you press acts: it opens the Hoody agent or a terminal on the computer you chose, starts a stopped one, or claims your free computer. When the server offers them, "Continue with GitHub" and "Continue with Google" rows (and buttons on the sign-in and sign-up forms, on a screen tall enough for them) start the browser or code sign-in and tell you which button to press on the page that opens; a first sign-in with a provider creates the account while sign-ups are open. The list is read once from the server's public sign-in options with a 3 second limit, and any failure just leaves the rows out. Signing in saves the session and nothing else: it never creates or starts a computer and never sets, replaces or clears your saved default computer or project, in the page or in the plain menu (`hoody login` keeps its own behaviour). Leaving a sign-in while it waits to save writes nothing and leaves the signed-in state as it was, also when another `hoody` process holds the encrypted local store and a newer sign-in has started since. A code-sign-in link too tall for the screen scrolls with Up and Down. It draws truecolor or 256 colours, your own palette on a 16-colour terminal, and bold and underline only with `NO_COLOR`; leaving gives your screen back as it was. Clicks and the wheel work everywhere; the row under the pointer lights up on a local terminal (`HOODY_TUI_HOVER=1` forces that on, `0` off). On a terminal narrower than 40 columns or shorter than 18 rows, with `TERM=dumb`, or with `HOODY_NO_TUI=1`, it shows the numbered menu instead. It needs a terminal: in agent mode, in CI and without one it exits 2. `ssh hoody.com` runs it for you.
- **`hoody computers urls list [computer] [--pick]` shows the Hoody Kit URLs of a computer, whole.** The computer defaults to the one commands act in. In a pipe it prints `kit<TAB>url`, one line per kit. On a terminal, `--pick` lets you open, copy, scan or inspect one.
- **`hoody ai freeusage get` shows how much of each of your servers' free AI allowance is used,** per lane (chat, decisions, search; `-o wide` adds speech, transcription and when the reading was taken). **`hoody ip lookup <ip>` looks up the country and network of up to 25 public IPv4 addresses** (comma-separated) from data the platform already holds. `hoody completion` lists both.
- **`hoody browser markdown get` returns a tab's page as Markdown,** optionally after opening a `--url` in it, with only the main content by default. `hoody completion` lists it.
- **`hoody auth 2fa enrollment undo --link-token <token>` undoes a two-factor enrollment from the "this was not me" link in the alert email,** and `api.auth.twoFactor.undoEnrollment({ token })` does the same in the SDK. It needs no sign-in. It turns two-factor off, ends every session, revokes all tokens and clears the password; asks for confirmation on a terminal. `hoody completion` lists it.
- **`hoody vm display get <name>` shows the browser address of a VM's screen** and its display number (`url` and `display` in `-o json`). A VM without a display is refused with 409 `DisplayNotConfigured`. `hoody completion` lists it.
- **Shell completion follows today's API descriptions.** `hoody exec schedules reload` offers `--no-dry-run`; `hoody notes collaborators add`, `notes nodes markopened`, `notes nodes markseen`, `notes reactions add` and `notes records create` offer `--idempotency-key`; `hoody agent todos update` offers `--automation`, `--blocked-reason`, `--needs-human`, `--no-needs-human`, `--question-for-human`, `--reason`, `--suggested-agent` and `--wake-at`; `hoody agent version` no longer offers `--cwd`, `--config-dir` and `--tool-computer`, because the version describes the running agent and takes no scope.
- **Names in place of ids.** On a command with name lookup, you can type the name where it takes an id: `hoody computers get ember`, `--realm acme`. Lists, receipts and errors show names beside ids, and ids and URLs are never cut.
- **`--http` and `--http-curl` print the request a command would send.** `--http` prints the method, the whole URL, the headers and the body; `--http-curl` prints the same request as a runnable `curl` line. Nothing that writes is sent, no confirmation is asked, and the token is never printed (the header reads `Authorization: Bearer $HOODY_TOKEN`). A command that cannot show its requests refuses both flags with exit 2 before it does anything.
- **`--mask-urls` (or `HOODY_MASK_URLS=1`) shortens every 24-character hex id on screen to its first and last 4 characters.** Use it when you share your screen. It changes nothing in pipes or in `-o json|yaml`, and `open`, copy and QR actions always get the whole value.
- **The `stream` commands follow a log.** `hoody daemon programs logs stream <id>`, `hoody exec logs stream`, `hoody proxy logs stream` and `hoody agent logs stream` print new entries as they arrive. `-f` and `--follow` on `hoody daemon programs logs get`, `hoody exec logs get`, `hoody proxy logs list` and `hoody agent logs list` exit 2 and name the stream command.
- **`--watch [interval]` redraws a list in place** on every read list (default every 2s; for example `500ms`, `2s`, `1m`). It needs an interactive terminal.
- **`--until-done` waits for an asynchronous operation to finish,** with `--timeout <duration>` to stop waiting (default 10m; for example `30s`, `5m`). It exits 0 when the operation is done, 1 when it failed, 124 on a timeout, 4 when not authorized and 130 when interrupted.
- **The 18 destructive commands that never asked now confirm on a terminal:** `hoody browser shutdown`, `hoody browser cookies clear`, `hoody curl jobs cancel`, `hoody curl jobs delete`, `hoody exec cache clear`, `hoody files backends delete`, `hoody files mounts delete`, `hoody notifications restore`, `hoody db history clear`, `hoody notes notebooks delete`, `hoody notes nodes delete`, `hoody notes comments delete`, `hoody notes reactions remove`, `hoody notes records delete`, `hoody notes collaborators remove`, `hoody agent acp credentialimports apply`, `hoody agent acp filesyncs apply` and `hoody egress upstream disable`. Without a terminal they run as before.
- **Structured CLI inputs accept JSON inline, from a file with `@<path>`, or from stdin with `@-`.** `kv batch set --items` also accepts repeatable schema-typed KEY=VALUE entries. Existing inline JSON invocations remain supported.
- **`ApiError` says whether the request reached the server.** `outcome` is `'answered'`, `'not_sent'` or `'unknown'`, `attempts` is how many times the call sent the request, and `retryAfterMs` is the answer's Retry-After in milliseconds. `outcome` covers the whole call: it is `'not_sent'` only when no attempt can have reached the server. The SDK exports the `ApiErrorOutcome` type, from the browser entry too.
- **Three client options for keyed retries and redaction.** `keyedInProgressWait: false`, on the client or on one call, makes a keyed request's 409 `IDEMPOTENCY_REQUEST_IN_PROGRESS` the answer instead of asking again; the default stays `true`. `redactBodyKeys`, on the client or on one call, names body and query fields that an `ApiError` shows as `[REDACTED]`, in its URL too. `credentialHeaders` names headers that carry a credential: an `ApiError` redacts them, and a request that sends one is never followed through a redirect to another origin. Every generated method also takes `responseIsFinal: true`, which makes that call final.
- **`hoody hub` is a new command: it opens the Hub in the terminal.** It uses the sign-in saved by `hoody login` (or an access key), starts in the realm you pass with `--realm` (an id, a name, or `global`), else in the realm from `HOODY_REALM`, `HOODY_REALM_ID`, `hoody use` or your last run, in that order, and draws the Hub's pages full screen. `hoody hub --demo` opens a sample account that lives in memory and sends nothing. The Hub is part of the standalone `hoody` binary for Linux on x86-64 and arm64 (the musl build has none; arm64 has not yet been run on arm64 hardware), and of the npm package on the same platforms with Node.js 26 or later. From npm on those platforms with an older Node.js or with Bun, the command says it needs Node.js 26 or later, or the standalone binary, and shows the installer. On other platforms and without an interactive terminal, it says why (and, where it helps, gives the web Hub's address), exit 2. The bare `hoody` page lists `hub` under Platform & account. Bare `hoody agent` opens the Hub too (see Breaking). After a sign-in, the success line suggests `try: hoody hub`, and `hoody agent login` on an agent with no sign-in over HTTP suggests signing it in from the Hub (`hoody hub`).
- **A request middleware can refuse a request, and the refusal is final.** A middleware that throws in `onRequest` now ends the call with an `ApiError` of status 0, code `REQUEST_REFUSED`, and the thrown value as `cause`. Its `outcome` is `'not_sent'` unless an earlier attempt of the same call may have reached the server. The request is not sent again, the middleware is not asked a second time, and `onError` cannot replay it. An `ApiError` that the middleware throws is the answer as it is, and is final too. Use this to authorize each send, for example a middleware that reads a token on every request and refuses when it has none. The README shows the recipe.
- **`onStepUp` answers a two-factor challenge once.** Set `onStepUp: async (error) => otp` on the client. When a call fails with 400 `OTP_REQUIRED` and its operation takes an `otp_code` body field, the hook gets the error and returns the code, or `null` to give up. The client sends the request once more with the code in `otp_code`. It never asks twice for one call, never replaces a code the caller already set, and does not run for a call that cannot be sent again (a stream body). The answer to the resend is the call's answer: no token refresh, retry or `onError` replay sends the code again. If the hook throws, the original error is thrown and the console gets a fixed line with the error's name, never its message.
- **`fetchCredentials` and `fetchCache` pass `credentials` and `cache` to `fetch`.** `fetchCredentials` is `'omit'`, `'same-origin'` or `'include'`, or an object to choose per namespace: one entry per kit namespace (`agent`, `terminal`, ...) or `api`, and `'*'` for every namespace not listed, for example `{ agent: 'include', '*': 'omit' }`. A request that sends `'include'` is treated as credentialed: it is never followed through a redirect to another origin. `fetchCache` is a `fetch` cache mode (`'default'`, `'no-store'`, `'reload'`, `'no-cache'` or `'force-cache'`). The names differ from `HoodyClient`'s `credentials` (the login credentials) and `cache` (the SDK's own response cache). Neither is set by default. `fetchCache` is opt-in on purpose: a browser adds `Cache-Control` and `Pragma` request headers for `no-store`, `reload` and `no-cache`, which turns a cross-origin write into a preflighted request. Node's `fetch` acts on `fetchCache` too, and `fetchCredentials: 'include'` makes the Node client follow redirects by hand, as it does for any credentialed request.
- **`maxResponseBytes` caps a buffered body, and `maxStreamBytes` caps a whole stream.** `maxResponseBytes` is a client option and a per-call option. A successful response over the limit ends the call with an `ApiError` of code `RESPONSE_TOO_LARGE` (status 0, never retried): a `Content-Length` over the limit is refused before any byte is read, and a body without one stops being read at the limit. An error answer (not 2xx) keeps its status, so the 401 refresh and the 429 retry still work; its body is cut at the limit, and a JSON body cut part-way does not parse, so the error then has no `code` and no body message, only the status (and `Retry-After`, if the server sent it). `maxStreamBytes` is a client option and a `streamEvents` option. Once the bytes read pass the budget the stream ends with code `STREAM_TOO_LARGE`; frames in the chunk that crosses the budget are not delivered, frames in earlier chunks are. Neither is set by default. A value that is set but is not a whole number above 0 (`0`, a negative, a fraction, `NaN`, `Infinity`, a string, `null`) throws a `ValidationError` from the client constructor or from the call, so a typo cannot switch a cap off.
- **A WebSocket refuses to connect when a credential you passed for it cannot be sent.** A browser WebSocket cannot send headers. When a call passes a `kitAuth` or a credential header (`Authorization`, `Cookie`, `Proxy-Authorization`, `X-Hoody-Token`, `X-Hoody-Computer-Claim`) for that socket, `connect()` now rejects with a `WebSocketCredentialNotSentError` (code `WS_CREDENTIAL_NOT_SENT`, `headers` lists the names) and no socket opens. The SDK exports the error type and `isWebSocketCredentialNotSentError`. A `kitAuth` set on the client keeps the old behaviour: one console warning, and the socket opens. Only the headers that credential would use are checked, whatever their name (a `kitAuth` with `header: 'X-Access-Pass'` is refused for that header); a per-call token that rides a query parameter connects. A static `webSocketImpl`, which takes no headers, refuses the same credentials on every runtime.
- **`api.servers.plans.listPublic({ locale })` lists the server plans, and works without signing in.** It calls `GET /api/v1/meta/subserver-plans`.
- **`hoody use -c`, `hoody agent` and `hoody config set computer` take a computer name as well as an id.** A name is looked up once (an exact match, the same case) and the id is what gets sent or saved. The lookup runs in the realm the id will be used with: the realm named in `hoody use <realm> <name>`, or the realm the profile that `config set computer` writes acts in (its own, else the root's). No match, several matches, or a listing that did not read every computer (a later page failed, or there are more than one lookup reads) is refused, with the command to list them. An id is passed through untouched, with no lookup, and a lookup that takes longer than 10 seconds is cancelled.
- **Four new `hoody agent` commands: spend caps, an agent's reasoning effort and a session's slash commands.** `hoody agent settings spendcaps get` prints the spend caps chats are held to. `hoody agent settings spendcaps set --max-session-usd <n> --max-turn-usd <n> --action stop|warn --expected-revision <rev>` replaces all three caps if their revision still matches; pass `--expected-revision ""` when no caps were set yet. `hoody agent definitions effort set --name <agent> --effort <level>` sets an agent's reasoning effort (`adaptive`, `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh` or `max`; `--effort ""` removes it). `hoody agent sessions slashcommands list --id <session>` lists the slash commands a session's chat offers.

- **`hoody unmount --force`** (SDK: `force: true` on `unmount()`, `unmountAll()`, `unmountByComputer()` and a handle's `unmount()`) unmounts without waiting for files still uploading. The `PendingUploadsError` and `UnmountError` classes and the `UnmountOptions` and `EarlierUnsent` types are exported from the package root and `'hoody-sdk/mount'`.
- **A mount reports files an earlier mount of the same URL left unsent** (`handle.earlierUnsent`; `hoody mount` prints a line). Those of a mount at the same path are uploaded as it starts when no `--cache-dir` is given; with one given, it lists them with the cache dir they are in (the default, or the `--cache-dir` a mount must give to upload them). For another path it names the path where mounting the URL again uploads them.
- **`hoody unmount --all` / `--computer` with `-o json` say whether each failed path is still mounted.** Each object in `failed` carries `"mounted": true|false`, the same as `UnmountError.failures[].mounted` in the SDK.
- **`curl.run()` JSON responses have `raw_headers`**, every header line in order (`[{ name, value }]`), typed in the SDK.
- **`exec.modules.listBundled()` returns `sdkUpdate`** on the `hoody-sdk` row (see Changed).
- **`files.archives.extract()` results and errors carry `replaced` and `replaced_count`** (see Changed).
- **Speech in and out of the agent: `agent.audio`.** `listModels()` and `listVoices()` show the speech models and the voices each one offers (CLI `hoody agent audio models list`, `hoody agent audio voices list`). `createSpeech()` turns text into audio (CLI `hoody agent audio speech create`, which writes the audio to a file); `createSpeech(data, { stream: true })` is the SDK-only form that delivers the audio in chunks as they are produced. `createTranscription()` turns a recording into text and `createTranscription(data, { stream: true })` streams the text as it is recognised; both take the recording as a multipart upload and are SDK and API only, with no CLI command in this release.
- **Spoken messages to a Bot.** `agent.bots.messages.sendAudio()` posts a wav or mp3 recording to a Bot as a message (SDK and API only: the recording is a multipart upload, and there is no CLI command in this release). An optional `Idempotency-Key` makes a retry with the same key and recording return the same message instead of posting it twice, and a call with a key is retried on a retryable answer (503 `speech_busy`) with the same recording and key.
- **Talk to a Bot live (SDK only).** `agent.bots.connectRealtime()` opens a live two-way conversation with a Bot over the OpenAI Realtime protocol (WebSocket); `agent.bots.connectVoice()` streams raw voice audio to and from a Bot (WebSocket, `hoody.voice.v1`). When another client takes the conversation over, the agent closes the voice, realtime or Twilio connection with reason "taken over": the client does not reconnect, even with `reconnectOnNormalClose`, and `onTakenOver(reason)` runs after `onDisconnect`.
- **The live Bot clients carry audio.** The client from `agent.bots.connectVoice()` offers `hoody.voice.v1`, sends the user's audio with `sendInputAudio()` (in the `in_format` given to `connectVoice()`: 16-bit PCM by default, or `pcmu` or `pcma`) and hands each audio frame from the Bot, 16-bit PCM, to `onOutputAudio()`. The send methods of the `connectRealtime()` and `connectTwilio()` clients take the frame's fields, so `input_audio_bufferAppend({ audio })` and Twilio's `media()` carry the audio.
- **Bot speech settings and a realtime guardrail.** A Bot has a `speech` settings object (language, the speech models it uses, per-day limits), set on `agent.bots.create()` and `update()` (SDK and API only; `hoody agent bots create` and `update` do not take it) and returned on every Bot. Bot guardrails gain `realtime_items` (max and used), shown by `get()`, `list()` and `setGuardrails()`. Each speech model from `agent.audio.listModels()` reports its `max_input_chars`.
- **Bots can take a phone call through Twilio.** `agent.bots.connectTwilio()` connects a Bot to a Twilio Media Streams call so the Bot hears the caller and answers by voice. Who may connect a call is set by the agent's proxy permission rules. Known limitation: this release was tested against a Twilio Media Streams simulator only; a live Twilio account is not supported in this release.
- **Agent sessions can wait for an outcome in another session's work** with `agent.sessions.waiters.create()`, `list()`, `get()` and `cancel()`. A waiter, owned by the session in the path, watches another live session of the same realm (`target`) until one of 1 to 4 named outcomes shows or its deadline passes (`max_s`, 10 to 86400 seconds, default 600). An outcome is exact (`match`: an event's kind, tool, exit status, error flag, text or turn outcome) or judged (`when`: a sentence a model checks against the target's tool calls, tool results and questions). Judged checks are model requests, capped by `max_checks` (1 to 60, default 20). The end arrives on the owner session's stream as `event.waiter_done`; `get()` reads a waiter for 24 hours after it ended. `create()` takes an optional `Idempotency-Key`; 409 when the target cannot be watched, 429 `waiters_exhausted` when too many are waiting.
- **Bots can pick the model each new delegate starts with** through `routing` on `agent.bots.create()`, `update()`, `get()`, `list()` and `setGuardrails()` (SDK and API only; `hoody agent bots create` and `update` do not take it). `light` and `heavy` each name a model and a thinking setting. `mode` `off` (the default) changes nothing; `suggest` shows the pick and changes nothing; `auto` starts each new delegate on the picked worker. With routing on, the task text, including the user messages it attaches, is sent to a separate routing check model. A model no session could start with is refused with 422 `model_unavailable`. `agent.bots.listDelegates()` shows what routing decided for each delegate in `route`. Send `null` to remove routing.
- **`cron.events.stream()`, `cron.events.connect()` and `cron.runs.list()`**, with `hoody cron events stream` and `hoody cron runs list` (see Changed).
- **`cron.crontabs.set()` takes `ifMatch`, and crontab reads return `etag`** (see Changed).
- **Bot chat channels: `agent.bots.channels`** (see Changed): `create()`, `list()`, `get()`, `getSetup()` (the steps and credentials a chat app needs, with the app manifest for Slack), `update()` (`label`, `enabled`, `home_chat`, `settings`), `setCredentials()`, `delete()`, `createPairing()`, `cancelPairing()`, `addPrincipal()`, `updatePrincipal()`, `removePrincipal()`, `updateChat()` and `deleteChat()`. CLI: `hoody agent bots channels list`, `create`, `setup get`, `get`, `update`, `delete`, `channels credentials set`, `channels pairing create` / `cancel`, `channels principals add` / `update` / `remove` and `channels chats update` / `delete`.
- **`agent.bots.channels.getSettings()` and `updateSettings()`** (CLI: `hoody agent bots channels settings get` / `update`) read and set a Bot's channel-wide settings (see Changed).
- **`agent.bots.resumeAll()`** (CLI: `hoody agent bots resume`) brings back suspended Bots (see Changed).
- **`terminal.sessions.wait()` takes `fresh` and `after_seq`** (`hoody terminal sessions wait --fresh`, `--after-seq`; see Changed).
- **`api.events.clear()` takes `all`** (`hoody events clear --all`; see Changed).
- **`files.touch(path, { touch, type })` takes `type`** (CLI: `hoody files touch --type`), a remote backend type such as `sftp`, `webdav`, `ftp` or `s3`. The files kit already served such a touch as an upload of the request body to that remote path (it needs remote access and uploads enabled); the SDK and CLI can now send it.
- **`SourceType` includes `oci-docker-hub-search`** (see Changed).
- **Web Push on `notifications.push`** (see Changed): `getPublicKey()` (CLI: `hoody notifications push publickey get`) returns the key a page passes to `pushManager.subscribe()`; `subscriptions.list()` (`hoody notifications push subscriptions list`) lists the stored subscriptions with their delivery health, never their browser keys or full endpoint URLs; `subscriptions.delete()` (`hoody notifications push subscriptions delete`) removes one; `test()` (`hoody notifications push test`) sends a test alert to one subscription or to all, also during a cooldown, and reports after at most 20 seconds: `accepted` (the push service took it, which does not mean the device showed it), `failed`, `removed` (reported gone and deleted) and `pending` (still retrying; it finishes in the background). `subscriptions.create()` is SDK and API only: it takes the fields of a browser's `PushSubscription` and needs the page's origin (`origin`), https with a domain name.
- **`hoody terminal commands run` takes `--command-env` and `--command-cwd`.** They set the command's own environment (a JSON object, given inline, as `@<file>` or as `@-`) and working directory, on an existing session too; `--env` reaches only a shell the call creates or resets. `hoody completion` lists both flags.
- **`hoody browser viewport set --viewport null`** sends `viewport: null` (a responsive viewport). The width and height flags could not send it, so the command had no way to clear a fixed viewport.
- **`hoody exec templates generate -o raw` prints the generated code.** Only the code is written, with a final newline, so `hoody exec templates generate <name> -o raw > middleware.js` saves the file.
- **`hoody signup` handles the browser check.** Hoody may ask a person to finish a short check in a browser before it creates an account. In a terminal, `hoody signup` and `hoody welcome` now show the link and the code the page shows, wait until the check passes and go on to email verification; before, the command printed `Signup failed` and asked for the email and password again. Without a terminal, `hoody signup` creates nothing, prints the link, the code and the command that carries on, and exits 2 (`-o json`: `challenge_required`, `verification_url`, `match_code`, `challenge`, `next`, `next_argv`). Run that command once the check is done, with the same password on stdin: it keeps the email, `--region`, `--invite-code`, `--profile`, `--config`, `--realm`, `--base-url` and the output format, and adds `--challenge <code>`. A `--base-url` with a query, a fragment or a user name, and a `--proxy`, are left out, with a line asking you to add them again. `--wait` keeps the command waiting instead. A check that does not pass is replaced by a new one. A check that runs out exits 1. When no answer ends the check (the wait runs out, or a `--challenge` run gets no answer), the command also exits 1 and says the account may exist if the check was finished (`-o json`: `outcome_unknown`). Exit 0 means Hoody accepted the signup, which it also answers for an address that already has an account.
- **`hoody agent sessions images get` and `agent.sessions.getImage()` read an image attached to a session message.** The transcript of a session shows an image block for each image a person attached, with its `image_id` and media type but not the bytes. Pass the session id and that `image_id` to get the image itself; `--out-file <file>` saves it byte for byte (`-` writes it to stdout).

### Changed

- The CLI follows the 1.0.1 API descriptions: `hoody agent fusions set` takes `--spec-name`, `--spec-method`, `--spec-members` and `--spec-params-*` instead of `--spec`; `hoody agent hooks upsert`, `delete` and `rules set` require `--scope`; the agent skills, workflows, jev, waiters and GitHub sign-in commands no longer take `--tool-computer`.
- **On a terminal, `hoody` commands print a tab line and their data in a fixed layout.** The tab line goes to stderr and names where the command acted; the data follows as a list, an item, a receipt, a value, a stream, bytes or an error. Pipes, CI, agent mode and `-o json|yaml` do not get this layout. To get the old layout on a terminal, type `-o table`, `-o wide` or `-o json`, or set `HOODY_AGENT_MODE=1`.
- **A command's `--help` is one line per flag, with examples, on a terminal and in pipes.** Required flags come first, then the common ones (the ones the first example uses lead), then a dimmed "More options" block with the rarely needed ones (request headers such as `--agent-realm`, aliases, retry keys; a flag the examples use is never there). A command with more than 20 optional flags keeps a line for the ones its examples use and lists the others by name under "More options" (`hoody files backends s3 create --help` is 45 lines, and 83 with `HOODY_HELP_SPLIT=0`). Each flag shows the first sentence of its description, cut to the width; a required flag's line wraps instead, so its values and rules stay whole, and `--help --full` prints every description whole. Flattened permission switches print as one row per object (`--permissions-computers-{create,read,update,delete}`). Every command and group page ends with examples. The examples the help draws put each command on its own line and its description on the line under it, so a copied line is only the command; the examples written by hand into twelve commands' help (`run`, `shell`, `login`, `signup`, `share`, `hub`, `boturls`, `agent`, `agent login`, `auth accesstoken get`, `computers urls list`, `daemon programs terminal connect`) still print some descriptions beside their command. A long example continues on the next line after ` \`, and a JSON argument wider than the terminal is laid out over lines inside its quotes. A group's commands are one line each, and notes such as the `open` views wrap at the terminal width. Agent mode and `HOODY_HELP_SPLIT=0` keep the full stock help.
- **On a terminal, a missing or mistyped input prints a short block instead of the whole help:** what is missing or unknown (with the near match, `did you mean --name?`), a command that fixes it (continued after ` \` when it is wider than the terminal), and the `--help` to read. An unknown flag is named first, so a typo is no longer hidden by a required-option error. The exit code and the Commander error code are unchanged. In pipes, CI, agent mode and with `-o json` the error is still Commander's message followed by the command's help in its old layout.
- **On a terminal, a single item shows sizes, durations and amounts in their unit** (`11.1 GiB`, `2h 50m`, `150.83`) where its field name says the unit (`used_bytes`, `uptime_s`, `total_cents`), and a time ahead of now reads `in 95d` instead of `just now` (less than a minute ahead, a clock difference, still reads `just now`). `hoody kits list` prints one line per kit; the URL samples are in `-o wide` and `-o json`.
- **The AI fix for a mistyped command (`HOODY_CLI_AI_FIX=suggest|auto`) runs only on an interactive terminal and sends no value you typed.** It handles an unknown command word or an unknown option, nothing else: a missing argument, a bare command group (`hoody computers` shows its help) and a help request are reported as before. In a pipe, in CI, in agent mode, with `--non-interactive` and with `-o json|yaml|ndjson|raw` the command fails with its own error and exit code and no provider is asked. The request carries the command words, the option names, `<v>` in place of every value and operand, `<payload>` in place of what follows `--`, the error and the commands' help; a word the CLI does not know is sent only when it is close to one of its commands. In `auto` mode the CLI runs your own command line again with only the mistyped command word or option name replaced, and only when the replacement is a typo correction of what you typed and the option is one the command has; your options, values and arguments are kept as typed. Any other suggestion is printed and nothing runs. `HOODY_CLI_AI_MAX_TOKENS` above 16000 is lowered to 16000.
- **Bare `hoody` prints one page and exits 0 in every mode.** The page shows where commands act, then every command (the same index as `hoody --help`, with `setup`, `welcome` and `use` in the Session section). It no longer opens a menu or a prompt, so `hoody | grep shell` works; the account steps it used to offer are in `hoody setup`.
- **A token-authenticated SSH session on a terminal is now interactive.** A session that signs in with a token (`HOODY_GATEWAY` and `HOODY_TOKEN` set) used to count as scripted even with a terminal on all three streams, so it got no prompts, no confirmations and no pickers. It now gets prompts and confirmations like any other terminal session; a command that needs an id you left out exits 2 and names the flag or argument instead of opening a picker, as everywhere over the gateway. Runs with no terminal, in CI, in agent mode or with `--non-interactive` are as before.
- **Messages that said to run `hoody` now say `hoody setup`.** The success message of `hoody users invites redeem` points to `hoody setup` instead of `hoody`, and so do two messages in the sign-up flow.
- **A name works where an id did.** On a command with name lookup, a value that is not shaped like an id is looked up by name instead of being sent as typed. An unknown or ambiguous name exits 1 with the lookup's message (the API's own error came before); a known name now succeeds. A value shaped like an id is sent as given.
- **`--realm <name>` accepts a realm name from your realm aliases and sends the realm id.** A write checks the name against the Hub first and stops if it cannot. A name in `HOODY_REALM` or in a saved realm is not resolved and behaves as before. `hoody use` saves where commands act; `hoody realms list` shows the names; shell completion offers them.
- **On a terminal, the 18 destructive commands that never asked now confirm** (listed under Added). Scripts, pipes, CI and agent mode are unaffected.
- **On a terminal, 13 more delete commands ask before they delete:** `hoody agent sessions delete`, `hoody agent sessions approval rules delete`, `hoody agent memory items delete`, `hoody agent definitions delete`, `hoody agent fusions delete`, `hoody agent hooks delete`, `hoody agent jobs delete`, `hoody agent loops delete`, `hoody agent mcp delete`, `hoody agent providers apikey delete`, `hoody agent skills delete`, `hoody agent workflows delete` and `hoody kv batch delete`. `-y` skips the question. Scripts, pipes, CI and agent mode run them as before.
- **`hoody open <service>` over SSH with no browser available prints the URL.** It used to start an opener on the remote machine. With none of `BROWSER`, `DISPLAY` or `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` set it prints the URL instead; with any of the three set it opens the browser as before, and in a terminal it now says `Opened <url> in your browser.`
- **`hoody --help` opens with the same context line as bare `hoody` and honours `HOODY_PROFILE`.** Its Session section lists `setup`, `welcome` and `use`. Shell completion offers all three and completes realm names after `--realm` and `hoody use`.
- **On a terminal, confirmations ask on stderr and name the target.** Outside a terminal, the prompt is unchanged.
- **Commands that generate a value now print it in a pipe.** `hoody auth tokens create` and `hoody auth tokens copy` print the new token, `hoody proxy aliases create` and `hoody proxy aliases update` print the alias URL, `hoody exec templates generate` prints the generated code, `hoody servers commands run` prints the command output, and `hoody auth 2fa setup confirm` and `hoody auth 2fa disable` print the rotated session tokens when the response carries them. Each prints a short block of labelled lines that starts with the value; use `-o json` to read one field in a script. They printed only `create completed successfully.` (or a similar line) and the value was gone.
- **Success lines and three tables say what the answer holds.** A success line in a pipe reads as plain words where it named the operation. `hoody terminal sessions create` says when the session already existed, and `hoody curl jobs cancel` says the cancel was requested (a job that had already finished is left as it was). `hoody terminal sessions list` shows ACTIVE where STATUS was always `-`, `hoody daemon programs list` shows NAME, ENABLED, USER and COMMAND (`-o wide` adds STATUS, PID and UPTIME, filled with `--include-status true`), and `hoody kv list` shows each key's size and content type, where it showed a VALUE column the kit never sends.
- **`agent.sessions.trim()` returns the trim's outcome, typed as a union.** `AgentSessionsTrimResponse` follows the agent's current trim answer: it is a union of a finished trim (`data.outcome: 'done'`) and a pending one (`data.status: 'pending'` with `data.request_id`), with no `data.deferred` or `data.note`. Read `data.status` where you read `data.deferred`, and narrow the union where an interface extended the old type.
- **WebSocket frame types that stand for several frames require only what every one of them carries.** A generated interface that merges frames of one type (in `AgentSessionsStreamFrames` on the agent session stream, or on the terminal socket) required a field when any one frame required it and typed a field from one frame only. A field is now required only when every merged frame requires it, and a field the frames type differently takes the union of their types (`outcome` on the session stream includes the trim outcomes). Check for `undefined` where you read a field that is now optional.
- **`agent.sessions.turns.start(id, data, { stream: true })` ends after its own turn.** It replaces `agent.sessions.startTurnAndStream()`. The stream used to stay open after the turn, as the agent keeps sending the session's later events, so a `for await` loop never ended. It now ends after the `agent_done` of the turn it started (the `X-Hoody-Turn-Id` response header, or the `replay_boundary` frame's `turn_id`), never another client's, after that turn's `turn_discarded` if the agent dropped it unrun, or when the session ends (`quit`, `session_closed`, `end`), and closes the connection. Pass `followSession: true` to keep reading the session as before. `agent.sessions.stream()` is unchanged. A raw `http.streamEvents()` call takes the same rule as `turnEnd` (type `IStreamTurnEnd`).
- **`streamAgentPrompt` waits for its own turn and cancels only that turn.** Another turn's `agent_done` on the same session no longer resolves `done`, and an error that turn raised no longer fails it; its own `turn_discarded` and `session_closed` end it like `quit`. Once the turn id is known, `cancel()` sends it (`{ turn_id }`), so it no longer stops a later turn.
- **`agent.sessions.turns.create()` takes the gate policy.** Pass `gatePolicy: 'auto_approve'` to have the turn's confirm gates auto-answered, as `agent.sessions.turns.run()` does.
- **The display viewer's embed options match the page.** `client.embeds.display.client()` now accepts the options the viewer reads and had no entry for, in `query`: `quality` (0 to 100), `quality_mode` (`auto`, `best`, `reduced`), `scroll`, `scroll_speed`, `touchpad_scroll_scale`, `colorScheme`, `theme`, `font`, `clipboard_poll` (`false` only), `reconnect_count`, `soft_giveup_attempts`, `soft_giveup_ms`, `slow_retry_delay`, `slow_retry_max_ms`, `record`, `record_format`, `record_codec` and `record_fps`. `override_height`, `web_notifications` and `notification_connection_type` did nothing in the viewer and are no longer accepted: a call that passes one throws `PARAM_EXCLUDED` (`override_height`) or `PARAM_DEAD` (the other two): drop it. The descriptions now state the viewer's real defaults (`keyboard` is on in mobile browsers, `swap_keys` on macOS, `offscreen` in Chrome and Chromium over https and Firefox 108 or later, where the viewer now draws in a Web Worker unless `offscreen` is `false`, `maximize_new_windows` on unless `decorations` is off).
- **Over the Hoody SSH gateway (`ssh hoody.com`), `hoody` runs no programs on the gateway and connects only to Hoody services.** `hoody pipe send` refuses `--from-cmd`, `--from-tcp` and `--from-unix`, `hoody pipe receive` refuses `--to-cmd`, `--to-tcp` and `--to-unix`, and `hoody pipe forward` refuses `--listen` and `--connect`; `hoody tunnel expose`, `hoody tunnel pull` and `hoody egress local start` are refused too. Each exits 1 before anything is sent and says to run it on your own machine instead; `pipe` with a file, `-` or `--text` still works. `hoody share` (with or without `--rclone`), `hoody mount`, `hoody mount --prune` and `hoody unmount` are refused the same way with exit 2, and `hoody mount --list` shows the saved mounts, each marked stale. Requests and sockets go only to HTTPS and WSS on port 443 under hoody.com or hoody.bot: any other `--base-url`, `HOODY_API_URL`, `HOODY_PIPE_URL`, `HOODY_CHAT_URL`, `HOODY_CLI_AI_URL`, Bot URL or `--zone`, a redirect to anywhere else, and any proxy (`--proxy`, `HTTP_PROXY`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `ALL_PROXY`) are refused, before a credential is read where the destination is known up front. `hoody update` prints that the gateway CLI is operator-managed and exits 0, and `--help` starts no update check. Commands that open a browser print the URL instead, and after a login the CLI prints the install command for your own machine instead of offering to install itself. A command that needs an id you left out, which opens a picker on your own machine, exits 2 instead and says which flag or argument takes the id and which command lists them. On your own machine nothing changes.
- **A PUT or DELETE that the server answered with success is not sent again when reading its answer fails.** The SDK and `hoody` used to send the change again, and a DELETE that had worked then reported 404. The call now ends with that failure, and so does a call whose response middleware throws after a success answer. An answer with an error status is retried as before. GET, HEAD and OPTIONS still retry, and so does a request that carries the idempotency key its server honours: the server answers the repeat from its record of the first. An `onError` hook that returns `true` no longer replays an unkeyed change either. `box.agent.jobs.delete` (`hoody agent jobs delete`) and `box.vm.isos.downloads.delete` (`hoody vm isos downloads delete`) are never sent twice.
- **A request middleware that throws now ends the call, where before the call could run it again.** A throwing `onRequest` (and the client's built-in realm and credential checks, which throw `ValidationError`) used to give a status-0 error that the default retry policy could retry and that `onError` could replay. The error now has code `REQUEST_REFUSED` and is final, on `stream()`, `streamEvents()` and the upgrade of a WebSocket too. Upgrade preparation (the WebSocket `connect()`) used to throw the middleware's own error; it now throws an `ApiError` with code `REQUEST_REFUSED` and the original on `cause`, so an `instanceof ValidationError` check on a refused `connect()` must read `cause` instead. A caller abort keeps its own error everywhere. A middleware that threw on a transient failure (a token fetch that failed) and relied on the retry now fails on the first attempt; retry inside the middleware if you need it.
- **A localhost or IP address base URL has one computers domain, in the SDK and the CLI.** The SDK client and `deriveComputersDomain` kept the host (`localhost`), while the CLI client and the `kitAuth` middleware answered the production computers domain. With a local API, kit URLs were built under `localhost`, the middleware looked for hosts under the production domain, and the `kitAuth` credential was not sent. All of them now read one function. A `*.localhost` API host follows the same rules as a production one: with `http://api.localhost` the SDK client and an embed now build kit hosts under `computers.localhost`, where they used `api.localhost`. A kit host under `localhost` gets the `kitAuth` credential; the API itself and other ports on `localhost` do not. An IP address base URL has no kit host, so `getKitUrl`, a kit method and the CLI's kit commands now throw "The API base URL is an IP address, so no computer kit host can be built from it" (`ValidationError` for a kit method); a client that only calls the account API on an IP address keeps working. `client.embeds` still refuses it with `TARGET_INVALID`.
- **20 account methods no longer take a `_realm` option, and always go to the base host.** These routes answer only there, so a realm could only make them fail with 403. The methods are `auth.sessions.list`, `revoke` and `revokeOthers`; `auth.email.startChange` and `cancelChange`; `users.retrySetup`; `inbox.markRead` and `markAllRead`; `servers.getStats`; `wallet.getGithubBonusStatus` and `claimGithubBonus`; `support.getLimits`; and `support.tickets.list`, `create`, `get`, `getByKey`, `getMessageByKey`, `reply`, `listChanges` and `close`. TypeScript code that passes `_realm` to one of them must drop it.
- **`undici` now needs 8.12.2 or later** (was 8.9.0). An install that kept an older cached `undici` now gets a current release. The Node floor for `undici` is unchanged (22.19).
- **The public OpenAPI spec states the credential of every account operation.** A public route says `security: []`, and every other account route names the bearer token.
- **Aborting `streamAgentPrompt`'s `signal` cancels the turn on the agent.** The abort used to drop the connection only: `done` said `cancelled` while the turn ran to its end. It now sends the same turn-scoped cancel as `cancel()` (`{ turn_id }`), and `done` resolves `cancelled` once the agent has answered it. An abort that comes before the agent has named the turn waits up to 10 seconds for the turn id and never sends a cancel without one, so it cannot stop another client's turn. If no id comes in that time, nothing is sent and the call or `done` rejects, saying the turn may still be running. A signal that is already aborted sends nothing and rejects with an `AbortError`. A cancel that fails, from the signal or from `cancel()`, now rejects `done` instead of reporting `cancelled`. `hoody agent prompt` is unchanged.
- **A JWT under a custom header is sent as `Bearer <jwt>`.** `kitAuth: { type: 'jwt', token, header: 'X-…' }` sent the bare token. The proxy's JWT rule reads a Bearer value from every header and refused it. The SDK's HTTP requests, the CLI's (`--kit-auth jwt` with `--kit-token-header`), kit WebSockets, the terminal client and `mount()` now send `Bearer <jwt>` on that header. A token that already starts with `Bearer ` is sent as given, and a `token` credential is still sent bare under a custom header.
- **An SDK app that runs a foreground `mount()` keeps control of its own shutdown.** On SIGINT, SIGTERM or SIGHUP the mount still unmounts, but the library no longer calls `process.exit`. If the app handles the signal itself, the mount only stops, and if that stop fails, `wait()` rejects with the reason. If nothing else handles the signal, the library raises it again once every mount has stopped, so the process ends as it would have with no mount running. `hoody mount` prints and exits as before, except after a SIGHUP: once the mount has stopped it exits 129, where it exited 0.
- **`TerminalClient` bounds its queue of unread stream output at 8 MiB.** Output that nothing reads from the stream (`data`, `pipe()`, `read()`) used to be kept for the whole session, so a client that listens only to `output` grew without limit. The client's queue of unread output now holds up to `maxBufferedOutputBytes` (a whole number of bytes, default 8 MiB; `Infinity` keeps everything). Once a reader has asked for output, up to 64 KiB more (the stream's `readableHighWaterMark`) sits in the stream's own buffer and is kept as well; after `setEncoding()` that allowance is counted at the most bytes a character can take, so it holds fewer bytes, never more. Past the limit the oldest queued bytes are dropped, even when an `output` listener throws, and an `output-dropped` event gives the number of bytes; a reader then gets that buffered prefix, the gap, and the newest output, possibly resuming inside an escape sequence. The `output` event still gets every chunk, a `read()` inside an `output` handler still gets the current chunk whole (it takes everything queued into the stream's buffer, as before), and the client never pauses the terminal on its own.
- **A flag with a closed list of values now refuses any other value before the request is sent.** `--status nope` said nothing and the server answered with its own error. It now exits 1 with `--status must be one of ...`. A repeatable or comma-separated flag is checked per value, and a flag whose values are an array of a closed list (`--modifiers`) is checked the same way. A value that differs from a listed one only in letter case is still sent as you typed it, because some lists are mixed-case and the server decides. A flag whose list is only examples (the description says the field also takes other values) sends what you type.
- **The update banner names the migration guide only for the update that needs it.** An update that crosses into 1.0.1 from an earlier version (1.0.0-beta.17 to 1.0.1, for example) points at `docs/MIGRATION-2026-10-NAMES.md`. A major update from 1.0.1 or later (1.0.1 to 2.0.0, or 2.x to 3.x) says to read the changelog and names no guide. An update that crosses both (1.0.0-beta.17 to 2.0.0) names the guide and the changelog.
- **The embed catalogue refuses the older `container_id` spelling on the display view.** The page still reads `container_id` from older shared links when `computer_id` is absent, so it retargeted the connection like `computer_id` does. Both are now refused in embed URLs.
- **CLI commands and flags have their stable names, and the old spellings stop with exit 2.** Several hundred commands, group aliases and flags were renamed or removed before the stable release (for example `hoody users freetier status`, formerly `users free tier status`; `hoody browser screenshots save`, formerly `screenshot browser`; and `hoody watch get <id>`, where the id was the `--id` flag). An old spelling exits 2 and names the new one. A renamed command prints what it printed before, with two exceptions: a confirmation prompt names the command as you now type it, and `hoody daemon programs terminal connect` (was `attach`) says "Connect needs a terminal" where it said "attach needs a terminal". Where a message names another command in its text, it names the new one (`hoody agent headless` lists `start --stream`, formerly `stream`). Shell completion (`hoody completion <shell>`) offers the final names only: it no longer offers the old spellings, the removed flags or the removed group aliases (`br`, `c`, `d` and the like). The bare `hoody` page lists `boturls` and, under Platform & account, `abusenotices`, and no longer lists `screenshot` (its commands are `hoody browser screenshots save`, `hoody display screenshots save` and `hoody terminal sessions screenshots save`).
- **`hoody files archives members read` takes the member as its second argument:** `hoody files archives members read <archive> <member>`. `--preview` is removed there and on `hoody files archives preview`, which now only lists the archive. On `hoody files archives extract`, `--extract` is renamed `--member`. Each old flag exits 2 and names the new form.
- **Search text is `--query`** on `hoody files search`, `hoody notes records search` and `hoody exec sdktypes list` (each was `--q`) and on `hoody exec logs get` (was `--search`). On `hoody files images convert`, `--q` is now `--jpeg-quality`. Each old flag exits 2 and names the new one.
- **`hoody db create` takes `--db`, like the other `db` commands.** `--path` exits 2 and names `--db`.
- **`--realm-id` and `--computer-id` are no longer CLI-wide flags.** Use `--realm` (a realm id or name) and `-c` / `--computer`. `--realm-id` exits 2 on every command and says it was renamed to `--realm` (on `hoody auth tokens realms add` and `remove` it says the realm is now a positional); `--realm B` now sends the command to realm B's host, which is what `--realm-id B` did. `--computer-id` exits 2 and names `--computer`, except on the commands where it is a real filter field (`hoody events list` and `events stream`, `proxy aliases list` and `create`, `boturls list`, `create` and `repoint`), which keep it. `--format` on a command that has no `--format` field exits 2 and names `--output`. Shell completion offers realm names after `--realm` and `hoody use` only.
- **On `hoody run search`, `run recipes create`, `update`, `resolve` and `search`, and `notes document get`, `--format` is now the command's own field.** It is sent to the server; before, it chose the output format. For the output format, use `-o` or `--output` (on `notes document get`, use `-o`: `--output` is refused there and says it was renamed to `--format`).
- **SDK methods have their stable names.** More than 200 methods and members, generated and hand-written, were renamed or removed before the stable release (for example `client.agent.bots.sendMessage` is now `client.agent.bots.messages.send`, and `client.agent.bots.stream` is now `client.agent.bots.streamLogs`). Hand-written members moved too: `HoodyClient.getKitUrls()` is now `listKitUrls()`, and `client.daemon.programs.attachTerminal()` is now `connectTerminal()`. The old names are gone. `docs/MIGRATION-2026-10-NAMES.md` lists every old name with its new one.
- **An old option key throws `RetiredOptionError`.** The option keys that carried an HTTP header lost the header spelling: `XHoodyCwd` is now `cwd`, `IfMatch` is now `ifMatch`, and `IdempotencyKey` is now `idempotencyKey`. A method called with an old key throws `RetiredOptionError` (a `ValidationError`) naming the new key, and sends nothing; the key is never ignored. `LastEventID` was removed from the streams that resume with their own query cursor (`since`, `since_seq` or `offset`). `proxyLogs.stream` keeps it as `lastEventId`.
- **`proxyLogs.stream` resumes with `lastEventId`.** Pass the `id` of the last frame you received, and the stream replays the frames after it. `afterId` only limits the first batch of a new connection.
- **State getters became methods, and the old getters throw.** For example `TerminalClient.state` is now `getState()`, `TunnelSession.id` is now `getId()`, and `MediaSession.active` is now `isActive()`. Reading an old getter throws and names the method. `NotificationDisplayClient.isActive` is now `isRunning()`: reading `isActive` throws, so calling `isActive()` throws too.
- **The events client has one state read.** `EventsClient.getConnectionState()` and `EventsManager.getState()` are retired and throw. `getLifecycleState()` is the one state read, with six states: `connected` is now `live` or `recovering`, `reconnecting` is now `offline`, and a closed stream is `closed`, not `idle`. The type `EventsConnectionState` is renamed `EventsLifecycleState`. `EventsManager.addLifecycleListener()` is now `addConnectionListener(event, callback)`, and `EventsClient.onLifecycle()` is now `onConnection()`, with the same handler keys; the old names throw.
- **`forceIPv4` and `forceIPv4Cache` are now `forceIpv4` and `forceIpv4Cache`.** The client options write the acronym as a word, like every other name. `HoodyClient` and `HttpClient` throw `RetiredOptionError` for an old key and name the new one. The type `IForceIPv4CacheConfig` is now `IForceIpv4CacheConfig`. `baseURL` and `getBaseURL()` keep their spelling.
- **Exported type names follow the method names.** Every type built for one operation is named `<Ns><Service><Method>` plus its kind (`Request`, `Response`, `Events` and the like). Types of operations that have no public method, numbered schema names (`Error2`, `HealthResponse3`) and package-prefixed names are no longer exported; a schema type keeps its spec name without the prefix (`main_KVEntry` is now `KVEntry`). `docs/MIGRATION-2026-10-NAMES.md` has the rule and the renamed hand-written types. The subpaths `'hoody-sdk/chat'`, `'hoody-sdk/ai'` and `'hoody-sdk/update-check'` are no longer exported: they served the CLI only, and importing one fails with `ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED`.
- **`hoody files get` and `files.get()` only list a directory or read a file.** The mode flags `--stat`, `--preview`, `--grep`, `--glob`, `--q`, `--thumbnail` and `--zip` are removed, with the flags that only those modes read. Each exits 2 and names the command that does the job: `hoody files stat`, `files archives preview`, `files grep`, `files glob`, `files search`, `files images convert` or `files zip`. In the SDK, `files.get()` refuses the same option keys, and `sha256` and `contents`, and names the method to call. The HTTP route is unchanged.
- **`hoody mount --list --full` shows the kit URL, subpath and start time of each mount.** `--verbose` and `-v` there exit 2 and name `--full`. Typed before the command (`hoody -v mount`), `-v` is still the global `--verbose`.
- **`hoody login --oauth` exits 2 and names `--web`,** which signs in the same way.
- **`hoody tunnel expose` and `hoody tunnel pull` take the local target only as their argument.** The argument also takes a URL: `hoody tunnel expose http://localhost:3000`. `--to` and `--url` exit 2 and name the argument form.
- **`--inline` on `hoody pipe send`, and `--json` and `--no-progress` on `hoody pipe receive`, are removed.** They had no effect. Each exits 2 and says so.
- **`hoody agent github diff` is now `hoody agent github diff get`, and `client.agent.github.diff` is now `client.agent.github.getDiff`.** The old command line exits 2 and names the new one.
- **More CLI flags have their stable names, and the old spellings exit 2.** On `hoody agent`: `sessions tools run --name` is now `--tool`, `sessions workflows start --name` is `--workflow`, `sessions shells stop --job-id` is `--shell-id`, `providers accounts remove` and `use --key` are `--account`, `acp secrets set --key` is `--secret`, and `mcp test` and `upsert --server-name` are `--name`. The key a turn, command or Bot message was sent with is `--idempotency-key` on `sessions turns keys get` and `retire`, `sessions commands keys get` and `retire` and `bots messages keys get` (was `--key`), as on `hoody cron runs list`; `sessions create --attach` and `--fork` are `--attach-session-id` and `--fork-session-id`, and `sessions waiters create --target` and `--from` are `--target-session-id` and `--from-turn-id`. A KEY=VALUE environment variable is `--env` everywhere: on `hoody computers create` and `update` (was `--environment-vars`), on `hoody computers env update` (was `--var`) and on `hoody daemon programs create`, `update` and `daemon ephemeralprograms start` (was `--environment`). On `hoody terminal sessions connect`, `--env-inject` and `--no-env-inject` are `--inject-hoody-env` and `--no-inject-hoody-env`. `hoody notifications send --display` is `--display-id`, and `notifications stream --displays` is `--display-ids`. `hoody browser screenshots capture` and `save --quality` is `--jpeg-quality`. On `hoody files backends sftp create`, `--password`, `--passphrase` and `--key` are `--pass`, `--key-file-pass` and `--key-pem`; on `files backends mega create`, `--password` is `--pass`; on `hoody exec magiccomments update` and `batch update`, `--comments-tokens` is `--comments-token`. Each old flag exits 2 and names the new one. The requests are unchanged.
- **Retired `hoody agent` flags now name their replacement.** `skills source get --root` and `--rel` name `--root-dir` and `--rel-dir`, `--lease` on `sessions approverlease claim` and `renew` names `--approver-lease`, and `--policy` on `sessions turns create`, `run` and `start` names `--gate-policy` (`start` takes it with `--stream`). They said only "removed from this command".
- **More ids and paths are arguments instead of flags:** `hoody servers plans quote <plan-id>`, `hoody exec scripts read|write|delete <path>`, `hoody exec magiccomments get|update <path>`, `hoody files archives members extract <archive> [member]` and `hoody terminal sessions screenshots save [terminal-id]` (terminal 1 when left out). `--plan-id`, `--path`, `--extract` and `--terminal-id` there exit 2 and name the argument form. With the path as an argument, `hoody exec scripts delete` takes `--keep-copy` and `--no-keep-copy` as switches: write `--no-keep-copy` where you wrote `--keep-copy false`, which now fails (exit 1).
- **Flags that did nothing, or that only the platform uses, are removed.** On `hoody run search`, `run test`, `run resolve`, `run jobs search start` and `run recipes create`, `update`, `resolve` and `search`, `--channel` and `--variant` (hoody-run does not apply them yet) and `--dry-run` and `--no-dry-run` (hoody-run never executes the app) exit 2 and say so; the older `--no-selector-dry-run`, `--no-selector-template-dry-run` and `--no-overrides-dry-run` give the same message instead of naming `--no-dry-run`. `--type` on `hoody files ftp get`, `s3 get`, `ssh get` and `webdav get` exits 2: the command already selects it. The internal-use-only flags of `hoody files backends mega create` (`--session-id`, `--master-key`) and `files backends protondrive create` (`--client-access-token`, `--client-refresh-token`, `--client-salted-key-pass`, `--client-uid`) exit 2.
- **`--json` on hand-written commands is replaced by the global `-o json`.** This covers `hoody support create` and `reply`; `hoody boturls ask`, `chat`, `status`, `answer`, `models list`, `updates list` and `events stream`; `hoody mount` and `unmount`; `hoody share list` and `stop`; `hoody local lock status` and `doctor`; `hoody pipe send`, `share`, `status` and `progress stream`; and `hoody run <app>`, `run close` and `run status`. `--json` exits 2 and names `-o json`. `-o json` prints what `--json` printed (`-o ndjson` also gives JSON lines on `boturls events stream` and `pipe progress stream`).
- **`--format` no longer chooses the output of `hoody config get`, `config validate`, `events stream` and `computers wait`.** Use `-o`: `config get -o json|yaml|table`, `config validate -o json`, `events stream -o ndjson`, `computers wait -o json`, or leave it out for the default. `--format` there exits 2 and names the `-o` value.
- **The `open` commands take CLI flag names instead of the page's URL keys.** `hoody files open --directory` and `--q` are `--dir` and `--query`. `hoody pipe open --n` is `--receivers` (`-n`), and `--audio`, `--live` and `--sha256` are switches (`--no-audio` turns audio off). `--parent-origin` on `terminal open`, `agent open` and `display open` is `--embedder-origin`. `display open --menu` is `--toolbar`. `--panel-width-pct` and `--panel-height-pct` on `terminal open` and `agent open` are `--panel-width` and `--panel-height`, which take `40%` or `400px`. `desktop open --env` and `hoody open --env` are `--desktop-env`. `notifications open --displays` is `--display-ids`. The pass-through on `exec open` and `display open` is `--query-param key=value`. Each old flag exits 2 and names the new one; a switch given a value (`--live 1`) exits 1.
- **`hoody run --env <name>` is `--local-env <name>`, `hoody exec remote send`, `call` and `connect --json` is `--json-args`, and the pass-through of an `exec` script command with no schema is `--query-param key=value` (was `--query`).** Each old flag exits 2 and names the new one.
- **`hoody config get -o yaml` prints the config file as YAML.** It printed JSON.
- **`hoody config validate -o json` prints the result as JSON.** It printed the text report.
- **`hoody events stream`, `computers wait`, `config validate` and `config get` refuse an `-o` value they do not print, with exit 2.** `events stream` takes `-o ndjson` or `-o json`, `computers wait` `-o json`, `config validate` `-o json` or `-o table`, and `config get` (the whole file) `-o json`, `-o yaml` or `-o table`.
- **`hoody exec remote send`, `call` and `connect` with `--json-args` keep the terminal output.** The old `--json` also switched a terminal to the plain output; `--json-args` only says how the arguments are read. For JSON output, use `-o json`.
- **`-o json` on the commands that took `--json` prints their JSON.** `hoody mount` (`--list`, `--prune` and a mount), `hoody unmount`, `hoody share list` and `stop`, `hoody local lock status` and `doctor`, `hoody pipe send`, `share` and `status`, `hoody run <app>` (with or without `--open`), `run close` and `run status`, and `hoody boturls ask`, `chat`, `answer`, `models list` and `updates list` print JSON under `-o json`, including the JSON error object when they fail. They printed their plain text. `hoody boturls status -o json` printed JSON already; when it fails it now prints the JSON error object too, where it printed nothing. `hoody pipe progress stream -o ndjson` (or `-o json`) prints JSON lines.
- **Two failures print the machine error object.** `hoody support create` and `reply`, when the CLI cannot make its HTTP client (an unknown profile, no config), print the error object on stdout under `-o json`, `-o yaml`, `-o ndjson` or `-o raw`, then exit as before. `hoody boturls events stream -o ndjson` (or `-o json`) ends a failed stream with one `{"error":{...}}` line after the events it printed.
- **More SDK option keys have their stable names, and the old keys throw `RetiredOptionError`.** The tunnel functions take the kit credential only as `kitAuth`: `token` on `exposeTunnel`, `pullTunnel`, `serveTunnel`, `resumeTunnelExpose`, `resumeTunnelPull`, `startTunnelSocks5` and `new TunnelSession()` now throws, where it was ignored. `shell()` and `terminal.run()` pick the terminal with `terminalId` (was `serviceIndex`). `browser.page.getSnapshot()` takes the operation budget as `operationTimeoutMs` (was `paramTimeoutMs`).
- **The tunnel option types follow their function.** `TunnelExposeOptions`, `TunnelPullOptions`, `TunnelServeOptions`, `ResumeExposeOptions`, `ResumePullOptions` and `TunnelSocks5Options` are now `ExposeTunnelOptions`, `PullTunnelOptions`, `ServeTunnelOptions`, `ResumeTunnelExposeOptions`, `ResumeTunnelPullOptions` and `StartTunnelSocks5Options`.
- **An old hand-written method name throws a `TypeError` that names the new method.** For example `HoodyClient.getKitUrls()` throws "HoodyClient.getKitUrls() was retired. Call listKitUrls() instead." This covers the renamed methods of `HoodyClient`, `client.run`, `client.daemon.programs`, `client.agent.bots`, `client.exec`, `ExecRemoteConnection`, `ExecRemoteSession`, `BotUrlClient`, `PipeStream`, `CurlChannel`, `CurlChannelStream`, `EventsManager`, `EventsClient` and `NotificationDisplayClient`. Before, the old name was `undefined`.
- **An option that only the other form of a method takes now throws a `ValidationError`.** `agent.sessions.turns.start({ gatePolicy })` or `({ followSession })` without `stream: true`, `display.screenshots.capture({ metadata: true })` with `base64`, `region` or `cursor` and `display.screenshots.getLatest({ metadata: true, base64 })` say what the option needs (`agent.sessions.turns.start: gatePolicy needs stream: true`); the old spellings `policy` and `XHoodyGatePolicy` there throw `RetiredOptionError`, which names `gatePolicy` and says it needs `stream: true`. Before, the option was dropped without a word.
- **`hoody agent <computer>` and `hoody shell <computer>` refuse a second, different computer typed with `-c` or `--computer`.** The argument won before and the flag was dropped without a word. Two different computers now exit 2 before anything is sent. The same computer given both ways is used once, so `hoody -c A shell A` (A a computer id) opens a shell on A; it used to run a command called `A` there. To run it, write `hoody -c A shell -- A`.
- **`--confirm` is removed from `hoody exec scripts delete`, `hoody exec logs clear` and `hoody kv table rollback`, and `hoody kv table rollback --dry-run` is now `--preview`.** These three commands ask before they act, as the other destructive commands do; pass `--yes` to skip the question, in a script too, where a script passed `--confirm true` or `--confirm yes`. `hoody kv table rollback --preview` shows what the rollback would change and changes nothing. The old `--confirm` and `--dry-run` exit 2 and say what to pass instead. The SDK calls are unchanged.
- **`--env` on `hoody terminal commands run` and `hoody terminal sessions connect` can be given more than once, and every value is sent.** Each `--env KEY=VALUE` is one variable, and a value with commas stays one variable (`--env LIST=a,b`). Before, only the last one was sent. The SDK is unchanged: `env` on `terminal.commands.run()` is still one `KEY=VALUE` string.
- **A two-factor challenge is data under `-o json` and `-o yaml`.** With `-o json` or `-o yaml`, a sign-in that needs a two-factor code prints the challenge as JSON on stdout: `requires_2fa`, `temp_token` and `next`, the command that finishes the sign-in. Nothing goes to stderr and the exit code stays 2. Plain output is unchanged.

- **The SDK and CLI give a computer create 7 minutes and send it with an `Idempotency-Key`.** A create can take several minutes, and the client gave up after 30 seconds while the server went on; the retry then got a 409 with no code and no computer ID, and a create under a new name made a second computer. `api.computers.create()` and `hoody computers create` now wait up to 7 minutes, send one key per call and send the same key again when they retry, so a retry returns the first create's computer. On 409 `IDEMPOTENCY_REQUEST_IN_PROGRESS` they wait for `Retry-After` and ask again with the same key and body, without spending a retry and never past the call's own deadline. To retry yourself after your own timeout or a restart, pass your own key (`headers: { 'Idempotency-Key': key }` in the SDK, `--idempotency-key <key>` in the CLI) and send the same body within 24 hours. It needs the API from this release.
