- **`hoody firewall rules list` shows outbound rules.** The table showed only inbound rules, and with only outbound rules it printed "No items found.". It now prints two titled tables, "Ingress (inbound)" and "Egress (outbound)", with a new DESTINATION column (DESCRIPTION with `-o wide`), "None." under a direction with no rules, and "No firewall rules." when there are none at all. `-o json` and `-o yaml` are unchanged.
- **A second Ctrl-C on a foreground `hoody mount` exits at once and leaves the path mounted.** The first Ctrl-C waits for files still uploading (it says how many); a second one exits the CLI without waiting, and the files stay queued and the path stays mounted until you run `hoody unmount <path>`.
- **`timeout` on `terminal.commands.run()` is typed `number`**, so a fraction compiles; the service rounds it up.
- **New error codes in the types:** `bot_owned_elsewhere` (409) on the `agent.bots.*` methods and Bot connections, `turn_key_retired` and `command_key_retired` (409) on agent session turns and commands, `store_failed` (500) on `agent.bots.delete()`, `COMPUTER_EXPIRED` (409) on the computer lifecycle and snapshot calls, `ACCESS_SUSPENDED` (403), `ENTRY_EXISTS` (409) on `cron.entries.update()`, `INVALID_AFTER_SEQ` and `INVALID_FRESH` (400) on `terminal.sessions.wait()`, `CONFLICT` (409) on the SQLite KV, history and read-only query calls, `INVALID_REQUEST` (400) on `watch.watchers.get()` and `delete()`, `SYNC_TIMEOUT` (409) on the display window actions and `input.act()`, `DISPLAY_BUSY` (409) on `terminal.system.stopDisplay()`, `COMPUTER_STOPPED` in the proxy-logs 503, and the computer create codes above.
- **Agent stream errors say when to send the message again.** An error event carries `retry_after_secs` whenever the model provider named a wait, not only with `quota_wait_too_long`, and the new `retry_at` gives the time that wait ends (RFC 3339, UTC). With `no_credentials`, `diag` names the provider and model that have no stored key. This applies to `agent.sessions.turns.start(id, data, { stream: true })` and `agent.sessions.connect()`.
- **The display viewer asks for webp by default**, which keeps text sharp. Its `encoding` option (also on `client.embeds.display.client()`) now sets the encoding the viewer asks the server for; `encoding=auto` lets the server pick.
- **`agent.completions.create()` waits up to 125 seconds, and an operation's own deadline no longer shortens yours.** It used the client's 30-second request timeout; it now waits up to 125 seconds, or 5 seconds past the body's `timeout_ms` when that is longer, and so does `agent.skills.hub.preview()`. A deadline that an operation declares is now a minimum: a longer client `timeout`, or none (`0`), stands. A `timeoutMs` on the call always wins.
- **`hoody daemon programs logs stream` and `hoody watch events stream` take the id as an argument.** `hoody daemon programs logs stream <id>` and `hoody watch events stream <id>`. Two different ids, or a blank one, are refused before any request.
- **`curl.schedules.create()` and `delete()` answers are typed.** The `data` of `create()` is `{ success, schedule_id, message }` (201 created, 200 already existed) and the `data` of `delete()` is `{ success, message }`, where both were `unknown`.
- **Files backend options take any string where only examples were listed.** About 60 options of the `files.backends.create*()` requests, such as S3 `endpoint`, `region` and `provider`, WebDAV `vendor` and Drive `scope`, were typed as a fixed list of values. They are now `string`, and the listed values are examples in the reference. Options that allow only those values keep their list.
- **Syncing a copied computer is refused for now.** `api.computers.sync()` (CLI: `hoody computers sync`) answers 409 `COMPUTER_SYNC_UNAVAILABLE` ("Syncing a copy is not available yet. To get the source's latest files, copy the source again.") once the usual checks pass, and the computer is left as it was. A sync of a running copy used to mark the copy failed while it kept running. To get the source's latest files, copy the source again with `api.computers.copy()` (CLI: `hoody computers copy`) and delete the old copy.
- **Proxy permission and hook writes refuse bodies that do not match their schema** with 400. The permission and hook methods (on `api.computers.proxy.*` and `api.projects.proxy.*`) used to convert and drop values: `access: 1` and `0` were stored as `true` and `false`, a JWT claim `1` came back as the string `"1"`, `enable_proxy: 1` became `true`, and misspelt keys were dropped without a word. Now numbers stay numbers, claims keep the type you wrote, and these answer 400: a token group with two locations, a project document carrying `computer`, a misspelt key, `enable_proxy: 1` or `"true"`, a hook `timeout: "500"`. A program that takes only a boolean, given a number (`files: 1`), answers 422. The `file_version`, `etag` and `schema_version` that GET returns can still be sent back on a whole-document PUT. Rules stored earlier are not changed; check stored rules you wrote with `1` or `0`.
- **Updating or copying an auth token refuses fields it does not know** with 400 `UNKNOWN_FIELD`, naming the field, as creating one already did. `api.auth.tokens.update()` and `api.auth.tokens.copy()` (CLI: `hoody auth tokens update`, `copy`) dropped them silently, so a misspelt option (`expires_in` for `expires_at`) had no effect.
- **Copying an expired auth token needs an explicit `expires_at`.** `api.auth.tokens.copy()` of a token that has expired, with no `expires_at`, made a copy that never expires. It now answers 400 (`details.field: "expires_at"`): "The source token has expired, so its expiration cannot be copied. Pass expires_at with the new expiration, or null for a token that does not expire."
- **Creating a computer is safe to retry with an `Idempotency-Key`.** `api.computers.create()` (CLI: `hoody computers create`) accepts an `Idempotency-Key` header. A retry with the same key and the same body gets the first request's answer, with the `Idempotent-Replayed: true` header, instead of making a second computer or failing on the name: 201 with the computer as it is now, the error when the create failed after making a computer (`data.computer_id` names it), 409 `COMPUTER_CREATE_INTERRUPTED` when it stopped before finishing, and 404 when the computer has since been deleted. While the first request is still running, a retry waits up to 20 seconds, then answers 409 `IDEMPOTENCY_REQUEST_IN_PROGRESS` with `Retry-After`. A key is kept for 24 hours and belongs to the user who sent it; a create that fails with nothing created frees its key. A key that is empty, longer than 128 characters or not printable answers 400 `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_INVALID`; a key reused for a different request answers 422 `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED`.
- **A name already in use is reported with the computer that has it.** `api.computers.create()`, `api.computers.update()` (rename) and `api.computers.copy()` answer 409 `COMPUTER_NAME_IN_USE`, with `data.computer_id` and `data.status` when you can read that computer (`status` is `creating` while it is still being set up). These conflicts came back with no code and no computer ID. If it is the computer you meant to create, use it; otherwise choose another name.
- **Every account API 409 carries a specific error `code`.** Conflicts answered 409 with no `code`, so a script could only match the message. Each now has its own code, listed in the API reference for the operation; a create or copy that cannot be placed while a server is busy answers 409 `SERVER_BUSY`, apart from `SERVER_DELETION_IN_PROGRESS`.
- **A restart recovers from a slow shutdown.** `api.computers.restart()` (CLI: `hoody computers restart`) gives the computer 60 seconds to shut down cleanly (it was 30), then stops it forcibly and starts it again, and a restart that stopped the computer without starting it tries the start again. The answer says so, for example "Computer restarted. It did not shut down cleanly within the deadline, so it was stopped forcibly and then started." `api.computers.stop()` also stops forcibly past the deadline ("Computer stopped. It did not shut down cleanly within the deadline, so it was stopped forcibly."). For `stop` and `restart`, `timeout` is now the clean-shutdown time, and the call can run up to 420 seconds longer than it; for the other operations it is still the time after which the operation is cut off. A shutdown that ends only after these attempts can still leave the computer stopped; the answer then reports the state the API read back (see the next entry), so check the computer's state after a failed restart and start it if it is stopped.
- **A failed start, stop or restart says what state the computer is in.** When the computer's server reports a failure, the API reads the computer's state back, and the answer's message and `data.status` say it is now stopped where it is (for example "The computer did not shut down in time, so the restart did not complete. The computer is now stopped; start it to bring it back."). The record showed `running` for several minutes, and the message advised a retry. Status code and error code are unchanged.
- **A deleted computer's URLs stop at once.** When `api.computers.delete()` (CLI: `hoody computers delete`) answers, the computer's service URLs already answer 404 `MAPPING_NOT_FOUND`. They kept serving for a moment and then held each request for about 30 seconds. When the call cannot confirm this with the computer's server, the URLs stop when the background removal runs. A `GET` of the computer can answer 404 shortly before its URLs do.
- **Every sign-in is a session you can list and end on its own.** Signing in (password, two-factor, GitHub, Google, device code, signup) now records a login session; the answer carries `data.session_id`. `api.auth.sessions.list()` (CLI: `hoody auth sessions list`) shows each one with its method, client, addresses, country, start and last renewal, and which is yours; `api.auth.sessions.revoke(id)` (`hoody auth sessions revoke <id>`, or `current` for this one) ends one; `api.auth.sessions.revokeOthers()` (`hoody auth sessions revoke --others`) ends every other one. An ended session answers 401 `SESSION_REVOKED` on every request and on token refresh, also when the refresh token has expired; a plain expired refresh token answers 401 `TOKEN_EXPIRED`. Ending a session does not disable the auth tokens it created; the list shows how many each session created, and `api.auth.tokens.*` removes them. Signing in more than 30 times an hour on one account answers 429 `SESSION_CREATE_RATE_LIMITED` with `Retry-After`. A password or two-factor change, "sign out everywhere" and a refresh token reused after 30 seconds end sessions as before. Tokens issued before this update carry no session, so every account signs in once more after it.
- **A hoody-files append is never sent twice.** `files.append()` and `files.upload({ append: true })` (Node and browser) retried the request after a lost connection or a 500, 502, 503 or 504, so a line could be appended twice while the call reported success. They now send it once: an explicit `retries` resends only a request that never reached the kit. Their 500 for a connection lost mid-body says so; the CLI already sent appends once.
- **Saving a whole permission document answers with the document as stored.** `api.computers.proxy.setPermissions()` and `api.projects.proxy.setPermissions()` answer with what GET returns, `hooks`, `schema_version`, `file_version` and `etag` included, as well as the ID of each new auth group. The answer left these out, so a script had to call GET again before its next save; the `etag` in the answer is now enough.
- **A computer permission document with hooks can be read and saved back.** A document from `api.computers.proxy.getPermissions()` that carried hooks could not be saved with `setPermissions()`: each hook's `id` and `applies_to` were refused. A hook rule in `setPermissions()` now takes `id` (required, exactly as GET returns it) and `applies_to: { groups }`. To add a hook, create it with `api.computers.proxy.createHook()` (CLI: `hoody computers proxy hooks create`), which gives it its ID. An ID used twice in one document answers 422.
- **`api.events.get()` returns the event's `payload`.** The detail answer always carried `payload: {}`, while the list carried the real payload. It now returns the same payload as `api.events.list()` (CLI: `hoody events get`).
- **A kit address of a stopped computer answers at once with 503 `COMPUTER_STOPPED`.** A request to a stopped computer's kit or port address hung for about a minute and ended with 504. It now answers 503 with code `COMPUTER_STOPPED` and no `Retry-After`, since waiting does not help: start the computer and send it again. An SDK or CLI call may still retry the 503 up to twice before it fails.
- **Permission-check errors on a computer's addresses are written in user terms.** 503 `migration_pending` says "The permission settings for this computer are being updated. Retry shortly." and 500 `unknown_schema_version` "The permission settings for this computer could not be read. Retry later." Status codes, error codes and `Retry-After` are unchanged.
- **hoody-exec no longer moves scripts to a new hoody-sdk prerelease or major on its own.** Scripts that load `hoody-sdk` at run time used to get every new `latest` within hours, even one that changes the API they call. hoody-exec now updates automatically only to a newer release of the same major as the installed one (the same minor below 1.0.0) where neither is a prerelease. Anything else, this release included, is held until you install it: `exec.modules.install()` (CLI: `hoody exec modules install`) with `hoody-sdk@<version>`, active after hoody-exec restarts. `exec.modules.listBundled()` (CLI: `hoody exec modules bundled list`) reports it on the `hoody-sdk` row in `sdkUpdate`: `latest` (the registry's `latest` at the last check), `held` (a newer `latest` not installed automatically), `staged` (`{ version, origin }`, the version that becomes active at the next hoody-exec restart; `origin` is `auto` for the periodic check or `request` for an install you asked for) and `checkedAt`; each is `null` when there is nothing to show. Pinning the SDK in the scripts `package.json` stops the notice.
- **A hoody-exec restart no longer waits on npm for pre-loaded packages no script uses.** The first restart after hoody-exec had run once installed every pre-loaded package that was missing before it answered again, so the kit was unreachable for about 15 seconds. A missing pre-loaded package is now installed for the first script that uses it, including a script that uses a pre-loaded global without `require` (`cookie.parse(…)`, `_.chunk(…)`, `z.string()`), which used to see `undefined` when the package was missing.
- **`curl.run({ response: 'json' })` keeps the first `Set-Cookie` in `headers` and adds `raw_headers` with every header line.** The JSON response kept only the last value of a header sent more than once, so all cookies but one were lost. `headers` is still one string per lowercased name: a list header sent more than once (`vary`, `cache-control`, `link`, `via`, `www-authenticate`, ...) has its values joined with `, ` in the order received, and any other repeated header (`set-cookie`, `content-type`, `location`, names not known as lists) keeps its first value, where it used to keep the last. The new field `raw_headers` (`[{ name, value }]`) lists every header line in order, with each name as the server sent it, one entry per `Set-Cookie`. Read cookies from `raw_headers`. The same applies to `hoody curl run` in JSON mode.
- **hoody-exec scripts know their computer on every trigger.** `metadata.projectId` and `metadata.computerId` are set for HTTP requests, `@schedule` runs, WebSocket init and `pre.js`, and remote init. Outside HTTP they could be missing, so `withComputer({ id: metadata.computerId, project_id: metadata.projectId, ... })` failed in a scheduled script.
- **SQLite refuses values that do not fit the statement's placeholders** with 400 `SQL_BIND_ERROR`, on `sqlite.sql.runTransaction()` (CLI: `hoody db transactions run`). Surplus values were ignored and the statement ran, so a request with 5 values for 4 placeholders stored rows and answered 200. An array must now hold one entry per positional slot (each `?` takes the next slot, `?NNN` and `$NNN` name slot NNN) and an object one key per distinct name; a missing or surplus entry is refused before the item runs. `reqIdx` names the item, and for `valuesBatch` the message names the row (`row 1: 5 values for 4 positional placeholders`). With `noFail`, a batch row that does not fit is skipped and listed in `rowErrors`, and the other rows run.
- **Cron publishes its events and keeps a list of runs.** `cron.events.stream()` (server-sent events; CLI: `hoody cron events stream`) and `cron.events.connect()` (WebSocket) deliver live events about the computer's crontabs: managed entries created, updated or deleted (`source` `api`, `expired` or `external`), whole crontabs replaced or edited (`crontab_changed`), and runs of managed jobs (`job_started`, `job_finished`). An edit made outside the kit, such as `crontab -e`, appears within about 2 seconds with `source: external`. `since_id` resumes from the last 1024 events, which are kept in memory only; an id from before a restart answers 409 `HISTORY_GAP`. `cron.runs.list()` (CLI: `hoody cron runs list`) lists the latest runs, newest first: up to 500 still running and the last 500 ended. Raw crontab lines and crontabs whose `SHELL` is not a POSIX shell have no job events and no runs, and a job that exits without reporting its end finishes with `outcome: lost`.
- **A crontab replace can be made conditional.** `cron.crontabs.get()`, `cron.entries.list()` and `cron.crontabs.set()` return the crontab's `etag` and an `ETag` header, and `cron.crontabs.list()` an `etag` on each crontab. `cron.crontabs.set(user, data, { ifMatch })` (CLI: `hoody cron crontabs set --if-match`) replaces the crontab only if it still has that ETag; otherwise it answers 412 `PRECONDITION_FAILED` and writes nothing. Without `ifMatch` the replace is unconditional, as before.
- **`sqlite.kv.set()` with `ifValue` compares a JSON value as a value** (CLI: `hoody kv set --if-value`). The comparison was on bytes, so a JSON string stored as `"v1"` never matched `v1`, and an object matched only in its original spacing and key order. Against a JSON value, `ifValue` is now read as one JSON value: any spacing and key order matches, and numbers compare as written (`1` and `1.0` differ). Text that is not valid JSON is the string with that text, so `v1` matches the stored string `v1`; a string whose text is itself JSON (such as `5` or `true`) is sent JSON-quoted, and the 412 answer says so when that is the cause. Text and binary values still compare byte for byte; `--if-match` (the ETag) is unchanged.
- **A notes socket receives comment events from connect.** `notes.sockets.connect()` delivers comment events for the documents its identity can read without a `synchronizer.input` sent first. A client that only listened received none.
- **Notifications reach enrolled browsers and phones as Web Push, also with no page open.** The notifications kit's own page enrolls a browser for push, for the page's selected display or for all displays (on iPhone and iPad, only from the page added to the Home Screen). After that, each notification new to a display's history is sent to every subscription whose `displays` include that display; a subscription created through the API without `displays` gets all displays. Rows already in the history when the kit starts, dismissed rows and expired rows are not pushed. Delivery is best effort: a network error, 429 or 5xx gets up to 3 attempts, and any other refusal fails at once. A failed delivery puts the device in a cooldown (1 minute, doubling with each failure in a row, at most 1 hour), and notifications in that time are skipped, not sent later. The history and the event stream stay the record. A subscription the push service reports gone (404 or 410) is deleted. When push is turned off on the kit, the push routes answer 503 `PUSH_DISABLED`.
- **Display screenshots are several times faster.** `display.screenshots.capture()` (CLI: `hoody display screenshots capture`) took about 6 to 7 seconds of server time per capture; in a test on a large screen a capture went from about 3 s to about 0.7 s. One value changed: `info.thumbnail` is `null` in the answers of the capture calls, because the thumbnail is made after the answer. `display.screenshots.list()` and the thumbnail calls show it once it is written.
- **Display window actions with `sync` answer 409 `SYNC_TIMEOUT` when the change is not seen.** `display.windows.move()`, `resize()`, `minimize()` and `focus()` answered 503 `DISPLAY_NOT_AVAILABLE` when the change was not seen in time, as if the display were down, and `display.windows.restore()` answered 500 `INPUT_ACTION_FAILED` for a window still minimized. When the display answers, they now return 409 `SYNC_TIMEOUT` ("Window <id> did not change size after the resize request within 5 s" and the like): the request was sent, and the window manager refused or adjusted it, or the window was already that way. Read the window's geometry or state before retrying, or send the request without `sync`. 503 now means the display did not answer. `display.input.act()` returns the same error for those actions.
- **A display that cannot start for missing software reports `details.reason: "dependency_missing"`.** The 503 `DISPLAY_NOT_AVAILABLE` answer carried one of four `dependency_missing: <name>` values; match `dependency_missing` instead. The message is "The display cannot start: this computer is missing software the display needs"; retrying does not help until the computer runs an image that has it.
- **Display error answers name no programs.** Each failure has a fixed message, for example "The input action failed" (500 `INPUT_ACTION_FAILED`), "The clipboard could not be read or written" (500 `CLIPBOARD_FAILED`), "The display did not respond in time" (503) and "The window does not exist or has closed" (404 `WINDOW_NOT_FOUND`). Codes and statuses are unchanged; match the `code`, not the message.
- **Display keyboard actions say when the keys may not have arrived.** `display.keyboard.type()` and `display.keyboard.press()` with a `window` that does not have keyboard focus still send the keys, as synthetic events, but the answer now carries `details.warning` saying so and how to focus the window first. They answered plain success while many applications received nothing. In `display.input.act()` and `display.input.actMany()`, keyboard entries carry the same `warning` in `result`.
- **`display.windows.focus()` works on a display with no window manager.** On a display started with `desktop: true` and no `desktop_env`, focus answered 409 `NO_WINDOW_MANAGER`, so a GUI app there could not be typed into. A viewable window is now given keyboard focus; the answer is 200 with `inputFocus: true` and a `details.warning` saying the window was not activated or raised and that `display.windows.getActive()` cannot report it. It answers 409 `NO_WINDOW_MANAGER` only when the window cannot take focus either. `display.input.click()` and `display.input.type()` there give the clicked window keyboard focus too, so the text that follows reaches it.
- **`display.keyboard.press()` without a `window`, with the pointer over the bare desktop of a display with no window manager, is refused** with 409 `WINDOW_NOT_VIEWABLE`, as `keyboard.type()` already was. It answered success while nothing received the keys.
- **Window position and geometry are right on a display with no window manager.** `display.windows.getGeometry()` reported a position that matched neither the request nor the screen, and `display.windows.move()` landed at the requested point plus the window's start offset, so clicks computed from the geometry missed. Geometry now reports the window's top-left corner, border included, in screen coordinates, and move puts it there. Where a desktop environment draws a frame, move places the frame at that point and geometry reports the window inside it. With `sync`, move waits up to 2 seconds (was 5), and a move to where the window already is answers at once.
- **`display.windows.restore()` brings a minimized window back on an xfce desktop.** It left the window minimized there.
- **A display mouse move to where the pointer already is answers at once.** `display.mouse.move()` and `moveBy()` with `sync: true` to the pointer's own position waited 5 seconds and answered 503 `DISPLAY_NOT_AVAILABLE` on a healthy display.
- **`terminal.system.stopDisplay()` waits for a screenshot of that display in progress.** The stop waits up to 25 seconds for the screenshot to finish before it stops anything. When it is still running after that, the stop answers 409 `DISPLAY_BUSY` ("Display :<N> is busy: a screenshot of it is in progress. Retry the stop") and the display keeps running; retry the stop.
- **`terminal.commands.run()` refuses a negative, too large or non-numeric `timeout`** with 400 `INVALID_TIMEOUT` ("'timeout' must be a number of seconds from 0 to 2000000"), and the command does not run. A negative timeout was raised to 0, which means no timeout, so the command ran to its end. A fraction is rounded up to whole seconds (`0.5` becomes 1) where it was cut to 0, and with `wait` the wait lasts the full timeout. `0` or no timeout still means no timeout. CLI: `hoody terminal commands run`.
- **A terminal session waits for its display only where one can start.** `terminal.sessions.create()` with `wait_until_display` on a computer where no display server holds the display and none can be started now answers at once, with `display_ready: false` and a message saying the display cannot be started there; the session is created. `wait_timeout` is capped at 300 seconds; when it runs out the answer is an error with code `timeout`, and the session keeps running.
- **A daemon program with a `ready_port` is reported started as soon as it serves.** `daemon.programs.start(id, { wait: true })` (CLI: `hoody daemon programs start`) waited a fixed startup window even after the program was listening; a measured start went from about 1.15 s to about 0.29 s. The start now succeeds once the port accepts a connection from the program's own processes. A program that listens and then crashes within its first second is therefore reported started; one that crashes before it listens is still a failed start. Programs without `ready_port` wait as before. Daemon error messages and the daemon API reference are written in user terms.
- **A daemon program that runs in a terminal keeps its output in its log.** For a program with `terminal_id`, output that reaches the terminal, stderr included, is also written to the program's stdout log as raw terminal output, so `daemon.programs.getLogs()` (CLI: `hoody daemon programs logs get <id>`) shows its last output after it ends. Output still waiting in the terminal when the program ends because nothing had attached, and output written faster than the log takes it in, is not in that log.
- **The first browser start in a computer no longer downloads Chrome.** `browser.instances.start()` and `restart()` (CLI: `hoody browser start`) with no `chromiumVersion` start the Chrome build supplied with the computer. The first start downloaded Chrome, about 16 seconds of a first start that took about 20 seconds, and could exceed the SDK's 30-second default timeout. The supplied build may lag the newest stable; `browserFullVersion` in the metadata reports the version that started. Pass `chromiumVersion: "stable"` (or a version) to resolve the newest build when the instance starts.
- **A browser navigation sent right after a failed one loads its page.** After a navigation failed (a redirect loop, a refused connection), the next `browser.page.navigate()` on the same tab, sent at once, could answer 502 `NAVIGATION_ERROR` although the page loaded. A failed navigation now waits briefly for the tab to settle before it answers (at most 2 seconds, never past its `timeoutMs`). The same holds for `browser.page.captureScreenshot()` and `exportPdf()` with a `url`.
- **A pipe transfer whose sender left mid-way is reported failed.** A sender with no `Content-Length` that dropped out mid-transfer was recorded `complete`: receivers got a clean end of a short file and the `sha256` receipt held the digest of the short bytes. The transfer now ends "Sender disconnected", receivers' downloads fail, and the receipt is `failed` with no digest. A transfer without a declared length takes about 0.1 s longer to finish; send a `Content-Length` to rule out a cut that arrives late.
- **Extracting an archive over existing files no longer destroys them when the archive is damaged.** A corrupt or cut-short zip or tar entry truncated the file it was replacing. Each entry now replaces a file only once it was read whole and checked, and the replaced file keeps its owner and mode; if the replacement fails, the old file stays and the error names the entry. A damaged archive answers 400 with "Archive corrupted or unreadable: <entry>: ..." where it answered a bare 500. The result and the error of `files.archives.extract()` (CLI: `hoody files archives extract`) carry `replaced`, the entries that replaced a file already there (at most 1000), and `replaced_count`.
- **An archive that is refused changes nothing.** `files.archives.extract()` refuses a TAR or ZIP archive that breaks a limit, holds a path that is not allowed or has a damaged entry before it writes anything. A new destination folder is built beside its final name and gets that name only once it is complete; a cancelled or failed extraction removes it. Failure answers carry `created` and `created_count`.
- **A copy cut short by a files kit stop answers 503 `SERVICE_STOPPING`.** `files.copy()` cut short because the files kit is stopping or restarting answered a bare 500. It now answers 503 `SERVICE_STOPPING` with `Retry-After: 5`: the copy was not made, so send it again once the kit is back. A copy that had already changed its destination still answers 500.
- **A create-only upload that loses a race answers 412.** `files.upload()` and `files.append()` with `If-None-Match: *`, and WebDAV uploads, answered 409 `FILE_PATH_CHANGED` when another request created the file first. They now answer 412, as when the file already existed.
- **A file's history is compared with the file on disk.** `files.get(path, { diff, from_seq })` (or `from_ts`) with no `to_seq` or `to_ts` compares that revision with the file as it is on disk now (410 when the file no longer exists, 422 when it is over the journal's size limit), where it compared two journal records. `history` answers carry `head_matches_disk`, and `at`, `revision` and `diff` answers the `X-Journal-Head-Matches-Disk` header: `false` when the file was changed, created or removed outside Hoody Files. The journal records only changes made through Hoody Files, not those made by the agent's tools, a terminal, a script or a program.
- **A server-side mount of a WebDAV backend no longer overwrites a newer change made on the remote.** When the mount uploaded a file that had been changed on the remote since, the remote's version was lost. The remote now keeps it as a conflict copy beside the file. Mounts of other backend types are still last-writer-wins.
- **`files.append()` refuses a cut-off body instead of appending part of it.** A body that ends early or runs long against `X-Expected-Length` is refused with 400 and nothing is appended; a declared length over the upload size limit is refused with 413 before the body is read.
- **The files kit stops when asked, also with mounts in use.** Stopping or restarting it (`daemon.programs.stop()`, a kit update, a computer restart) could time out and leave it unreachable (503) for about 13 minutes. It now shuts down promptly, and deleting a backend closes its connection to the remote when no other mount uses it.
- **Custom agent providers on public hosts with an IPv6 address are accepted.** `agent.providers.create()` and `update()` (CLI: `hoody agent providers create --endpoint`) refused every host that also has an IPv6 address, which is most public APIs, with 422 `provider_invalid`. The host's IPv4 addresses are now checked; a host with no IPv4 address is refused with 422 `provider_invalid` "has no IPv4 address".
- **A running agent session stops using a provider key you deleted or replaced.** Deleting or setting a provider key (`agent.providers.deleteApiKey()`, `setApiKey()`), OAuth sign-in or sign-out, changing the default credential, and switching or removing an account left live sessions on the old credential. An idle session now picks up the change at once and a busy one before its next turn. With no other credential left, it answers `no_credentials` until a key is stored again or the session switches model. A turn already running finishes on its old credential.
- **`agent.sessions.setModel()` refuses a model the catalogue does not list** with 422 `model_unavailable`, `details.reason` `unknown_model`, and changes nothing. It accepted any model name. `agent.models.list()` gives the choices.
- **The agent's bash tool honours a `timeout` shorter than the default.** It was raised to the default; only a value above the cap is lowered.
- **An agent whose Hoody sign-in was revoked or has expired says so.** `agent.whoami()` (CLI: `hoody agent whoami`) checks the sign-in (waiting at most 5 seconds) and reports a revoked one in `last_error` as `hoody_auth_expired`; calls that need the platform answer 409 `hoody_auth_expired` instead of 400 `bad_request`, and a command on a computer no longer fails with a misleading "exited 0" message. The error names the fix: sign the agent in again (`hoody agent login`, `agent.login()`). New sessions stay on the agent's own machine while the sign-in is refused, so local commands keep working.
- **Agent errors about setting up credentials on a computer say what did not happen and what to do**, for example "could not set up this session's Hoody credentials on computer <id>, so the command did not run; try again, and if it keeps failing, the agent's logs have the details".
- **An oversized upload to the agent answers 413.** A transcription, a Bot audio message or another agent request body over its size limit could end with 502 instead of the agent's 413. The agent now reads the rest of a body it refuses, so the 413 arrives, as do the transcription and Bot audio operations' own refusals. A refusal made before the request reaches the operation, such as a rate limit, and a sender slower than about 10 seconds for the rest of the body can still end with 502.
- **A Bot takes `instructions`, and its log can be read from the end.** `agent.bots.create()` and `update()` (CLI: `--instructions` on `hoody agent bots create` and `update`) take optional `instructions` on how the Bot should work, such as tone or format; they never override its rules or guardrails and are not passed to delegates (`""` clears them on update). A Bot's name, role, instructions, guardrails and dispatch limits together must fit in 16 KiB; a write past that answers 400 `bad_request` with `details.field`. `agent.bots.getLogs()` and `getArchive()` (CLI: `--before`) take `before`: the newest rows below that `seq`, oldest first; and `getLogs()` (`--tail`) takes `tail` for the newest rows of the log.
- **`ssh <host> '<command>'` runs commands with shell syntax.** `&&`, `;`, `|`, `$(…)` and quoting were refused; the command now runs in your computer's shell, as in an interactive session, and returns its output and exit code. A command is refused with a message saying why when it contains a NUL byte, is not valid UTF-8, or is longer than 131071 bytes ("Put it in a script file and run that instead").
- **A burst of SSH logins no longer locks out a valid key with "Permission denied".** A client that tries too often sees "Too many SSH login attempts from your address. Wait about N seconds, then connect again." SSH into a computer connects over IPv4; an IPv6 connection is not accepted.
- **A Bot served by another agent process answers 409 `bot_owned_elsewhere`.** Every call on such a Bot answers this when another agent process on the machine holds it, or when its lock cannot be taken (`details.reason` `held` or `lock_unavailable`), while `agent.bots.list()` shows it as a locked row: the other `agent.bots.*` methods, the Bot voice, realtime and Twilio connections, the Bot URLs and the OpenAI- and Anthropic-compatible doors. Nothing of the Bot is read or changed. The answer carries `Retry-After: 5`, `details.retry_after_secs` and `details.owner` (`pid` and `started_at`, or `null`). Retry after that wait, or reach the Bot through the agent that serves it.
- **Bots are suspended when the agent's account signs out, changes, or no longer serves the Bot's realm.** A suspended Bot stops working and is not listed, and its routes answer 404 `not_found` as for an unknown id. It resumes on its own when the same account serves its realm again. `agent.bots.list()` reports how many are hidden in `suspended_count`, and `agent.bots.resumeAll()` (`realm=all` for every realm; CLI `hoody agent bots resume`) brings them back under the account signed in now. Calling it again answers an empty list; a 503 means a Bot stays suspended for now, so retry.
- **Session turns and commands report a retired `Idempotency-Key`.** `agent.sessions.turns.start()` (with or without `stream: true`), `agent.sessions.turns.create()` and `turns.run()` answer 409 `turn_key_retired`, and `agent.sessions.commands.send()` and `commands.get()` answer 409 `command_key_retired`, when the key can no longer admit anything. `details.admitted` is `"false"` (send it under a new key) or `"unknown"` (check whether the earlier request took effect first). A 503 on these calls can now mean the session's turn or command record could not be read: honour `Retry-After` and retry with the same key, never a new one.
- **A workflow run refused with 503 may still have been admitted.** When the session's turn record cannot be read, `agent.workflows.start()` and `agent.sessions.startWorkflow()` answer 503 with `Retry-After`. Check `agent.workflows.runs.list()` (or the session's stream) before you run the workflow again.
- **Deleting a Bot that is being stopped answers 503 with `Retry-After`.** `agent.bots.delete()` on a Bot being stopped (for example while the agent shuts down) answers 503 `service_unavailable`: send it again after that many seconds. A delete repeated after one that went through answers 404 `not_found`, and a Bot store the agent cannot read or write answers 500 `store_failed`.
- **A memory write intent is spent by every wipe request that reaches the wipe.** `agent.memory.deleteProject()` now spends the intent from `agent.memory.createWriteIntent()` also when the wipe is refused because the realm no longer matches or the project is missing, so every retry needs a fresh intent. A request refused before that (a malformed realm, an unreadable body, 429, 403) leaves the intent valid.
- **Bot client keys starting with `chan:` are refused** with 400 on `agent.bots.messages.send()`, `agent.bots.messages.sendAudio()`, `stopDelegate()` and the compatible doors, as `compat:` already was.
- **Clearing events with no filter needs `all: true`.** `api.events.clear()` (CLI: `hoody events clear`) with none of `event_type`, `resource_type`, `resource_id` or `before_date` deleted every event in scope. It now answers 400 `INVALID_BULK_DELETE_PARAMS` unless `all: true` (`--all`) is sent. `realm_id` only narrows the scope and does not count as a filter.
- **Auth tokens can read events but not delete them.** `api.events.delete()`, `api.events.clear()` and `api.events.purge()` (CLI: `hoody events delete`, `clear`, `purge`) answer 403 "This endpoint requires first-party login (auth tokens are not allowed)." to an auth token. Sign in to delete events.
- **An auth token cannot change the account profile.** `api.users.update()` (CLI: `hoody users update`) answers 403 "Auth tokens cannot update the account profile. Sign in to change alias, public key or password." to any auth token.
- **A computer claim needs exec permission.** `api.computers.createClaim()` (CLI: `hoody computers claims create`) with an auth token needs `computers.actions.exec` as well as `computers.read`, since a program that accepts the claim may treat it as full access to the computer, shell included. Otherwise it answers 403 "This token does not have permission to authorize computers".
- **A project's member list needs a member-management permission.** `api.projects.get(id, { include_permissions: true })` (CLI: `hoody projects get --include-permissions`) with an auth token needs `projects.members.invite`, `remove` or `change_roles`, as the member list does. Otherwise it answers 403 "This token does not have permission to manage project members".
- **The project's proxy permission document is withheld from realm-scoped requests.** `api.computers.get()`, `list()` and `listByProject()` with `include_proxy_permissions` leave out `project_proxy_permissions` on a realm-scoped request and for an auth token restricted to realms, even when the project is in that realm, because that document opens computers in every realm. `api.projects.proxy.getPermissions()` answers 403 in the same cases. Read it on the base domain with unrestricted credentials.
- **An expired computer answers 409 `COMPUTER_EXPIRED`.** `api.computers.start()`, `stop()`, `restart()`, `pause()` and `resume()` on a computer whose server was terminated answered 403 `OPERATION_NOT_PERMITTED_ON_EXPIRED`, and `api.snapshots.create()`, `restore()`, `delete()` and `setAlias()` (CLI: `hoody snapshots create`, `restore`, `delete`, `alias set`) answered a 403 with no code. All of these now answer 409 `COMPUTER_EXPIRED` and do nothing; the snapshot calls check again right before they act, so a computer that expires mid-call is refused too. A computer under an access suspension answers 403 `ACCESS_SUSPENDED` where it answered `CONTAINER_ACCESS_SUSPENDED`.
- **A cron entry update cannot duplicate another entry.** `cron.entries.update()` (CLI: `hoody cron entries update`) answers 409 `ENTRY_EXISTS` when its new `schedule` or `command` makes it another managed entry's line, which made cron run the command twice; `details` gives that entry's id. An update that keeps the entry's own line is never refused. An update now collapses the whitespace in `schedule` as a create does.
- **A terminal wait can be for the next prompt.** `terminal.sessions.wait()` (CLI: `hoody terminal sessions wait`) takes `fresh: true` (`--fresh`), which ignores every match already on screen, or `after_seq` (`--after-seq`), which matches only rows changed after that `seq` (from `snapshot.seq` of a snapshot or of the previous wait). Both apply in `regex` and `either` modes, and the answer then carries `after_seq`. An `after_seq` that is not a non-negative integer or is larger than the terminal's current `seq`, an `after_seq` sent with `fresh: true`, or an `after_seq` in `stable` mode answers 400 `INVALID_AFTER_SEQ`; a `fresh` that is not a boolean, or `fresh: true` in `stable` mode, answers 400 `INVALID_FRESH`.
- **Terminal search matches a prompt at the end of its row.** `terminal.sessions.search()` (CLI: `hoody terminal sessions search`) and regex waits searched each row padded to the terminal width, so `>>> $` or `\$ $` never matched. A row now also ends after its last non-blank cell plus one blank; every hit that matched before still matches. `\C` is refused with 400 `INVALID_REGEX`.
- **Terminal command history reports status the way a command result does.** Each `terminal.commands.list()` entry (CLI: `hoody terminal commands list`) carries `status` (`queued`, `running`, `completed`, `timed_out`, `cancelled`), `exit_code`, `completion`, `timed_out`, `cancelled` and `started`, with the values a single command's result gives. `exit_code` is `null` while the command runs or waits and when its status is unknown, where it was `-1`; an aborted command reads `cancelled` where it read `completed`.
- **SQLite refuses a second name for an open database.** A hard link to a database the kit has open under another path answers 409 `CONFLICT` on every route that opens it, naming the open path, and nothing runs; so does a maintenance `restore` whose `src_path` is such a link. Writes through the second name could fail with 500 or run beside the first.
- **A KV rollback never overwrites another spelling of a key.** On a table whose key compares without case (`NOCASE`) or trailing spaces (`RTRIM`), `sqlite.kv.rollback()` and `sqlite.kv.table.rollback()` (CLI: `hoody kv rollback`, `hoody kv table rollback`) that would restore `key` while `Key` is stored answer 409 `CONFLICT` naming `conflicting_key`, and change nothing. They overwrote or deleted `Key`, or answered 500.
- **Time travel answers the moments before a write made with `history=false`.** `sqlite.kv.get()` with `at_timestamp`, the table snapshot, the diff and the table rollback answered 409 `TIME_TRAVEL_CHAIN_GAP` between a recorded write and the next unrecorded one. They now return the recorded value. A table rollback still refuses a key whose current value was not recorded.
- **`sqlite.sql.runTransaction()` cannot change the KV store's internal tables** (CLI: `hoody db transactions run`). An item that changes a row or the schema of a `<table>_history` table or a `hoody:*` table, directly or through a trigger, or that creates a trigger whose body names one, is rolled back with 400 `INVALID_PARAMETERS`; reading them still works. Setting `PRAGMA writable_schema` is refused with 400.
- **Run can search Docker Hub (off by default).** A new `oci-docker-hub` source (`source_type: oci-docker-hub-search`) offers Docker Official Images and verified publishers only, ranked below every other source. It runs `docker run --rm -i docker.io/<namespace>/<name>[:tag]`, pulls the image on first run and needs `docker`. Turn it on with `run.sources.update('oci-docker-hub', { enabled: true })` (CLI: `hoody run sources update oci-docker-hub --enabled`). It is in a new config only; add it to an existing one with `run.sources.create()`.
- **Run says when selector hints are ignored.** A selector with `channel`, `variant` or `publisher` gets the same candidates as without them, since no source filters or ranks on them yet. The answer now carries a `SELECTOR_HINT_IGNORED` warning (no `source_id`) naming the fields that were set.
- **Daemon webhooks for a port-range program name the instance.** Their payload carries `metadata.instance` (`<name>_<port>`) and `metadata.port` (typed on `WebhookPayload`), so each event is attributed to its own process.
- **Watch answers a malformed watcher id with a JSON error.** `watch.watchers.get()`, `update()`, `delete()` and `watch.events.list()` / `stream()` with an id that is not a UUID answer 400 `INVALID_REQUEST` with `{ code, message }`, where they answered a text/plain 400. An unknown path answers a JSON 404 `NOT_FOUND`, and an unsupported method a JSON 405 `METHOD_NOT_ALLOWED`.
- **A watch event for a growing file reports its size before the write.** A write and its close merged into one `modified` event that reported the size after the write in both `old_size_bytes` and `new_size_bytes`; the merged event now keeps the earliest `old_size_bytes`.
- **A files backend connect with wrong credentials no longer replaces working ones.** `files.backends.create*()` (CLI: `hoody files backends <type> create`) for types such as webdav, s3 and http saved the credentials without trying them, so a typo replaced the stored secret of a connected backend. A connect now proves them by listing the remote's root, as an update does: a failure is a 400 that saves nothing. While a mount of the backend is in use, a connect with other credentials answers 409.
- **A read from a remote backend that does not answer no longer hangs, and a remote that refuses is no longer a 404.** The lookup of a path on the remote is bounded at 30 seconds (504 `BACKEND_UNREACHABLE`); a refused connection, a TLS failure or rejected credentials answer 502 `BACKEND_UNREACHABLE`, and only a path the remote does not have is a 404. A stat with `backend` returns the metadata JSON, where it returned the file's content.
- **A mount with settings of its own can be started again after a restart of the files kit.** It is listed in status `error` with "the mount has settings of its own and was not started after the restart; update it to start it"; `files.mounts.update()` starts it and `files.mounts.delete()` removes it. Before, the restore refused it, an update could not start it, and a delete left its link behind, so a new mount at that path answered 409.
- **A second start of the files kit no longer disturbs the running one's mounts.** A start that cannot bind its port now fails before it restores or sweeps anything; it leaked an rclone mount and repointed live mount links. The 503 `MOUNT_STORAGE_UNAVAILABLE` message names the directory the storage check fails on, why, and how to correct it.
- **Bots can talk on Discord, Slack and Telegram through chat channels.** A channel connects a Bot to a bot account in one of those apps, so the people paired with it talk to the Bot there. Creating one checks the credentials with the chat app and stores them; they are never returned. Slack needs `token` and `app_token`; a Telegram bot with a webhook is refused 409 `channel_conflict` unless `delete_webhook` is true; an `Idempotency-Key` makes a retry return the same channel. With the default `dm_policy` `owner`, the answer carries the owner's enrolment code once, to send to the bot in a direct message. New credentials must be for the same bot account (else 409 `channel_identity_changed`). A delete answers 200, or 202 while the connection is still stopping. A pairing code carries rights (`chat`, `control`, `gates`); owner changes need a paired owner (else 409 `owner_needs_pairing`); a group chat is approved only with `acknowledge_group_readers: true`. A Bot has at most 8 channels (`max_channels`) and a channel at most 32 people (`max_principals`); past either limit the answer is 429 `channels_exhausted`.
- **Channel-wide Bot settings.** What every channel of a Bot inherits is read and set as one object: `channels_paused`, `hide_secrets`, `lang`, `max_channels`, `rate_bot_per_day` (0 = no limit, up to 3000), `shared_identity` (only `off`) and `unset`. A refused value answers 422 `setting_invalid` or `setting_conflict`, and nothing is applied.
- **Bots report their channels and whether a turn is running.** A full Bot record from `agent.bots.get()`, `create()`, `update()`, `setGuardrails()` and `list()` (not a locked list row), and the `state` event of `agent.bots.streamLogs()`, carries `channels` (`id`, `kind`, `label`, `state`, `state_reason`, `paired_count`; `[]` when none) and `turn_running`. Log rows from a chat channel carry `sender.via` `telegram`, `slack` or `discord`.
- **A Bot's `[limit]`, `[provider]` and `[error]` log rows name the messages of their turn.** They carry `message_ids` as a reply does, so a message whose turn ended in such a row can be matched to it; it may be the only row that turn writes.
- **SQLite keeps the latest commits of a database file that is renamed or replaced while open.** They stayed in the `-wal` file beside the old name, so the file opened without them under its new name, and a database put at the old name later read that `-wal` as its own. Before it closes a database, the kit now copies the `-wal` into the file wherever it is now. If that cannot finish (another program holds a read transaction on the moved file, or an I/O error), the kit logs the path and retries in the background; until it succeeds, opening, creating or deleting a database at the old path, opening the moved file, and restoring from either path answer 503 `DATABASE_BUSY` and change nothing. If the last try at shutdown fails too, the commits stay in the `-wal`: before opening anything at that path again, rename that `-wal` to the moved file's name plus `-wal`.
- **SQLite history writes no longer slow down as a key's history grows**, and op numbers, history keys and every history, time-travel and rollback answer are unchanged. Directory-mode LIST lists every key that exists for the whole request and its `total` matches the items. A user table named like one of SQLite's metadata functions (`pragma_table_info` and the like) no longer breaks KV reads and writes, change feeds, `VACUUM` or query history on that database. The Studio's SQL and JSON editors are bundled, so it loads no editor from another site and works offline.
- **Terminal text is always valid UTF-8.** Output that decodes to a code point above U+10FFFF is shown as U+FFFD in `terminal.sessions.getSnapshot()`, `search()` and `wait()`, where it produced bytes no UTF-8 reader accepts and made searches miss that row. A pattern that can match an empty string now steps over a whole character, so it no longer returns a match that starts inside a multibyte character.
- **The daemon no longer writes program environment values to its logs**, only their names, and its own log files in its storage directory are owned by root and closed to other users (a daemon log an older launch left in another directory is not changed). Logs written before this release can still hold those values: delete them, and change any secret they held. At boot, programs that share an id with another are not started (logged once; the other programs still start), and a relative `storage.config_path` is refused at startup. A sandboxed program whose start fails because its display is not ready is quarantined, as at boot, instead of being left configured to run outside its sandbox. A present but empty `ingress_rate_limit` counts as an ingress policy when a program's firewall rules are restored.
- `hoody shell --help` and the SDK reference say what one-shot output is: the command's exact bytes, stdout and stderr apart, with its exit code; terminal text with `--terminal-id`, `--shell tmux` or the SSH bridge.
- The agent skills describe `hoody files exists` and the `notes comments create` block-anchor example.
- The terminal `desktop` option is described as it behaves: `desktop: true` shows the whole display as one screen and starts no window manager or desktop environment; set `desktop_env` (`xfce`, `mate`) for one. The display "no window manager" help lists what still works.
- Each kit's API reference names the kit's public URL as its server.
- The SQLite reference states the exact-fit rule for `values` and `valuesBatch` and each `SQL_BIND_ERROR` case; the agent reference says when key changes reach running sessions; the `files.append()` reference documents its 400 and 413 refusals; the proxy logs reference describes its 503 answers in user terms.
- The agent reference describes 403 `forbidden` as "Hoody Kit programs are reached through their URLs only." and its 413 answers in user terms.
- The watch reference lists the default `ignore_dirs` (`node_modules`, `.git`, `target`, `__pycache__`, `.hg`, `.svn`, `.cache`, `dist`, `.next`, `.nuxt`, `vendor`, `bower_components`): build-output folders such as `dist` are skipped unless you pass `[]` or your own list, and hidden folders are also skipped while `skip_hidden` is true.
- The API reference says what `timeout` means for `stop` and `restart`, and when a deleted computer's URLs stop.
- The firewall reference says how rules are evaluated: `drop` and `reject` apply before `allow`, and traffic no rule matches is allowed, so `allow` rules alone restrict nothing; to permit only some addresses, drop the rest.
- The curl reference documents session-id 400s with the messages the kit sends.
- The files reference tags every download and extraction operation and says that a mount create over the 100-mount limit answers 429 with no code.
- The agent skills in the package were checked against the current SDK, CLI and kit API descriptions and corrected where they differed, and no longer mention the bot kit.
- **`hoody files archives members extract` requires the member.** Without one it downloaded the whole archive as text. `hoody files archives members extract <archive> <member>` now exits 1 with the missing-argument error and sends nothing when the member is left out.
- **`-o wide` of `hoody daemon programs list` shows INSTANCES for a port-range program,** as running over total (`3/4`).
- **A kit's JSON answer is printed as sent.** A stored value, a file or a transparent curl answer that has `statusCode` and `data` or `message` keys is no longer unwrapped (`kv get`, `files get`, `curl run`).
- **A required body field is a required flag.** Where the server requires a field of a block it always receives, the CLI refuses the call before anything is sent (`agent fusions set`, `agent mcp upsert`, `bot-urls create`, `browser wait`, `notes comments anchor set`, `computers proxy hooks create`, `vm create`, and others). `agent definitions model set` needs `--model` and `agent definitions turns limit set` needs `--turns`.
- **`computers update` has no `--env` any more.** It replaced every variable; use `computers env update|set|delete`.
- **`browser restart` keeps the instance's settings.** It no longer sends defaults for the options you leave out.
- **`notifications list` draws the notifications as a table, and `notifications restore` says what it did.**
- **`hoody shell --shell tmux -- <command>` is refused (exit 2).** Run tmux as the command: `hoody shell <computer> -- tmux new -d -s <name> '<command>'`. `terminal.run({ shell: 'tmux' })` throws the same way.
- **`--terminal-id` must be a positive whole number.**
- **`db transactions run` needs `--transaction`.** The sqlite kit requires the transaction body; the call is refused before anything is sent.

### Fixed

- **`hoody auth tokens realms remove` says what it did.** It printed `Deleted successfully.`, though only the realm leaves the token and the token is kept; it now prints `Realm removed from the token. The token is kept.`
- **`hoody help` refuses a command that does not exist.** `hoody help bogus` printed the full help and `hoody help computers bogus` the help of `computers`, both with exit 0. Both now answer as `hoody bogus` and `hoody computers bogus` do: `error: unknown command 'bogus'` with a suggestion on stderr, exit 1. A word after a command that takes arguments is still ignored (`hoody help computers list extra` prints the help of `computers list`).
- **`hoody agent logs list` and `hoody agent logs export` read every log source again.** They sent the computer the command routes to (`-c`, `HOODY_COMPUTER` or the saved computer) as the proxy source's `computer` filter, so every other source, and no source at all, was refused with `computer does not apply to source ...`. The filter has its own flag now, `--filter-computer <id>`, used with `--source proxy`. `hoody completion` lists the flag.
- **`hoody agent prompt --wait` waits for the turn.** It gave up after the 30 s request timeout while the turn kept running. It now waits as long as the agent holds the request (up to 290 s, then the agent answers that the turn is still running), and on a timeout it names the command that finds the running turn.
- **Required nested fields are checked before anything is sent.** `hoody curl schedules create` needs `--request-url` and `hoody run search` needs `--app`; without them the commands now stop with a usage error, where they sent the request and printed the server's parse error (`missing field request`, `missing field selector`).
- **`hoody notes records list --filters` and `--sorts` take JSON documents.** Each takes one JSON array inline, from a file with `@<path>`, or from stdin with `@-`, and malformed JSON or a value that is not an array is refused before the request. A leading `@` used to be sent as text.
- **`hoody notes nodes create --attributes` says how to send an object.** A `KEY=VALUE` value of an untyped map is sent as text, so `--attributes fields={}` sent the string `"{}"`, which the server refused. A value that is a JSON object or array is now refused with the whole-object form to use (`--attributes '{"name":"Tasks","fields":{}}'`), and the help shows it.
- **`hoody realms list --include-usage` shows the counts on a terminal.** The terminal list adds PROJECTS, COMPUTERS, SERVERS and TOKENS columns; it printed the ids only, and the counts were in `-o json` alone.
- **`hoody referrals get` labels the Creator threshold as one.** The field that holds how many customers the Creator level needs read `Active customers for Creator`, next to your own counts; it reads `Customers needed for Creator` now, in `referrals get` and `referrals join`.
- **A reference example takes a flag's value from its description when the description gives one.** When a flag's description contains a backticked `name=value` pair for that flag, the generated example takes the first value in that pair (`Failed` from `state=Failed,Orphaned`), so `hoody vm list` is shown with `--state running` instead of a value the service refuses.
- **A text answer prints as written on a terminal.** A command whose answer is text, such as `hoody notifications metrics` or `hoody vm metrics` (the Prometheus text format), printed it on a terminal as a JSON string with its newlines escaped. It now prints the text itself. `-o json` still prints the JSON string; a pipe now gets the text too (see Fixed).
- **`--until-done` says when a command cannot wait.** On a command whose operation reports no completion event (for example `hoody vm create` and `hoody vm isos downloads start`), the help for `--until-done` now says that the command does not report completion, so the flag is not available there, and to check the resource's status with its `get` or `status` command. Using the flag is refused with the same words before anything is sent. The CLI reference and the agent skills no longer show the flag in such a command's example. Commands that can wait are unchanged.
- **The HTML reference names the SDK service on each service page.** A service page groups operations by their HTTP tag, which is not always the SDK service they live on, so each page now names the SDK service and lists each method (`box.vm.console.read()`). Two tags that differ only in case (`Agent` and `agent`) now share one page, where before one page overwrote the other. The TypeScript example on an operation page now calls the real method with its arguments in order (`box.vm.console.read('name')`), where before it showed a made-up `SDK.<namespace>.<operationId>()` call. A byte body is passed as bytes (`box.vm.console.write('name', new Uint8Array(/* bytes */))`), a text body as a string and a form body as a `FormData`, as the method's signature takes them. A parameter limited to fixed values takes one of them. An operation whose body shape the signature does not pin down (a choice of shapes, or any JSON value) has no TypeScript example. A page names a method only when one was generated from that exact operation (same id, HTTP verb and path), so the `PATCH` alias of a `PUT` route no longer shows the `PUT` method, and an operation with no method of its own has no TypeScript example.
- **Event streams without a resume cursor no longer say they can resume.** The generated source comment of an event-stream method mentions resuming with `{ since }` only when the route takes a resume cursor.
- **Tunnels with more than one socket (`maxConnections` above 1) keep each stream on one socket until it ends.** A stream was released from its socket when the visitor's side of it ended, so the rest of the response went out on the main socket while its first part had gone on another, and the kit could read them out of order. It now stays on the socket that opened it until this side sends its end or a reset, or a reset comes in. Pairs with the hoody-tunnel kit change, which keeps each stream's frames on one socket in the other direction.
- **A command answered with no body prints nothing instead of `null`.** After a `204` or `205` answer or a `HEAD` request without headers to show, commands such as `hoody notes notebooks delete`, `hoody notes notebooks export --dry-run true` and `hoody files get` printed `null` on standard output. They now print nothing; the exit code and standard error are unchanged. A `200` answer whose `data` is `null` still prints `null`, and `-o json` still prints the valid document `null` in every case.
- **`hoody pipe connect` and `hoody pipe forward --ws` report a refused connection under Bun 1.4.4.** When the server refused the WebSocket (403, 409 or 429), Bun 1.4.4 emitted no further event, so the command waited, then exited 0 with no output. The SDK now ends the socket itself and the command fails with `Error: … refused: <reason>`, as on earlier Bun versions and on Node.
- **`hoody shell` no longer leaves stale text on screen after a dialog closes.** While attached, the CLI turned on raw input but left the terminal's output processing on, so each line feed from the remote app also moved the cursor to column 1 and the screen was redrawn in the wrong place. Output processing is now off while attached and restored on detach, on errors and on Ctrl+C/SIGTERM/SIGHUP. Windows and non-terminal output are unchanged.
- **`hoody terminal commands get` and `cancel` take `--terminal-id <n>`.** A command belongs to the terminal it ran on, and both commands always reached terminal 1, so a command started with `hoody terminal commands run --terminal-id 7` could not be read or stopped: the answer was 404 `COMMAND_NOT_FOUND`. Pass the number `commands run` used, or `0` for an ephemeral command. Without the flag both read terminal 1 as before. `hoody completion` lists the flag.
- **`hoody cron runs list` says that run history is kept on disk.** Its help said the history was kept in memory and a restart emptied it. The cron service keeps the runs still running and, for each entry, its last 50 ended runs of the last 30 days on disk; only when it cannot use the disk does it keep them in memory, and `GET /api/v1/cron/info` then reports `history_persisted: false`.
- **A save through the encrypted local store no longer pairs a new API address with the old token.** A sign-in or `hoody config set` under a local lock wrote `config.json` (which holds `tokenOrigin` and `baseUrl` in plain text) and then the encrypted lock file. When the lock write failed, for example on a full disk, `config.json` named the new API while the lock still held the old token, and the next command sent the old token to the new API. Both files are now written in full before either replaces the old one, and a failed save leaves both as they were. A save that stops between the two (the process is killed, or the old `config.json` cannot be put back) is undone by the next `hoody` command before it reads the token; until it is, the token is not used.
- **`hoody local lock enable` no longer stops every later command when its password step fails.** It wrote a backup of `config.json` before it asked for the lock password. When that step failed (for example two different entries at the prompt, no terminal, `--non-interactive` with no password source, two password sources, or a missing `--local-password-file`), the backup stayed, and every command except `hoody local lock` then stopped with exit 12 (mixed lock state). The backup is now written after the password step, just before the lock.
- **The local lock's policy for credentials from flags and the environment also covers a split sign-in.** With the policy on, a command that took the username from `-u` and the password from `HOODY_PASSWORD` (or `HOODY_USERNAME` with `-p`) was not refused and signed in. It now stops with exit 14, as it does for two flags or two variables, unless you add `--allow-ephemeral-token`.
- **The help of `hoody local lock ephemeral enable` and `disable` describes the policy the right way round.** It said that `enable` stops commands from using the saved credentials. `enable` refuses a token, or a username and a password, given by a flag or the environment: the command opens the lock to read the policy, then stops with exit 14 unless you add `--allow-ephemeral-token`. `disable` accepts them again. Commands given none of them are not affected. A new lock starts with the policy on.
- **The agent skills carry no emoji.** Warning signs read `Warning:`, the onboarding skill no longer puts an icon before each program and tells the agent to use no emoji in the message it sends, and the skills listing marks a large file with `(large)`.
- **The Windows installer (`install.ps1`) adds its folder to your PATH.** It adds `%USERPROFILE%\.hoody\bin` (or `HOODY_INSTALL_DIR`) to your user PATH once, keeps entries such as `%JAVA_HOME%\bin` as written, and the PowerShell window that ran it finds `hoody` at once; other open terminals need to be reopened. It no longer prints a PATH command that froze such entries. It also puts the previous `hoody.exe` back when it is stopped during an upgrade, removes the `hoody.exe.old-*` copies earlier upgrades left, gives up on the version lookup after 30 seconds and says why a lookup or download failed, asks the `hoody.exe` it replaces (not the first `hoody` on PATH) before it refuses a downgrade, tells a signature verifier that could not run from a signature that does not verify, and refuses PowerShell before 5.1 and hosts other than Windows.
- **`hoody notes nodes create` says which attribute is wrong.** With the updated Notes kit, a create whose attributes do not fit the type (a page sent with `title` in place of `name`, a database without `fields`) answers `400` `validation_error` with each failing field in `details`, where it answered `500`. Its `--type`, `--parent-id` and `--attributes` flags now have help text, and `--parent-id` says that a page can go under the notebook's Home section.
- **`hoody agent bots voice connect` reaches the agent of the selected computer.** It talked to the agent through an account client that was not scoped to a computer, so every call was refused before anything was sent. It now looks up the selected computer and talks to that computer's agent, with the kit credential from `--kit-auth` (or the other kit flags) when one is set. With no computer selected, it exits 2 and says to select one.
- **`hoody browser screenshots capture` works again without `--mask`.** The CLI sent `maskColor=#FF00FF` on every capture, and the browser kit refuses `maskColor` without `mask` (`400 VALIDATION_ERROR`), so every capture failed. It now sends `maskColor` only when you pass `--mask-color`; the kit still uses `#FF00FF` for `--mask` boxes by default.
- **`startLocalExit` sends the egress credential on its startup exit-IP check.** The check's CONNECT carried no credential, so a computer whose egress permission rule asks for one refused it and the local exit did not start. It now carries the `kitAuth` for `egress` in a header; a token rule that reads a query parameter still needs `verify: false` (`--no-verify`).
- **`code.ui.getManifest()` is typed.** The spec declares its answer as `application/manifest+json`, and the SDK typed only `application/json` bodies, so the method returned `unknown`. Types now follow any JSON media type the client reads: `application/json` or a `+json` type, whatever its parameters or case.
- **`notes.files.upload()` names a last answer that is not JSON.** A `200` under another content type, such as a proxy's HTML page, was reported as a missing `uploadId`. The error now gives the status and the content type.
- **`hoody` commands that only work on the base host no longer fail with 403 from a realm.** With a saved realm or `--realm`, these commands were sent to the realm's host: `hoody auth sessions list`, `revoke` and the other session commands, `hoody auth email change start` and `cancel`, `hoody inbox markread`, `hoody servers stats`, `hoody users setup retry`, `hoody wallet githubbonus status` and `claim`, every `hoody support` command, and the step that claims your free server after you sign up or sign in. They now go to the base host, and `--realm` has no effect on them.
- **`mount()` refuses a token sent as a query parameter.** A `token` credential with `param` was sent as `Authorization: Bearer`, which a query-only permission rule does not read. rclone cannot add a query parameter to every WebDAV request, so `mount()`, its probe and `buildAuthDelivery()` now refuse it by name before anything is sent.
- **`hoody unmount` signals rclone only while the recorded pid is still that rclone.** The pid is recorded with its start time. If the start time differs, or the process is not the rclone this mount started (`rclone mount hoody: <path>` with the mount's own config file), the pid is not signalled, so after rclone died a process that was later given its pid is left alone. The check runs again right before each signal, after the wait for uploads and the unmount.
- **A mount that is still starting can no longer be taken over or pruned.** A background mount waiting for its daemon is recorded as owned by the process starting it. A second `hoody mount` of the same path is refused, where before it took the record and its failure unmounted the first mount. `hoody mount --prune` keeps such a record, and `hoody unmount` refuses it while the process starting it runs. That process writes the record again only while it is still its own claim; if the record was removed meanwhile, it stops its rclone and says so. Claiming, reclaiming and pruning hold one lock file. A path that is a mount point already is refused, and a mount that fails removes only its own record and unmounts only what its own rclone mounted.
- **`hoody mount` reports an rclone that cannot be started** (for example, not executable) as an error. It used to crash and leave the record and its 0600 config behind. A failure to remove the record of a failed mount no longer replaces the mount's own error.
- **`hoody mount --background` succeeds only with a daemon pid.** When the daemon process cannot be found, the mount is unmounted and reported, so it never succeeds with no pid that unmount could stop later. Only the rclone this mount started counts as its daemon, so a wrapper such as `sudo hoody mount …` is not taken for a second one. On macOS a daemon started through an `rclonePath` with another name is found too. The daemon of a path with spaces is now found; before, the search took 15 seconds longer and recorded no pid.
- **`hoody agent realms use` takes the realm as `--id`.** `--active-realm-id` exits 2 and names `--id`.
- **`formatEd25519SshPublicKey` and `parseEd25519SshPublicKey` refuse a line break inside a key line.** A comment with a line break in it made `formatEd25519SshPublicKey` (and `generateEd25519SshKeyPair`) return two lines, which `authorized_keys` reads as two entries, and `parseEd25519SshPublicKey` read a second line as the comment. Both now throw. Line breaks around the line or the comment are still trimmed.
- **The curl channel holds at most 64 MiB of unread response bytes per stream.** When a reader falls that far behind, the stream fails with a `CurlChannelError` whose `errorType` is `"body_buffer_full"` and its request is cancelled; before, unread bytes were kept without a limit. Pass `maxBufferedBodyBytes` to `CurlChannel.connect()` for another cap; `client.curl.channel.connect()` uses the default. A fetch through the channel (`createCurlFetch()`) stops waiting as soon as its signal aborts, also while the channel opens and while the request body is read or compressed. A 204, 205 or 304 answer no longer throws while the response is built; it and a `HEAD` answer have a null body. `Response.url` is the address after redirects, or the request's own address when the relay gives none. A request body that starts with a UTF-8 byte-order mark is sent with it.
- **`hoody login --token` exits 3 when the token is accepted but cannot be saved.** It exited 1 there; the password, two-factor and browser sign-ins already exit 3. It also prints the warning the other sign-ins print when `HOODY_TOKEN` or `HOODY_API_TOKEN` is set, because later commands send that variable's key in place of the saved sign-in. The help of `--realm` names `global`.
- **The `h` key of `hoody computers urls list --pick` shows the kit credential only.** A header from the config's `headers` whose name looked like a credential was shown as `$HOODY_KIT_TOKEN`, and a kit token under a header name saved in the config could be left out. The preview now reads the client's own kit credential.
- **`cli/CLI_AUTHENTICATION.md` describes `hoody logout`, `hoody auth accesstoken get` and `--realm global`.**
- **`hoody agent prompt` refuses `--model`, `--agent` and `--cwd` on an existing session.** The three flags apply when a session is created. With `--session-id`, or with `--continue` when a session exists, they were dropped without a word and the turn ran on the session's own settings. The command now says so and exits 2 before it sends the turn.
- **`hoody agent prompt` on a busy session waits for its own turn.** When the turn queued behind a running one, the command ended at that turn's end: it exited 0 before its own reply, or failed because of the other turn's error. It now ends at the turn the agent named for it. A turn the agent drops before it runs (queued behind a turn that waits on a confirmation) ends the command with exit 1 and a sentence that says so. Generated stream commands follow the same rule.
- **`hoody agent login` reports a reply it cannot fully read.** A reply whose realm list or missing-permission list held an entry that is not text made the command fail after the token was already installed. Such entries are now left out.
- **A token that already starts with `Bearer ` is sent once.** `--kit-token "Bearer <value>"`, a `kitAuth` token or JWT with the scheme on it, and an account token given that way (`--token "Bearer hdy_..."`, `HOODY_TOKEN`) went out as `Authorization: Bearer Bearer <value>`. The CLI and the SDK's kit, WebSocket and terminal requests now send such a value as given.
- **The browser sign-in refuses an answer that is not a device answer.** A code or page address that is not text ended `hoody login` with an internal error or was drawn as `[object Object]`. The command now says that browser login is unavailable and exits 1.
- **`hoody computers stats` and `hoody projects stats` now show the readable usage figures in the standalone binary too.** Before, only the npm install showed them: the binary printed the answer without `metrics_human`, with no message.
- **`hoody hub` says why the saved sign-in could not be used.** Every failure read as "signed out". An account that needs a two-factor code is now sent to `hoody login`, a server that did not answer says to try again, and a certificate that was not accepted is named as such. Only a refused sign-in reads as signed out.
- **`hoody auth refresh` prints one instruction when the renewal got no answer.** It printed "try again in a minute" and then told you to run `hoody login`. It now prints only the first.

- **`hoody unmount` right after saving a file no longer loses it.** A file closed less than about a second before the unmount was never uploaded, and the unmount still said "unmounted". Unmount, Ctrl-C on a foreground `hoody mount` and the SDK's `unmount()` functions now wait while files are still uploading. When none finishes within a minute, they fail with `PendingUploadsError`, keep the mount so the uploads can finish, and you unmount again later.
