A single file that takes longer than a minute to upload therefore needs another unmount, or `--force`. Files that were not sent (after `--force`, or when the upload process had stopped) are named, with the mount that uploads them; `UnmountError.failures[]` says whether each path is still mounted (`mounted`).
- **Ctrl+C on `hoody agent prompt` cancels the turn.** It exited at once and the agent went on running the turn you stopped. Now Ctrl+C (or SIGTERM) prints `[cancelling turn… press Ctrl+C again to exit without waiting]`, asks the agent to cancel (waiting at most 3 seconds), then prints `[turn cancelled]` or `[no turn was running]` and exits 130 (143 for SIGTERM). When the cancel fails or does not answer in time, or you press Ctrl+C a second time, it says the turn may still be running and gives the command that cancels it: `hoody agent sessions turns cancel --id <session id>`. This holds for streamed output and `--wait`.
- **Interrupting a CLI write tells you it may still complete.** Ctrl+C during a command that changes something (any request other than a read, streamed writes included) exited silently, while the server could still finish the work. It now prints "Interrupted: hoody <command> was already sent, so the server may still complete it. Check before running it again." and exits 128 + the signal number. Reads still exit silently.
- **Uploads that are cut off on the way no longer replace the file.** `box.files.upload()` (Node and browser) and `hoody files upload` did not send the body's length, so the files kit could not tell an upload cut short from a complete one. They now send `X-Expected-Length` whenever the length is known (bytes, `ArrayBuffer`, `Blob`, the CLI's buffered body); a value you set yourself is sent as given, and a stream of unknown length is unchanged. A cut upload is refused with 400 `INVALID_PARAMETER` ("Upload body incomplete …"), an `ApiError` in the SDK and exit 1 in the CLI, and the file keeps its previous content.
- **A pipe transfer cut short is no longer seen as complete.** `hoody pipe send <file>` streamed a regular file without a length, so when the sender stopped mid-transfer the receiver got a clean end. It now declares the file's size, and `PipeStream.send()` declares the length of a string or byte body (or your `contentLength`), so a cut transfer fails. FIFOs, empty files, stdin and streams of unknown length go out as before.
- **`hoody pipe send /name` meets a receiver waiting on `name`.** A leading slash, the form the CLI help shows, put the CLI on a different pipe than the SDK, so the sender waited for a receiver that was already waiting. `PipeStream`, `PipeMedia` and the CLI's pipe pages now drop leading slashes, as the generated SDK already did.
- **A `Blob` body is sent with the right Content-Type.** `notes.files.uploads.writeChunk()` with a `Blob` went out with no Content-Type and the notes kit answered 415; the same held for every method that sends raw bytes. A `Blob` now goes out with the type you set in the header or the `contentType` option, else its own type when the operation accepts it, else the operation's declared type. `FormData` is unchanged.
- **`hoody files mounts delete` works without `--uploads wait`.** The CLI always sent `wait_seconds=120`, which the files kit accepts only together with `uploads=wait`, so every other unmount failed with 400 `INVALID_PARAMETER`. `--wait-seconds` is now sent only when you give it.
- **`hoody files exists` prints the path's details.** It printed `null` on success. It now shows size, type, modification time and ETag (`-o json` gives every header, credential headers masked) and still exits 1 when the path does not exist. The read flags that only apply to `files get` are no longer offered; for revisions use `hoody files get <path> --history`.
- **`hoody notes comments create --anchor-type block --anchor-block-id <id>` pins the comment to the block.** `--anchor-block-id` filled an older top-level field and the notes kit answered 400. The flag now sends the anchor's block id.
- **`hoody mount` no longer puts credentials on a command line.** A mount password reached `rclone obscure` as an argument, and some header credentials (a token sent in a header other than `Authorization`, a computer claim) were passed to rclone as arguments, where other users of the machine could read them. The password now goes to rclone on standard input and those headers go into the mount's private rclone config (see Breaking for the rclone versions this needs). Mount errors no longer repeat a credential or the server's reason phrase.
- **`hoody mount` on Windows creates only the folder above the mount point**, and refuses a folder that already exists there, since the mount creates it. `hoody unmount <16-hex id>` unmounts the mount with that id, and otherwise reads the argument as a path.
- **Calls through `withRealm()` keep the base URL's path.** With a client whose base URL has a path (`https://example.com/proxy`), a realm-scoped account API call went to `https://<realm>.example.com/api/v1/...` and dropped `/proxy`. Only the host changes for a realm now.
- **Aborting a call while it waits to retry rejects at once.** An abort during a back-off, a server's `Retry-After` or an `onError` retry took effect only after the wait. The call now rejects with `ABORTED` at once, through the error middleware like any abort, in Node and in the browser.
- **`agent.bots.ask()` does not take a cut turn's marker for the reply.** When the Bot's turn was cut off, `ask()` could return the `[response interrupted: …]` row as the reply. It now returns `reply: null` with `turnState` `interrupted` (the agent restarted) or `cancelled`; the row is still in `rows` and `onRow`.
- **`hoody agent prompt -o pretty` prints each thought once.** Thinking text is buffered and printed as one dimmed line before the next tool line or reply, at the end of the stream and when you press Ctrl-C. Standard output still carries only the reply.
- **The CLI never shortens a URL.** The "You're in!" screen, the welcome-back card and the `hoody --help` banner print every URL whole, on its own line where it does not fit, so clicking or copying it gets the full URL; card rows wrap at word boundaries instead of cutting text. Table columns holding URLs are never narrowed.
- **`hoody exec openapi generate` and `merge` print the document.** They printed "completed successfully." and dropped it. The default output is now the OpenAPI document as JSON; `-o json` and `-o yaml` still print the whole answer. Their help and the `hoody exec sdks` help spell out "OpenAPI".
- **`hoody proxy logs stream` reports missing entries.** A `gap` event (the server no longer holds entries after your position) prints one notice naming the ids it skipped, and a run of `purged` events one line such as "3 log entries (id=10 to id=12) were deleted on the server", also with `-q`. They were printed as log lines. With `-o json`, `ndjson` or `raw` each event is one line with its `event` name.
- **`hoody files backends get`, `update`, `delete` and `test` call their argument "Backend ID"** (it said "File ID"), and `hoody files backends list` with no backend says how to connect one.
- **The CLI judges a full URL the same in any spelling.** A request path written as a full URL with an upper-case scheme or a leading space was treated as a path in one place and as a URL in another, so a kit token could be sent as `Authorization` to a URL outside the API. Every spelling now gets the same rule, and the destination is unchanged.
- **An error printed through a pipe shows the server's message, not its JSON.** When a service answers an error as `{"error":{"code":…,"message":…}}`, plain output (a pipe, CI, agent mode) printed `HTTP 404:` and the raw JSON, followed by a generic hint. It now prints the message, shows the code beside the status (`Error [404 NOT_FOUND]`), and leaves the generic hint out when the service sent a code and a message of its own. Output on a terminal already showed the message. The exit code is unchanged.
- **A status hint no longer contradicts the server.** A 409 whose answer says what happened (`No upstream lease with this id is in force`) is no longer followed by "A conflicting resource already exists". A 401 about a missing export ticket (`hoody notes document get --format html`) no longer tells you to sign in again. The 404 hint says to check each ID in the command, where it said "ID/name" on commands that take no name. Signing in with `-u`/`-p` and a password too short to belong to any account says the username or password is invalid, where it pointed at `--help`; `hoody login` answers `Invalid email or password.` in that case.
- **A validation error lists what was refused.** When the answer carries its issues as a list (`details` or `errors`), the CLI prints one line per issue under `Details:` (`/fields/f1: expected object, received string`), up to ten, where it printed only "One or more fields are invalid."
- **`hoody curl run` reports the requested URL's status as that URL's answer.** A 404 or 500 from the URL you asked for was printed as a failed Hoody request, with a hint that the curl route was not found or that Hoody had an outage. It now says `The requested URL answered HTTP 404 Not Found`, followed by the body. The exit code is still 1. A refusal by the curl service itself (an invalid URL, a full queue) reads as before.
- **The error object of `hoody support create` and `reply` carries the server's message.** Under `-o json` and `-o yaml`, a refusal in the nested envelope (`{"error":{"code","message"}}`) printed `"message": "HTTP 404: "`; it now prints the server's sentence, and the stderr line reads `✗ NOT_FOUND: <message> (HTTP 404)`. `hoody login`, `hoody signup` and `hoody screenshot display` print the server's sentence on stderr the same way.
- **An error about a positional argument names the argument.** `hoody code stop abc` said `--id must be a number`, a flag the command does not have. It now says `<id> must be a number`.
- **A computer name works wherever a computer is taken.** `-c <name>`, `HOODY_COMPUTER=<name>` and the computer argument of `hoody shell` and `hoody open` take a computer's exact name, as `hoody computers get <name>` does. They sent the name where the API wants an id and got a 422. A name that matches no computer, or several, is refused with the ids to pick from.
- **`hoody shell <computer> -i -- <command>` passes piped input to the command.** `echo hello | hoody shell <computer> -i -- cat` prints `hello`; there was no way to send it. Forwarding is opt-in with `-i` / `--stdin`: without it standard input is never read, so `while read id; do hoody shell "$id" -- uptime; done < ids.txt` keeps its file and a caller whose standard input stays open (ssh, an agent harness) is never kept waiting. When input was piped without `-i`, one stderr line says it was not forwarded (not in agent mode, in CI or with `-q`). With `-i`, up to 4 MiB is forwarded, and more is refused with exit 2; the input is read to its end before the command starts. With `--terminal-id`, `--shell tmux` or the SSH bridge no input is forwarded. `-i` together with `--local-password-stdin` or `--local-password-fd 0` is refused with exit 2 before either reads, since both would read standard input.
- **`-o yaml` reads back as the data it printed.** A string such as `- option`, `---` or `.inf` was printed unquoted and read back as a list, nothing or a number, and a key such as `a: b`, `null` or `00` broke the document or read back as another key. These are now double-quoted; everything else prints as before. This covers `hoody exec <script> -o yaml` too.
- **`hoody mount <computer>:/<path>` takes any computer name.** `dev:/data` and `build_box:/data` were refused: the target had to be 4 to 64 letters, digits and hyphens. A target is now a computer id or a name as the API allows it (3 to 100 letters, digits, `-` and `_`), with an optional `:/<path>`; a local path, a `host:port` and a malformed URL are still refused.
- **`hoody open` takes every kit that `hoody kits list` prints.** `daemon`, `tunnel` and `vm` and the aliases `n`, `note`, `tun` and `proxy-logs` were refused as unknown services. `hoody open run` names the command that takes `--app` (`hoody run open --app <name>`), `hoody open http abc` names `abc` (it said `NaN`), and terminal numbers 4501 to 5000, which are agent sessions, are refused before a URL is printed.
- **`hoody exec <script>` sees a script right after `hoody exec scripts write`, `move` or `delete`.** The script list was kept for five minutes, so a new script was an unknown command and a deleted one answered 404.
- **`hoody exec <script> -o yaml` prints YAML,** and `-o json` prints `null` for a script that returns null. YAML printed as JSON, and a null result printed nothing.
- **`hoody pipe status -o yaml` and `hoody pipe health -o yaml` print YAML.** They printed the text line and the raw JSON.
- **An answer that holds a list shows its rows on a terminal.** `hoody files get <directory>`, `hoody files search` and `hoody files get <file> --history` printed only the number of entries, paths or revisions on a terminal. They now print the rows as a table under the summary, as every command whose answer holds a list of records does.
- **`hoody use <realm> <computer-id>` checks that the computer is in the realm.** A computer of another realm was saved with it, and commands then failed with a 403. The pair is now refused with exit 1 and nothing is saved.
- **`-q` silences the success line of `hoody config set`, `hoody config clear` and `hoody use`.**
- **`exec logs get` prints the log lines, and fifteen other exec reads (`exec store get`, `exec routes list|resolve|test`, the `exec scripts … validate` checks, …) print their answer instead of a bare success line.** In a pipe they printed only "completed successfully." and dropped what they read. `exec logs get` now prints one log line per line (the count goes to standard error); the others print the response as JSON, the same document `-o json` prints. `-o json` and `-o yaml` are unchanged. Like the other reads, `hoody exec routes list` and `hoody exec scripts tree get` now take `--watch` to poll.
- **`hoody daemon ephemeralprograms list` names the command that starts one.** With no program it pointed at `hoody daemon programs create`; it now points at `hoody daemon ephemeralprograms start`. `-o wide` adds the ID column that `status`, `logs get` and `stop` take.
- **In a pipe, a text answer prints its text.** `hoody files get notes.txt > copy`, `hoody notes document get --format md | …`, `hoody browser text get` and the `metrics` commands wrote a JSON-quoted string. They now write the text byte for byte, so `> copy` is the file (no newline is added; on a terminal one is added when the text has none, as `-o raw` does); `-o json` and `-o yaml` still print the JSON string.
- **A whole-array document gets the checks KEY=VALUE entries get.** `hoody kv batch set --items '[{"key":"k","value":"v","bogus":"1"}]'` was sent and the server dropped `bogus` without a word, and a value of the wrong JSON type came back as a bare "Invalid JSON body". An undeclared field, a missing required one, a value outside a closed list or of the wrong JSON type is now refused before anything is sent (exit 1), naming the entry and the field. Values keep their JSON types: nothing is converted.
- **A script's own error is not called transient.** When a script run with `hoody exec <script>` throws or times out, the CLI printed "This is likely transient. Retry in a few seconds"; the same call fails the same way every time. It now prints the kit's sentence (`VM Error in script 'x.ts': Error: boom`) alone, and agent mode no longer advises a retry.
- **`hoody daemon programs connect`, `hoody share` and `hoody mount` take a computer name.** Given `-c <name>` (or a name as the mount target), they looked the name up as an id and failed; they now resolve it to the computer's id first, like the other kit commands.
- **`hoody agent prompt --wait` exits 1 when the turn was cancelled.** It exited 0, so a script's next step ran. A turn that ended with `/exit` still exits 0.
- **`-o ndjson` works for sign-in and downloads.** `hoody login`, `hoody signup`, `hoody auth refresh` and the other sign-in commands print the answer as NDJSON records instead of the human summary, and never prompt. A download command refuses to print bytes under `-o ndjson` and, with `--out-file`, prints its summary as one record.
- **`hoody shell -i` refuses every spelling of `--local-password-fd 0`.** `00`, `0x0` or `0.0` name standard input to the password reader, but the check that refuses `-i` beside it matched only `0`, so the command was sent with empty input. It now exits 2, as it does for `0`.
- **The agent help of `hoody agent prompt` names `--gate-policy auto_approve`.** It still described the retired `-y`, which the command refuses.
- **`hoody kv list --at-timestamp` lists every key.** The listing at a past time pages by offset only, and the CLI stopped after the first page of up to 1000 keys. It now reads every page, and the answer's `count` is the number of keys listed.
- **A KEY=VALUE map keeps a key named `__proto__`.** The entry was dropped from the request.
- **Two `hoody exec` flag helps describe what the computer does.** `hoody exec packages pin --packages` offered a version spec (`axios@1.4.0`), which the computer refuses: it takes dependency names, replaces a declared range with the installed version when that version satisfies it and a dist-tag (`latest`) with the installed version, leaves an exact version as it is, and reports under `unpinnable` a name that is not declared or not installed, a range the installed version does not satisfy and a dependency not from the registry. `hoody exec logs search --files` said a file name must not hold a path, but the names `hoody exec logs list` prints (`access/access.log`) are the ones it takes, and its help now says which files are searched without it.
- **`hoody snapshots list` prints the whole snapshot name in a pipe and in agent mode.** It was cut at 26 characters, so the name could not be passed back to `hoody snapshots get`.
- **`hoody exec templates generate` prints generated code of several lines under its label, one indented line each,** instead of one line with `\n` escapes.
- **`-o ndjson` on a command that answers one item prints the whole item on one line.** It printed the elements of the item's one list field, or nothing.
- **Confirmation questions on a terminal say what the command does.** They name the matching egress or ingress rules, every program rule of a group, every hook of a service, auto-renew on or off, the other sessions, events older than N days, the backup codes, the amount of a transfer or payment and the array element removed.
- **`hoody exec scripts types validate`, `returntype validate` and `dependencies validate` exit 1 when the result is invalid.** The answer is still printed.
- **`hoody exec logs clear`, `hoody exec cache clear` and the magic-comment dry runs no longer print a success line when nothing changed.**
- **`hoody exec sdks import` prints `SDK imported.`** It printed `Sdk imported.`.
- **`--help` lists the values a flag with a short fixed set of values accepts** (`one of: a, b`).
- **`hoody browser viewport set --viewport` takes only `null`.** Other text is refused before anything is sent; the size goes in `--viewport-width` and `--viewport-height`.
- **`hoody vm stop` shows its `<name>` as required in the usage line.**
- **A mistyped command before `--help` is refused.** `hoody computers bogus --help` printed the help of `hoody computers` and exited 0; it now exits 1 with the same unknown-command error as `hoody computers bogus`.
- **`hoody pipe url get -o json` prints the URL as a JSON string.** `-o yaml` and the pipe output keep the bare URL.
- **`hoody files touch -o json`, `-o yaml` and `-o ndjson` print `null` when the file is created,** as they do when it already exists. They printed an empty string.
- **API commands take a computer name for `--computer`,** as `hoody computers get <name>` does: `hoody snapshots list --computer my-box`. They sent the name where the API wants an id and got a 422.
- **Clearer error text:** the waiting states of `hoody auth device poll` are sentences, a 502 that names the upstream status says whether a retry can help, a refused agent stream shows the server's message, `hoody pipe cheatsheet get` names itself and prints an HTML or long error page as one line, a 404 `session not found` from `hoody agent prompt` names the attach command, and a 401 on a terminal names `HOODY_TOKEN` or `--token` when one of them supplied the token.
- **A kit answer is never read as an envelope.** Only the account API answers `{ statusCode, message, data }`. A kit call (and `exec` script calls) now returns the body as sent in `data`, so `sqlite.kv.get` or `files.get` of a stored value shaped like `{ statusCode, message, data }` returns that value whole instead of its inner `data`. `statusCode` is the HTTP status.
- **A stopped or paused computer is not retried,** and the error names `hoody computers start` or `hoody computers resume`.
- **Pipe commands name the cause when the pipe server cannot be reached,** and `pipe url get -o ndjson` prints a JSON string.
- **Error text:** `KIT_NOT_READY` appears in the error heading; an unknown terminal key is named with the supported keys; a merged command names every form when a required argument is missing; an empty allowed value is shown as `""`.
- **A GitHub sign-in recovery is shown as the command,** with its optional flags (`hoody agent github auth login ... --host ... --activate`).
- **Terminal views:** environment variable and cookie names are shown as sent, computer stats show percentages, glob and grep warn when the result is truncated, and memory search and bot lists draw their rows.
- **Help:** a flag whose description is only notes and its allowed values starts with its name again; `files ssh get|upload --help` shows an example with `--pass`.
- **SDK:** a sign-in redirect is refused as `REDIRECT_REFUSED` without retries; `frames()` of an agent socket ends when the socket closes; an empty required path segment is refused before sending; `kv.exists()` answers true on 304; `curl.run` and `notes.document.get` have typed overloads per response; `getKitUrl('http')` and `('https')` use ports 80 and 443.
- **Agent help and skills:** `-o json` examples read fields at the top level (no `.data`); `servers commands` take `server_id`; corrected examples for marketplace filters, cookies, `browser act` and `wait`, `curl storage get`, `proxy logs list`, `computers sync` and `update`.

### Security

- **`hoody agent prompt` no longer sends your account token to the computer.** The interactive `hoody shell` had the same fallback and no longer has it either: when the API hands back no kit token, the shell sends no account token in its place. `hoody agent prompt` used to ask the API for a computer claim first and, when the answer carried no kit token, sent your account token to the computer's agent instead. It now sends only the kit credential you configured (`--kit-auth`, `--kit-token` or the other kit flags), and nothing when you configured none: the computer's address is the credential.
- **A CLI request to a full URL on another site no longer carries your Hoody credentials.** `Authorization` was already removed there; an `X-Hoody-Token` from the configured `headers` and the kit token under a renamed `--kit-token-header` were not, and now are, for downloads too.
- **`hoody agent prompt -o pretty` no longer passes control characters from the agent's stream to your terminal.** Tool lines, thinking and other event lines replace them with spaces first, as the other human outputs already did, so a stream cannot move the cursor, set the window title or reorder text. The reply on stdout is unchanged.
- **Use undici 8.12.2 or later with the SDK.** `npm audit` reports high-severity advisories for undici before 8.12.2 (GHSA-4qv8-2p9j-2685, GHSA-f72v-6w45-q472, GHSA-xjj6-f33q-929c, GHSA-4w7w-5xrc-9h26, GHSA-2m5v-pch6-74hx, GHSA-qrxm-fqhp-jwvm, GHSA-9fm3-97px-rjf6, GHSA-55wc-3pg8-jfw6). The SDK now requires `undici@^8.12.2` (was `^8.9.0`), so a fresh install gets a fixed release. A project that pins or overrides `undici` to an older 8.x must raise it. The SDK's own lockfiles now pin 8.12.4.
- **More CLI views replace control characters and bidi marks in text that comes from a computer or the API.** `hoody proxy logs stream` in `-o pretty`, `table` and `wide` (the request URL in a log entry is written by whoever made the request), `hoody computers wait`, `--until-done` errors, the `command:` line of `hoody run`, `hoody daemon programs terminal connect`, the public URL of `hoody tunnel expose`, the status and header lines of `hoody pipe send` and `receive`, `hoody exec <script> --help`, `hoody agent login`, the error lines of `hoody agent prompt`, and the page address and code of the browser sign-in. The JSON formats print the data unchanged. An address or link target that held such a character is shown as text, not as a link, and the browser sign-in makes its page address a link only when it is an `https` address.
- **A stream frame with a `__proto__` key can no longer change what `-o pretty` draws.** The cleaned copy of a frame took such a key as its prototype, so a line could show fields the frame did not carry, or the command could exit 1. Every key is now copied as plain data.
- **Shown-ID masking covers the generic stream line.** With ID masking on, a 24-character ID inside the data of an event the CLI has no formatter for was shown in full.
- **`hoody boturls ask`, `answer`, `chat` and `updates list` replace control characters in what a Bot sends.** A Bot URL can be another party's Bot. Its reply text, status, ref and row fields are cleaned before they are drawn, and a ref that is not one plain word is left out of the suggested command; `-o json` prints the data unchanged.
- **`hoody update` cleans the failure detail of an update channel that fails its signature check.** In a pipe, the detail quoted bytes of the channel file before they were verified.
- **`hoody help <path>` never runs a command.** A help request with a mistyped word (`hoody help network strt`) is refused as the bare path, and with `HOODY_CLI_AI_FIX=auto` the auto-fixer then ran the corrected command (`hoody network start`). A help request is now never re-run and the fixer is not asked.
- **Sign-in passwords no longer follow a redirect to another site.** `hoody login`, `hoody signup` and the automatic sign-in sent the password in the body with no credential header, so a 307 or 308 resent it to any site the answer named. A CLI request whose JSON body names a credential field, at any depth, now follows a redirect only on the site it was sent to, and refuses any other (`REDIRECT_REFUSED`).
- **The SDK never resends a request body to another site on a redirect.** `HttpClient` (Node and browser) confined redirects only by the request's headers and query, so a sign-in or sign-up POST answered 307 or 308 resent its body, password included, to any site the answer named. A redirect that would send a body to another origin (a 307 or 308, or a 301 or 302 of anything but a POST) is now refused with `REDIRECT_REFUSED` and nothing is sent there, whatever the body holds. A 303, and a 301 or 302 of a POST, still follow as a GET without the body, and a redirect on the same origin resends the body (a JSON body as the same text: it is encoded once). In a web browser's page or worker, where fetch does not reveal where a redirect leads, a request with a body now fails closed: every redirect of it is refused with `REDIRECT_REFUSED`, a harmless one (a 303, or one on the same origin) included, since the two cannot be told apart there.
- **Error messages no longer quote secret values.** A header value the runtime refuses (a line break in a token) is reported by the header's name only, and nothing is sent. A malformed JSON answer is reported by its size, and a malformed `--<flag> <json|@file|@->` document by the flag, the source and, where the parser gives one, the line and column.
- **`hoody shell <computer> --env NAME=VALUE` refuses a name that is not a variable name.** The name was put into the command unquoted, so shell syntax in it ran on the computer. It now exits 2 and nothing is sent.
- **`hoody local lock recover --finalize` finishes the migration it reports.** It removes a plaintext `password` left in config.json at any stage, finishes a migration stopped between writing lock.json and recording it, and refuses, changing nothing and keeping the backup and the journal, when config.json cannot be read, holds a value the lock does not keep, or holds a field at both the top level and profile "default". `hoody local lock enable` refuses a field that both the top level and profile "default" hold, since the lock keeps both in one place, and `disable --write-plaintext` puts each value back at the level it came from and keeps an empty profile value, so that profile still does not inherit the top-level one. `hoody local lock password set` keeps the key derivation settings unless `--kdf-profile` is given.
